# Learn the recommended way of working through real Xezar development

New real-task entries go in `.xezar/docs/dogfooding.d/<runId8>.md`, one dated entry per file, using the same record template as the entries below. The release role folds them, newest first, above the existing entries, and deletes the fragment files. **A read-only or merge-only run has no step that commits a fragment, so it never writes here or to any other repository path**: `integration`, `code-review`, `qa`, `design-review`, `research`, `business-analysis`, `issue-triage` and a `quick-task` skill write their observation to their evidence dir `.local/xezar/tasks/<runId>/dogfooding.md` instead, and the leader folds it later. Do not append to this file: it is append-only at the top, so every pull request editing it conflicts with every other open pull request.

Every future authorized feature/bug/docs task is also an opportunity to assess the workflow, skill and effective settings it actually uses. Do not launch an extra goal merely to fill this ledger. The leader preserves the feature's AC and records concise operational observations alongside its delivery evidence.

Four evidence levels, never conflated: **adapted** (installed and statically checked), **fixture-tested** (named isolated case passed on identified kit bytes), **real-task verified** (observed in an actual authorized task, with runtime/kit/result evidence), **recommended** (reviewed guidance justified by real-task results and known limits). Copying or a green fixture is insufficient for a recommendation.

Record: task/goal and criterion, workflow/skill/effective config, code head/base, runtime/build and kit digest, expected behavior, observed behavior, evidence link, problem/impact, hypothesis/change, regression or control, outcome, remaining limit, owner/next trigger. Keep private logs in primary .local/xezar/tasks; publish only safe conclusions in this maintained guidance. Missing tokens/cost/timing remain unknown.

Loop: observe during real work → classify problem versus environment/unknown → fix the narrow workflow/skill/check/config cause within authority → test the regression and unchanged quality → validate in a later relevant real task → record the bounded recommendation and limits. Do not schedule fixed reviews, weaken gates, fabricate performance benefits or overwrite active snapshots. Recommendations can be revised with rationale and evidence; not every one-off brief needs a permanent skill.

Installation validation is reported in installation.md. Real-task entries follow; each names its evidence level.

## Real-task entries

### 2026-09-23 — Quota documentation needed the formatter as well as the contract

- The frozen quota contract established `ok`, `out`, `unknown` and `resetsAt`, but the cockpit formatter and chip component held two user-visible edges the fixture alone did not: `unknown` is excluded from the can-work count, and a definite `out` row keeps an agent in the chip even when no other plan fact was reported.
- Searching the complete routing documents separated active probe-task instructions from dated findings. The active guidance now uses `read_quota`, while the historical Codex probe observation remains dated evidence in the findings log.

### 2026-09-23 — PR #885 Codex hook response round 1, `address-review-findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Codex — real-task observed and live-verified

- A copied `CODEX_HOME` under macOS exposed that lexical path comparison is not identity: xezar supplied `/tmp/...` and the real Codex app-server reported the same directory as `/private/tmp/...`. Comparing `realpath` values (with a lexical fallback for an absent path) keeps the wrapper-mismatch guard fail-closed without refusing the supported copied-profile proof.
- An immutable copied `config.toml` did not prove a refused grant while the exact handler key/hash was already trusted: Codex treated the repeated write as a no-op. Prepending a harmless non-Bash hook changed the copied profile's handler key, forced a fresh trust write, and produced the required refusal before any turn. A refusal proof has to establish that the operation would really mutate state; read-only permissions alone are not evidence of that.

### 2026-09-23 — #867 S5, the cockpit half of agent quota, `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: an approved mockup (`designs/agent-quota/`, design review PASS on #870) drawn against the spec's first-draft answer, and a merged contract (`agent-quota.ts`, frozen fixture under D33) that is much slimmer — no `summaries`, `stale`, `nextCheckAt`, `warnings`, `statusReason`, `serverTimeZone` or `loginKind`. The brief said "the contract wins".
- Observed: **a mockup approved against a draft shape is a list of departures waiting to happen, and the cheapest place to settle them is one known-gaps row plus one helper per derived fact.** Every fact the design needed but the answer lacked (the D30 summary, Stale, the refresh gap, the zone) became one named function in `lib/agent-quota.ts`, and the same list went into G-49 and the PR body, so the reviewer reads one inventory instead of discovering the gaps screen by screen.
- Observed: the AC-37 "cockpit = MCP answer" test only means something when its expected values come from the RAW fixture, not from the cockpit's own helpers — a helper that drops a value would otherwise vouch for itself.
- Observed: a "byte-for-byte unchanged sidebar" snapshot (#467) caught a class added to the footer for the band's border. Moving the band inside the footer, where an empty slot renders no element, kept the snapshot and the look.
- Observed: one live test failed once because the SSE hint arrived while the very first GET was in flight, and TanStack folds an invalidation into an in-flight first fetch. Redesigned onto a deterministic signal (wait for the first answer; assert `isInvalidated`/`fetchStatus` synchronously) rather than a sleep.
- Remaining limit: the refresh route (`POST /api/v1/workspace/agent-quota/refresh`, #867 S3) was not on `main`; the control is built and tested against a mocked client, and the typed client cannot name it until S3 merges.

### 2026-09-23 — Review response setup still starts from the base

- The `address-review-findings` worktree again started at current `main`, while the guarded delivery had to descend from PR #903's reviewed tip. The clean, commit-less task branch first had to be repositioned to that tip before the authorized main merge; the review-response skill still describes retaining the response commit but not this prerequisite.
- `npm run typecheck` rebuilt the tracked Codex hook bundle after the shared refusal copy changed. The focused source tests passed before that build, so the generated artifact only became visible at final typecheck and needed its own follow-up commit.

### 2026-09-23 — review round 1 on #890 (#886 pushed leader events), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a sol `## Code review` (REQUEST CHANGES: two Majors, two Minors) and a parallel `## QA` (PASS) on the author's head `338f89c7`; a brief naming the fix for each and "merge `main` first".
- Observed: **a mixed-version pin is only real when the older side's bytes answer.** The reviewed P4 case ran this tree's `listenMcpSocket` with `version: '0.18.0'`, which changes a label, not the implementation. What worked: boot the published 0.18.0 (a global install, first proven byte-identical to the npm tarball whose shasum matches the registry) in an isolated scratch project, put a recording Unix-socket proxy between it and the source bridge, drive a Claude Code-shaped client through status → attach → one event → read → ack, and keep the captured frames as a hash-pinned fixture a replay server answers from — including 0.18.0's own `version-mismatch` and `unknown-method` refusals, recorded from the same service, so a replay never invents an answer. Five named breaks (protocol bump, a `session/open` field 0.18.0 never sends, a renamed `leader/push` param, a renamed `session/open` param, an edited capture) each go red.
- Observed: **the parallel QA's live proof of the new blocker used the very calls the review said must not count.** QA's "active-silent" scenario polled `leader_events status` for six minutes and saw `claude-code-push-not-seen` fire; after the fix a `leader_events` call is never activity, so that script now correctly stays on the soft blocker. A live re-check has to keep the session busy with other tools. Two independent verdicts on one head can disagree about what the head is FOR, and the later fix silently turns one of them into evidence of the old behaviour.
- Observed: **a named break that does not apply reads exactly like a test that is green both ways.** One red-proof one-liner (`perl … if !$done++ && /re/`) incremented its guard on line 1 and never substituted; the suite stayed green and looked like a coverage gap. Grepping for the break before reading the result caught it. Every break in the proof scripts now either greps its own effect or is re-run after a fix.
- Observed: the step-0 merge of `main` was clean, so `merge-recovery.sh record-intent` was followed by a plain `git merge` and never needed its `commit` verb; the intent record stays behind as the audit trail of an authorized merge that simply did not conflict.
- Remaining limit: P1 (named root cause) and P2 (delivery restored) are still not met; the next step is the owner relaunching the live leader with `--debug-file`.

### 2026-09-23 — Bounded agent quota checks

- The S0 evidence corrected the planned Claude path before implementation: the local `/usage` command is the proven zero-token fallback, while a natural-language request for `get_usage` consumes tokens and is never used.
- Contract-first request schemas and one shared scheduler kept HTTP, MCP, startup and viewer-driven checks on the same per-login gap, concurrency and timeout rules.
- Process tests exercised fixed argv, temporary working directories, minimal profile environment plumbing and saved-child timeout termination without invoking a shell.

### 2026-09-23 — Full predecessor identity is required for counter recovery

- `counters init --predecessor 0e89332e` reported unknown history even though the named predecessor evidence existed; retrying with the resolved full run ID recovered round 1 and allowed the explicitly authorized final round to be consumed without inventing budget.
- The review-response delivery contract kept the response commit on this run's own branch while the guarded push targeted the existing PR branch at its pinned reviewed tip.

### 2026-09-23 — pushed leader events missing on Claude Code 2.1.280 (#886), `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a leader report that eight delivered rows never appeared as `<channel source="xezar">` messages while `status` said attached, `canPush: true`, and three unverified candidate causes (the 2.1.278 → 2.1.280 update, the source-checkout bridge against the 0.18.0 cockpit, session binding after `stop` + `attach`).
- Observed: **none of the three candidates reproduced.** Seven isolated live runs (real interactive `claude` under a PTY, scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, dummy API key, scripted model endpoint, own `XEZ_HOME` and port) delivered every push on 2.1.278 and 2.1.280, with the tsx source bridge against the installed 0.18.0 cockpit, after `stop` + `attach`, with a background task running, with the parent-session variables inherited, and with `remoteControlAtStartup`. The existing A-19 acceptance fixture (`mcp-real-clients.test.ts`) plus `--debug-file` was the fastest bisect tool: Claude Code logs `Channel notifications registered` or `skipped: <reason>` there, and nowhere else.
- Observed: the live leader `claude` was started from a Terminal shell that inherited another Claude Code session's variables (`CLAUDECODE=1`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_PID`, a messaging socket of a dead 2.1.272 process), created one minute after the last channel entry in that session's transcript (2026-09-15). In isolation those variables do not stop channels, but they do stop Claude Code writing a transcript at all, so "no channel entries in the transcript since 09-15" is absence of evidence, not proof that pushes stopped then.
- Observed: the first version of the owner-takeover guard test passed under its own named break because `sessionClosed` also reset the state; only the break-per-behaviour pass caught it.
- Remaining limit: the drop was not reproduced, so P1 (named root cause) and P2 (delivery restored) are not met; the owner's account-side conditions (claude.ai login, org policy, Remote Control bridging) could not be tested without a personal account. Next step is a leader relaunch with `--debug-file` to read Claude Code's own skip reason.

### 2026-09-23 — a completed turn is not a completed headless process

- A real Codex 0.155.1 denial reached `turn.completed`, then the headless CLI exited normally with status 0 after 22.2 seconds. A QA window shorter than the runner's bounded EOF/watchdog path can misclassify that interval as a hang; live QA should wait for the CLI result or record the exact observation bound.
- The review-response counter initializer needs the predecessor's full run id. A user-facing short id is insufficient even when exactly one matching evidence directory exists, so recovery first has to resolve the durable directory name.

### 2026-09-23 — review round 3 on #890 (#886 pushed leader events), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` of final re-check `42119d8a` (sol, one Major) on head `d9a67c29`; the owner authorised this third round and no fourth.
- Observed: **a success signal is not a coverage signal.** Round 2 made a read count only once it answered without an error, but "answered" still included a gap ("Nothing was replayed") and a page that stopped short. The fix records the journal rows the answer actually carried, so the blocker is judged per pushed row instead of per point in time.
- Observed: **the phase-record CRITERIA check accepts only `AC-` or `DP-` criterion IDs**, and refuses others with a generic "no <ID>: <criterion> line" message that does not name the accepted prefixes.
- Observed: **the dogfooding fragment format is enforced only by the gates step.** A fragment written as a single bullet passed every author-side check and failed `repository-checks.sh`, which cost one gate-return round. A xezar restart also killed a foreground `npm test` mid-suite (exit 137); the rerun was clean.

### 2026-09-23 — The generated Codex schema answered what a dead account could not

- With the Codex account out of credits, `codex app-server generate-json-schema` under a throwaway `CODEX_HOME` was enough to build both Codex quota items without a model turn: it showed per-model buckets (`rateLimitsByLimitId` + `normalModelSlug`) exist, and that 0.156.0 has no `turn/failed` notification — a failed turn arrives as `turn/completed` with `turn.status: "failed"`, which the runner had been treating as an ordinary turn end (the #565 symptom).
- The schema does not carry the reset time of a `usageLimitExceeded` error; the Codex binary's own format strings (`%b %-d<suffix>, %Y %-I:%M %p`) did, and they showed the existing text parser would have read only the clock and guessed the day.
- Validating the hand-written fixtures against the generated schema with the repository's own `ajv` turned "schema-shaped" from a claim into a check. The live proof still waits for Codex credits.

### 2026-09-23 — Codex hook proof kept the real profile immutable

- A production-runner proof against Codex 0.156.0 copied the active `CODEX_HOME`, isolated
  `XEZ_HOME`, and used a scratch repository under `/tmp`. The trusted `Bash|apply_patch` hook
  blocked an `apply_patch` file creation, and its shared refusal reached the v1 run stream as a
  `note`.
- Hashing both real `~/.codex*` profiles' `config.toml`, `hooks.json`, and `rules/` before and after
  gave byte-identical results. Keeping the profile clone and the xezar cache under the same
  disposable root made the proof exercise persistent registration and trust without touching the
  live profile.

### 2026-09-23 — A child `close` event does not prove its process group is empty

- A timeout sent SIGTERM to the saved provider-process group, then the group leader's `close`
  handler cleared the pending SIGKILL timer. A surviving grandchild could therefore outlive the
  completed quota check even though the direct child was gone.
- Full-path process tests should save the spawned PGID, verify the required signal reached that
  exact group, and assert the group is empty afterward. Direct-child liveness and command-pattern
  searches do not establish the process-group boundary.

### 2026-09-23 — Review evidence must outlive its run

- The review-response brief named a QA evidence directory that no longer existed even though the
  QA worktree and NDJSON transcript remained. The real captured provider reply was recoverable
  from the transcript, but durable review inputs should be copied into the primary evidence
  directory before the producing task finishes.
- The review-response worktree started at the current base rather than the reviewed PR tip. With
  no unique task commits, restoring the task branch to the immutable reviewed tip before the
  guarded base merge preserved the required ancestry and left the author's checkout untouched.

### 2026-09-23 — the 0.19.0 changelog fold, `release` step `changelog`, `xezar-release-changelog`, pi — real-task observed
- Input: the one-line brief `bump: minor`, the tag `v0.18.0` (`1c21a95a`, 2026-09-22), `origin/main` `da87671b`, 42 first-parent commits since the tag, 31 committed `changelog.d/` fragments and 43 `.xezar/docs/dogfooding.d/` fragments. Deliverable: the `# 0.19.0 (2026-09-23)` section, the folded fragments and the folded dogfooding entries in one commit.
- Observed: **the commit subject is not a reliable PR number, and this release had two outright contradictions.** `3877c7ef` (PR 913) ends `(#893)` because the PR title itself carries that reference, and `f4fa389b` (PR 907) ends `(#867)` because it was squash-merged with a custom message under integration authority — its commit subject is not the PR title at all. Six further commits (`884`, `866`, `845`, `847`, `844`, `839`) carry no `(#N)` suffix. The `gh pr list --json mergeCommit` map resolved all 42 first-parent commits; a subject-suffix parse would have mis-numbered two bullets and dropped six.
- Observed: **a fragment's filename is the only reliable fragment-to-PR map.** Fragment names are either the PR number (`838-b.md`, `867-cockpit.md`), an issue slice (`854-xezar-skills-3-floor.md`), or the branch (`xez-e59d020d-leader-contributor-docs.md`); the number inside the bullet is usually the umbrella issue, not the PR. `gh pr view --json headRefName` is what turns `31b3eada` into PR 837 and `e59d020d` into PR 836. Seven fragments named no PR at all and had to be given theirs in the folded section, exactly as the 0.18.0 entry recorded.
- Observed: the `v0.18.0` tag sits on the changelog-record commit itself (`1c21a95a`, `docs: record 0.18.0 in the changelog (#834)`), so the first-parent range `v0.18.0..origin/main` begins with the Release workflow's own `chore(release): v0.18.0 (#835)` bump — the bump exclusion is not optional when the tag is a changelog commit.
- Observed: the date-window PR search (`merged:>=2026-09-21`) returned 44 PRs for a 42-commit range. Two were merged before the tag commit on the tag's own day and 30 more belong to 2026-09-21, all already recorded in 0.18.0; the first-parent range, not the date, is the boundary — the same finding the 0.18.0 entry recorded, now with a larger superset.
- Remaining limit: no PR was opened or merged in this step; the CI wait, the changelog PR merge, the Release dispatch and the bump-PR merge all belong to the workflow's last (handoff) step.

### 2026-09-23 — review round 2 on #890 (#886 pushed leader events), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a sol re-check `## Code review` (REQUEST CHANGES, one Major) on the round-1 head `06868d99`; a brief marking this the last round.
- Observed: **an observer that fires before validation cannot also be the evidence that the call happened.** Round 1 fixed "a `leader_events` read is not unrelated activity" by passing the tool and action through the arrival edge — correct for activity, which must stay fail-safe, and wrong for the read, which suppresses a blocker and so must only count once it validated and answered. The fix split the two meanings into two edges (`called` on arrival, `succeeded` after a non-error answer, dated at arrival) instead of moving the one edge, so the fail-safe half did not change.
- Observed: **a named break run through a zsh function silently tested nothing.** `npm test -- $T` with `T` a space-separated string does not word-split under zsh, vitest found no test files and exited 0, and all four breaks read as "green both ways". Re-running under `bash` with an array gave four reds. A red-proof harness must assert that its tests actually ran (a `Tests N failed` line), not only the exit code.
- Remaining limit: P1 (named root cause) and P2 (delivery restored) are still not met; they need the owner relaunching the live leader with `--debug-file`.

### 2026-09-23 — Review response needs engine-owned PR identity

- The check step does not inherit `XEZ_TASK_ID`, but its worktree path resolves the run ID and the engine-owned `runs.json` record supplies the numeric PR reference. That let acquisition fetch one PR head without trusting model output or adding configuration.
- A stashed-source red proof on macOS Bash 3.2 caught the empty-array expansion for bare reads; Linux-only execution would not have exercised the failure.
- Live QA showed that adding a wrapper without removing broader overlapping prefixes leaves the wrapper bypassable. Contract coverage now checks both the intended wrapper and the exact remaining Git-prefix set.

### 2026-09-23 — Trusting the Codex read-only hook headlessly

- The earlier spike's negative result was load-bearing: `thread/start` accepted an inline hook but `hooks/list` still did not discover it after thread creation. The implementation therefore keeps the thread override for session intent and installs the identical generic handler through Codex's user-layer `hooks.json`, the only discovery path proven live on 0.155.1.
- A foreground live `CodexAppServerRunner` run proved the complete path: `git status --short` ran; a semicolon compound, a redirect and `gh pr merge 0` were denied with `syntax.compound`, `syntax.redirection` and `prefix.entry`; neither denied filesystem side effect appeared.
- The app-server's normalized hook hash cannot be recreated safely from file bytes. Asking `hooks/list`, granting the returned key/hash through `config/batchWrite`, then re-reading trust kept the adapter fail-closed and made start/resume use the same sequence.

### 2026-09-23 — A denial proof needs an absolute canary

- A first live Codex proof used `touch qa-hang-canary` and checked the scratch repository root; one sample created that relative file despite the session text saying the hook denied the command. Repeating the proof with a unique absolute `/tmp/.../qa-hang-canary` path produced three unambiguous denials and clean exits. A safety proof should name and inspect the exact absolute side effect, not infer the command's working directory.

### 2026-09-22 — review response round 1 on PR #850 (read-only steps, #849), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a binding REQUEST CHANGES (fable) and a QA FAIL (sonnet) that both reproduced the same Major with a credit-free `codex sandbox` probe, plus an owner decision that revised criterion C from `read-only` to CONFINED (`workspace-write` + network + the run's own writable roots).
- Observed: **the criterion was right about intent and wrong about the mechanism, and only a probe of what the role must DELIVER found it.** The original run verified `read-only` against the app-server schema — a correct fact about the enum — but the question was what a review step needs to post its verdict (network, the handoff and evidence dirs outside the worktree). `codex sandbox -c …` answers that in seconds with no model turn and no credits; it is the cheap check to run before choosing a sandbox for a role, the Codex counterpart of reading Claude's init tool list.
- Observed: the fix reused two things already on the wire — the `config` override the runner sends for MCP isolation (#324) and `spec.additionalDirectories`, which Claude, pi and OpenCode already consumed and Codex alone ignored. No new knob, no new env var.
- Remaining limit: no live Codex turn (workspace out of credits), so CONFINED is fixture-tested (whole `thread/start` / `thread/resume` params) plus the reviewer's `codex sandbox` probe of the same `-c` keys, not live-verified through `thread/start`'s `config` override.

### 2026-09-22 — review response round 1 on PR #858 (`xezar state-names --json`, #852), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed

- Observed: **a fast path keyed on `argv[2]` is not the same as "answered before the boot".** The
  author run's own test for the flag-first form used a plain temporary folder, which resolves to the
  global layout and never reaches `createProjectStateFiles`; the reviewer found the write only by
  pairing the flag with a git repository and `--single-project`. Lesson: a "writes nothing" claim is
  tested in the folder shape where the ordinary path DOES write, and asserted as an unchanged tree.
- Observed: the response path the skill describes worked here without the historical
  DELIVERED-versus-branch conflict: this task branch was cut from `main` at the PR's own base, so a
  fast-forward to the reviewed head, a commit here and a `DELIVERED` record naming the PR branch let
  `worktree-git.sh push` deliver to the PR branch while this branch keeps the same commit.

### 2026-09-22 — Proving review-response regressions

- Observed: a red-proof command can accidentally select a control assertion that also passes against the reviewed bug. The first memory-only proof filtered on “keeps observations in memory”; the old file-backed store also kept an in-memory map, so the proof was green. A dedicated assertion that the store exposes no persistence path and a fresh instance starts empty made the named break real. The failed green attempt was retained beside the corrected red log instead of being rewritten as evidence.
- Observed: notification counts can race an asynchronous duplicate write. The #882 regression initially asserted the WS-facing listener count after the first answer appeared, which could beat the second `markOut`; spying on the authoritative `markOut` seam proved exactly one write while the listener assertion continued to pin one published change.

### 2026-09-22 — Generic peer-kit references in shipped guidance

- A peer's internal documentation filenames are not an end-user contract; shipped guidance should name only the peer's confirmed public files and describe internal sources generically.

### 2026-09-22 — port-memory sentinel band read from the host (#874 round 1), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: the Codex sol review of PR #878 (run `8a19e5d4`) raised one Major – the fixed 20000–32767 band sat below the DEFAULT ephemeral ranges only, and every OS lets that range be reconfigured.
- Observed: the sentinel band is now derived from the range the host reports at file start (macOS `net.inet.ip.portrange.first/last` and `hifirst/hilast`, Linux `ip_local_port_range`); any other platform, an unreadable range or no free block below the range is a hard failure, never a fallback band. This host reported 49152–65535 for both macOS ranges, so the band here is still 20000–32767.
- Observed: the red proof had to revert only the band-selection function, not stash the whole file, because the fixture case lives in the same test file as the fix; a whole-file stash would have removed the test with the fix and proven nothing.
- Not verified live: a host with a reconfigured range; covered only by the injected-range fixture case.

### 2026-09-22 — server-mode harness teardown redesign (#876), `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-fix`, claude/opus-5.5 — real-task, fixture-tested
- Input: CI `Hosted server boundary and proxy` red on docs-only PR #873 with `FAIL cleanup: ENOTEMPTY … /repo` after all eight cases passed.
- Observed: **a CLI's exit is not the end of what it started.** A probe that listed the server's process group right after `serve` exited on SIGTERM found a background `git clone --bare` of the team-skills catalog (and its `git-remote-https` children) still writing into the scratch home; the dry-run agent of a run also lives on to finish its turn, writing `notes.md` into the scratch repo. The harness removed the folder while they ran.
- Observed: **the old file could not show the defect locally** – six of six concurrent runs green – so a plain `git stash` of the harness is a green-either-way baseline, not a red proof. The red proof had to be the new check with only the new wait removed (five of six red with `BREAK-TEARDOWN-TREE`).
- Observed: zsh does not word-split `P="bash script"; $P args`; use a shell function for a repeated kit command.
- Observed: `phase-record.sh check` needs criterion IDs spelled `AC-1:`, not `AC1:`; the refusal message says `<ID>: <criterion>` without naming the required prefix.
- Remaining limit: `serve` still leaves descendants running after SIGTERM; the harness now ends them itself, and whether the server should reap them on shutdown is a product question not settled here.

### 2026-09-22 — #867 S2 agent-quota read side, `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, codex — real-task observation
- Input: the engine read-side slice after the quota contract and frozen fixture merged in #868, using the three S0 live captures as normalizer fixtures.
- Observed: **an optional field read from the injected `RunManager` is unsafe when server tests use a permissive proxy fake.** The first MCP coverage run treated the proxy-produced function as a quota store and failed across unrelated route tests. The server now accepts only an actual `AgentQuotaStore` instance and otherwise constructs its private fallback, while production still shares the real manager store.
- Regression/control: the focused API/MCP suite passes with both a real injected quota store and the repository's fake-manager construction sites; temporarily removing the MCP action implementation made both new `read_quota` tests fail.
- Remaining limit: the installed-host MCP bridge and a dry-run stream producing a real usage-limit notification remain live QA, recorded in `docs/testing/coverage-gaps.md` and the draft PR.

### 2026-09-22 — end-user documentation pass after the 0.18.0 release, `docs-maintenance` step `author`, `xezar-docs-maintenance`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a leader brief with seven verified survey items (G1–G7) plus one qualitative goal, explicitly labelled as predating the 0.18.0 merges and requiring re-verification. Scope split with a parallel task by directory: this run owned `docs/guide/**` and `README.md` only.
- Observed: **a wrong freshness footer is the failure that hides the others.** All 18 guide pages claimed to describe 0.16.0 two releases after the fact, and the brief's own diagnosis — that this is why nothing else got noticed — held up: the stale `echo 'replace me with…'` block in two pages had survived the whole of 0.17.0. A footer maintained by hand is a claim nobody re-checks. Re-verification also moved three of the seven items: `providers connect --account` and `accounts import-global` were **already** documented in the CLI reference by the 0.18.0 merges, so G5 was smaller than briefed; `gateSlots` has no stored `null` at all, so the brief's "what does `null` mean" had no true answer and the page says so instead; and the generated workflow has **two** shapes, not the one the brief named — `implement → verify → report` only where `init` discovered a check. A brief written from a survey is an input to verify, not a specification to transcribe.
- Regression/control: `node scripts/check-links.mjs` (exit 0) proves every relative link and anchor, including the four new anchors and the reordered getting-started page whose headings had to keep their slugs. `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/release/generic-instructions.test.ts` (8 passed) is the control that matters for `README.md`, which is the packed npm README and a scanned instruction producer; `npm run typecheck` green. The G6 claim about the repository's own manifest was verified harder than the brief asked: `git show v0.18.0:packages/xezar/package.json` still reads `0.17.0`, so the **tag itself** predates the bump, and `docs/publishing.md` step 4 states the same window in its own words.
- Observed, gate stage: **between a publish and its bump, the unit gate is red for every branch in the repository, and the cause looks like the candidate.** Two gate attempts failed on `test/unit/serve-pty.test.ts` — "a line ran past 80 columns (87)" on the update banner `⬆ xezar 0.18.0 is available (running 0.17.0)`. Neither failure was caused by this markdown-only candidate: the banner's text is fetched live from the npm registry and prints only while the registry answers a version the local manifest does not, so it appeared the moment 0.18.0 was published and would have failed on the base commit identically. The first return reported it and left it, per the rule that an unrelated failure is never repaired inside another pull request. By the second return the bump had landed (`origin/main` `1e9d3462`, `chore(release): v0.18.0`), so the repair was a **refresh** — merge `origin/main`, which brought only the four version stamps and made the two versions agree — and not a test edit. The product defect survives the refresh: the banner is a plain `console.log` outside the width-bounding renderer (`packages/xezar/src/index.ts`), so it will redden the same suite in the next publish-to-bump window. This is the same window G6 documents for readers, met from the other side.
- Remaining limit: the guide's screenshots are still the 0.16.0 captures under `docs/screenshots/0.16.0/`; pages now say 0.18.0 above 0.16.0 images, which re-shooting fixes and a documentation edit cannot. The npm-propagation half of G6 rests on the leader's single observation tonight (~15 minutes) and is written as an observation, not a guarantee — no measurement of the registry's real propagation window was taken. Nothing pins the footer string in any test, so the next release can drift the same way; the pull request proposes stamping it from the release workflow, which is the owner's decision and was not made here.

### 2026-09-22 — redesign the flaky attach-bound case, `xezar-bug-fix` step `investigate`/`author`, `xez-bug-investigation`, claude/sonnet-5 — real-task observed

- Input: #846, a flaky test found on CI (`leader-delivery.test.ts`, the injected OpenCode attach-bound case) reading `expected 149 to be greater than or equal to 150` — the case measured the injected bound with `Date.now()` wall-clock arithmetic around the `attach` call.
- Observed: **the production seam already carried the deterministic signal; no source change was needed.** `checkOpenCodeAttach` builds its abort signal as `AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs)` from exactly the value `LeaderDelivery` was constructed with (`opencodeAttachCheckMs ?? OPENCODE_ATTACH_CHECK_MS`). Spying on the global `AbortSignal.timeout` and asserting the injected 150 (not the shipped 10 000) was the argument it received replaced both the flaky lower bound and the "not the 10 s default" upper-bound guard with one assertion that is coupled to the SUT's actual behaviour, not to timing.
- Regression/control: red proof by temporarily reverting `checkOpenCodeAttach(adapter, this.#opts.opencodeAttachCheckMs ?? OPENCODE_ATTACH_CHECK_MS)` to the hard-coded default — the redesigned case failed with `expected "timeout" to be called with arguments: [150]` / `Received: [10000]`, a clear diagnostic rather than a timeout. Restored, the case is green; the file plus a second suite file (`leader-setup.test.ts`, actually at `src/onboarding/leader-setup.test.ts` — the brief's path was off by a directory) ran clean twice concurrently (69/69 both runs); `npm run test:coverage:mcp` held `leader-delivery.ts` at 97.62/91.79/99.33 (stmts/branch/lines), 2467/2467 tests green.
- Remaining limit: this redesign covers only the one case named in #846; no other timing-sensitive assertion in this file was audited.

### 2026-09-22 — re-capture the user guide's screenshots at 0.18.0, `docs-maintenance` step `docs`, `xezar-docs-maintenance`, claude/sonnet — real-task observed
- Input: issue #838 item D, one of eight items split across four parallel runs by explicit file-scope fences. The remaining limit named by a sibling run's fragment (`31b3eada.md`, above) — "the guide's screenshots are still the 0.16.0 captures" — is exactly this item's brief; the two runs correctly did not overlap.
- Observed: **the generated capture README's own title was wrong, and had been for at least one release.** `packages/web/e2e/capture/docs-screenshots.capture.ts` computes `docsVersion` from `SCREENSHOT_DIR` specifically so the "version chip is pinned to v${docsVersion}" line never drifts from a second hand-typed literal — but the README template's own title line, three lines above that comment's promise, was still the literal string `# xezar 0.16.0 cockpit screenshots`. Every past re-capture (0.15.0 → 0.16.0 per `CHANGELOG.md:1510`) had silently shipped a title one version behind the version it was demonstrating. Fixed to interpolate `docsVersion`; nothing in the repository asserts the README's title against `SCREENSHOT_DIR`, so a future re-capture that skips reading the generator's own source could reintroduce this by hand.
- Observed, capture run: one of 22 capture cases (`settings-appearance-roomy`'s `dark/375` mobile variant) timed out at 25s waiting for `document.documentElement.dataset.density === 'roomy'`; its other two variants of the same state had already succeeded moments earlier in the same run. Re-running only that named state (`-- -t settings-appearance-roomy`) against the still-warm fixture server passed cleanly on the first try, with no other symptom anywhere else in the 22-case run — read as a one-off harness timing flake rather than a reproducible product break, and reported as such rather than silently retried away.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/web/src/docs-screenshots.test.ts` (44 passed) is the control that matters — it independently re-derives every filename from `manifest.ts` and asserts each is on disk, is a real PNG/GIF, is within its byte budget and is named in the README, so a dropped or misnamed file from the capture run would have failed it rather than being caught only by eye. `npm run typecheck` green. `grep -rn "screenshots/0\.16\.0" README.md docs/` (exit 1, no output) proves no stale embed survives.
- Remaining limit, restated because it is the actual point of this item: **no automated check can confirm a captured image shows the right thing.** All 39 PNGs plus the tour GIF were produced by the scripted harness (never hand-edited or copied forward), and the one cockpit-visible change in this release window worth a reviewer's first look is the Agent accounts pane redesign (#831) — but that a picture exists and passed its byte/name contract says nothing about whether the picture is correct, and the PR body says so explicitly rather than implying the capture run is a substitute for a person looking.

### 2026-09-22 — read-only steps on Claude Code and Codex (#849 A–C, engine half of E), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a leader brief slicing #849 into an engine task (this run) and a kit task on another branch, with the read-only signal frozen in advance (a resolved `allowedTools` naming neither `Edit` nor `Write`) and a file fence. The frozen signal was the part that made the slice cheap: no key to name, no env var to document, one predicate both runners import.
- Observed: **`--allowedTools` is an approval list, not a tool list, and the difference is visible in one command.** A live `claude -p … --permission-mode acceptEdits --allowedTools Read,Grep,Glob,Bash` printed `Edit`, `NotebookEdit` and `Write` in its init tool list; adding `--disallowedTools Edit,Write,NotebookEdit` left `Read` alone. The research that filed #849 read this from source; the init event confirms it on the installed CLI (2.1.278) in seconds, which is the cheaper check to reach for next time a tool-limit claim is made.
- Observed: the Codex `read-only` value was verified against the installed app-server's own schema (`codex app-server generate-json-schema`, `ThreadStartParams`/`ThreadResumeParams` → `SandboxMode` enum), not only `codex --help`, because the app-server protocol and the `exec` CLI are separate surfaces that could disagree.
- Regression/control: the red proofs swapped each runner file back to its `HEAD` copy and restored it from a saved copy instead of `git stash push -- <file>`, because the stash stack is shared with every peer worktree; same proof, no shared state. Guard tests that pass both ways (the default writing list keeps its argv and sandbox; an absent list is a writing step) are labelled as guards in the PR, and the pi pin is a guard by construction since pi did not change.
- Remaining limit: no live Codex turn was run in the read-only sandbox, so "a Codex review step cannot write" is fixture-tested plus schema-verified, not live-verified. `planner.ts` and `auto-name.ts` pass `allowedTools: []`, which the signal reads as read-only — harmless for text-only calls, but a reader of the signal should know an empty list counts.

### 2026-09-22 — kit slice D of #849 (read-only bashAllowlist), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, claude/sonnet-5 — real-task observed
- Input: the kit half of a split issue (#849) — add `bashAllowlist` to the four read-only workflows (`code-review`, `design-review`, `qa`, `business-analysis`), leaving the engine slice (Claude/Codex/pi runner behaviour) to a separate task. The brief pre-supplied an "expected" prefix list; the actual work was justifying it against the four skills' own text and differentiating per role (`business-analysis` gets no `gh`/`npm` — its own skill text never posts a PR comment or runs code) rather than copying one list onto all four unchecked.
- Observed: **a `git clone` of a task worktree is not a clean base for a real dry-run proof.** The obvious approach — `git clone --local` the worktree, then `xezar run --workflow code-review` — failed twice: first on `XEZ_TASK_ID` leaking from my own shell into the spawned run (identity mismatch in `bootstrap.mjs`), then on the new task's own worktree resolving its base against `origin/main`, a stale remote-tracking ref inherited from the source worktree's shared `.git` (it mirrors the real upstream's last fetch, not my new commit) — so the fresh task worktree checked out old kit content that conflicted with the new commit sitting in the clone's root. `git archive HEAD` into a bare directory, then a fresh `git init -b main` with no `origin` remote at all, avoided both: no inherited identity env, no stale remote ref to prefer over local `main`.
- Regression/control: `node .xezar/checks/catalog-check.mjs` (CATALOG OK, 18 workflows/20 skills), `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` (751 passed, 0 failed), `node --test .xezar/checks/xezar-contract.test.mjs` (35 passed, 0 failed), `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/tracked-files.test.ts` (7 passed) all green with the new field. The real proof: `XEZ_DRY_RUN=1` + `XEZ_MOCK_ARGS_FILE` on a headless `xezar run --workflow code-review` in the throwaway snapshot recorded the engine's actual built `--allowedTools` for the `review` step — `Bash(git:*),Bash(gh:*),Bash(bash .xezar/checks/:*),Bash(ls:*),...` — one `Bash(<prefix>:*)` per allowlisted command, plain `Bash` gone, matching `buildAllowedTools` in `claude-cli-runner.ts` read cold rather than assumed.
- Remaining limit: the enumeration is honest about its own weight of evidence — `git`/`gh`/`bash .xezar/checks/` are directly quoted from the four skills; the generic read-only text utilities (`cat`, `sed`, `grep`, `rg`, `jq`, `wc`, `head`, `tail`, `find`, `diff`, `sha256sum`/`shasum`, `mkdir`, `cp`) are inferred as support for git/gh output and the shared evidence-dir/verdict-packet pattern rather than named verbatim in the prose, and `npm` for `design-review`/`qa` leans on `docs/testing/agent-browser.md` and "exercise the change", not the skill file alone — flagged here so a reviewer checks the inference, not just the conclusion. The pi-drops-the-shell consequence (criterion E's kit half) is read from `pi-runner.ts` `piTools`, not exercised live against a real pi session.

### 2026-09-22 — closed Git reads for the five read-only roles — real-task observed
- Input: the owner’s “wrapper now, broker later” decision on #863, rank 3 and the wrapper grammar from its research, plus #849 E6’s five read-only workflows. The delivered slice separates trusted acquisition from model-visible inspection: the check step fetches the configured base and fixed pull-request refspec, while `git-read.sh` exposes twelve read operations and no transport.
- Observed: an exact option allowlist is smaller than Git’s apparent read surface. Even the safe operations need different grammars: `cat-file` admits four single-letter modes but no batch mode; log/show formats need a fixed value set; options after `--` become paths and therefore need a separate repository-escape check. Treating “unknown” as refusal kept all three abbreviation probes closed without maintaining a second abbreviation denylist.
- Observed: this repository’s workflow parser intentionally accepts only block-style step mappings. The first compact inline acquisition step passed YAML syntax but failed `catalog-check.mjs`; expanding the same check to the established four-line shape made both the real loader and the catalog agree. A workflow edit should be checked by the repository parser, not by generic YAML validity alone.
- Regression/control: with only `.xezar/checks/git-read.sh` stashed, the infrastructure suite was RED at 760 passed / 29 failed: 12 allowed-operation cases, 3 global-option refusals, 4 forbidden per-operation options, 3 abbreviations, 4 unknown operations, 2 compound/redirection arguments and 1 escaping path. Restored wrapper: 789 passed / 0 failed. `catalog-check.mjs` was green and `xezar-contract.test.mjs` passed 38/38.
- Remaining limit: the wrapper narrows argv and hardens Git’s environment, but it is not a no-write boundary while the role can still execute elsewhere in a writable process tree. #879 owns the broker and OS-boundary follow-up; the shipped kit guidance states that limitation explicitly.

### 2026-09-22 — `xezar state-names --json` publishes the `.local/xezar/` top-level name list (#852), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: a leader brief restating #852's criteria A–D, naming the base (`main` `fdbcbed4`) and the module and fixture #845 had already merged. The half that made this cheap was done in the earlier task: the payload, its schema and its byte fixture existed, so this run added a door rather than a data model. A two-issue slice where the first publishes the data and the second publishes the door is worth repeating — the second issue had one decision left in it, not five.
- Observed: **a fixture is only a contract for the code that can reach it.** `packages/xezar/src/__fixtures__/` is test material and `dist/__fixtures__` is not emitted, so a command that read the fixture would have passed every unit test in this repository and answered nothing on an installed copy — which is the only place the consumer runs it. The assertion that earns its keep is therefore in the packaged test: `dist/__fixtures__/...` is NOT in the tarball, and the installed CLI still prints those exact bytes. Both were verified in the foreground (`npm run test:package`, 22 of 22, after `npm run build:web`).
- Observed: a stdout that a caller pipes into a parser is a different kind of surface from an exit code, and the boot sequence is what threatens it. `main()` prints a layout line, may print a first-run notice, and in single-project mode CREATES state files, all before the command switch. Answering the command at the very top of `main()` — ahead of the shared `parseArgs`, the repository lookup and the first-run door — is what makes "stdout carries the payload and nothing else" and "it writes no file in any layout" true rather than hoped for. The cost is that the word must come first and `--json` is not a global flag; both are documented and pinned by cases rather than left to be discovered.
- Regression/control: the red proof restored `index.ts` alone to its `main` copy while the new module and test stayed, so the failure was behavioural (6 of 9 cases red: the byte comparison, the table, both usage refusals, the ordering rule and the help listing) rather than a missing import. The 3 that stayed green are the in-process controls, which is the correct split: they test the generator, not the wiring. The stash was taken with `git stash push -m <unique tag>` and restored with `git stash apply <sha>` plus a tagged `drop`, never a bare `stash`/`pop`, because the stack is shared with every peer worktree.
- Remaining limit: an early draft matched the command word anywhere in `process.argv`, which would have hijacked `xezar run "… state-names …"`. It was caught by reading the diff, not by a test that existed at the time; the case that now pins it (`--help state-names` must not print the payload) was written afterwards. A first draft of an argv-level interception deserves that case up front. Also: `npm run test:package` cannot run in a fresh worktree until `npm run build:web` has run once, and its failure names a missing `web/dist/index.html` rather than the missing build — a minute lost each time.

### 2026-09-22 — review response round 1 on PR #857 (#849 kit slice D), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, claude/opus-5 — real-task, live-verified
- Input: a binding opus review (REQUEST CHANGES, 2 Blockers from live `claude` probes) and a DeepSeek advisory on a `bashAllowlist` for the four read-only kit workflows.
- Observed: **a command-prefix allowlist has to be probed command by command, and the probes found three write paths neither review named.** `sed -n 'w <file>'` overwrote a tracked file under `Bash(sed -n:*)`; `rg --pre <program>` ran a program under `Bash(rg:*)`; `git diff --output=<file>` wrote a file under `Bash(git diff:*)`. The first two were closed by dropping `sed` and `rg` (the `Read` and `Grep` tools cover them); the third cannot be closed by a prefix and is stated in the changelog. Claude Code's own matcher knows `find -delete`/`-fprint` and refused both under `Bash(find:*)`, so `find` stayed.
- Observed: **under `--permission-mode dontAsk` a redirect is refused even into an `--add-dir` directory, and so is any command that expands a variable** (`mv "${XEZ_HANDOFF_FILE}.verdict.json.tmp" …` was denied under `Bash(mv:*)`). The skills' "write the .tmp, then `mv`" could not be done from a verdict role's shell whatever the list said, so the rename moved into `.xezar/checks/verdict-packet.sh` and the list grants that one script instead of `mv`.
- Observed: **a probe harness that reads the model's reply can mis-score.** A batch run reported "no tool call" for two commands that a direct re-run showed ran (the pipe into `verdict-packet.sh`) or were denied (`sed -i`). Every row in the recorded result comes from the tool call and tool result in the session's stream-json, not from the model's summary.
- Regression/control: `catalog-check.mjs` CATALOG OK; `xezar-contract.test.mjs` 35/35; `infra-tests.sh` 757/0 in the task worktree with `node_modules` (six new `verdict-packet.sh` cases). The headless `XEZ_DRY_RUN=1` `xezar run --workflow code-review` never exits by itself (its last step is interactive); the argv file is complete once the review step has spawned, so stop that one process by its own id rather than waiting on it.
- Remaining limit: no pi, Codex or OpenCode session was run; `design-review` and `qa` lists were built from the skills and `docs/testing/agent-browser.md` but not exercised in a real design review or QA run.

### 2026-09-22 — flaky port-memory test redesigned (#874), `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, claude/opus — real-task observed
- Input: two gate attempts on 2026-09-22, each red only on `serve-port-memory.test.ts` with `no free port in P–P+49`, while four or five gate runs shared the machine; #807 had already moved sentinels away from 65535.
- Observed: **macOS hands out `listen(0)` ports sequentially** (eight calls returned 62955–62962 in a row), so a sentinel taken from `listen(0)` sits exactly where every concurrent suite's next server lands. The brief's suggested fix — an OS-assigned sentinel with a probe range derived from it — would have kept the defect; one eight-line experiment settled it before any code was written.
- Observed: an outgoing loopback connection does NOT block a later `listen` on its local port (Node sets `SO_REUSEADDR`), so the ports that ran out were other suites' listeners, not client sockets.
- Observed: the incident reproduced deterministically with a test-process preload that opens 60 `listen(0)` servers after the first listen — the same thing concurrent suites do, without a busy loop or "load"; old file red with the exact incident signature, new file 10 of 10 green.
- Observed: the named break `remember-before-listen` goes red by the start-record wait timing out (60 s), not by the registry assertion, and it does so identically on the old file: the second boot binds the wrongly remembered port directly, so no `start=` field is printed. The red is genuine but slow; not changed here.
- Remaining limit: a Linux host configured with an ephemeral range reaching below 32768 would put the sentinel band back inside it; not seen on any host this repository runs on.

A wave that wants a verbatim fold must have the authoring step fill in the number, or the release step will always touch the fragment.

The `gh pr list` merge-commit cross-check is what identifies both; the first-parent subject is a convenience, not the authority.
- Observed: the PR search window (`merged:>=<tag date>`) returns every PR merged on the tag's calendar day, which is a superset of the range — 12 of the 26 returned PRs were merged before the tag commit and are already recorded in 0.17.0. The first-parent `v0.17.0..origin/main` list, not the date, is the boundary.

### 2026-09-21 — #819 PR 2, `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code Opus 5 — real-task observed
- Input: a leader brief naming the spec file in a peer task's evidence directory, seven accepted criteria and a red-proof rule that forbids `git stash` and requires a scratch COPY of the pre-fix files.
- Observed: the red proof by file copy works well and is strictly better than a stash here — `git show <base>:<path>` into the evidence directory, copy over the three sources, run only the spawn-based test file, restore from the working copy. All 8 cases were red on `9a737b5f` and the worktree was byte-clean afterwards (`git status` showed exactly the five intended paths). A test that spawns the real CLI is what made that possible: a unit test importing `src/index.ts` cannot exist, because that module runs `main()` on import.
- Observed: two of the three CLI-level assertions initially failed for reasons that were about the harness, not the change. `getRepoInfo` answers `null` in a repository with no commit (`git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` fails on an unborn branch), so "am I in a git repository" for a line `init` prints is better answered by `existsSync('.git')`; and vitest pins `TMPDIR` inside the worktree, which breaks `node_modules/.bin/tsx` (it opens a named pipe under `TMPDIR` and the long path is `EINVAL`) — `node --import tsx` has no such problem and is the precedent `project-machine-state.test.ts` already set.
- Steering mid-turn: the leader added two customer requirements while the implementation was in flight (a bootstrap passes the flag on EVERY start, so the repeat must be quiet; and the import output may name account ids and providers only). Both were cheap to fold in at that moment and would have been expensive after the tests were written a second time — the mid-turn channel earned its keep.

### 2026-09-21 — #819 item 6, refuse before validating, `feature-implementation` step `Implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed

- Input: spec `819-spec.md` r1 § 6 and § 11 PR 6 (business-analysis run `5dc7454f`), criteria P6-AC1–AC5 accepted by the leader; base `main` `7bf81ad3`. Deliverable: refusal-first ordering, an accepted-keys hint, `leader_events` `status`/`read` accepting and ignoring `operationId`, the 0.16.0-vs-0.17.0 shape answer.
- Observed: **the spec's "run preflight BEFORE safeParse" hid a load-bearing detail – the door.** A refused call whose arguments validate reaches the tool through the MCP door, which records a `refused` audit row with the boundary and files a receipt when the call carries a key. Answering every refusal from a pre-validation hook would have silently dropped both for calls that were always valid. The fix asks the hook only in place of an argument error; a valid refused call still goes through the door. "What was the old order load-bearing for" had to include the audit trail, not only the three guarantees the brief named.
- Observed: **"accept and ignore operationId" on a read is not a schema loosening alone.** The door files a receipt for any call whose PARSED arguments carry a key, so accepting it without dropping it would have created exactly the receipt-over-a-read the old rejection existed to prevent. zod 4's `.overwrite()` drops it during parsing and keeps the schema a plain object, so the published input schema is unchanged; `operation-id.test.ts`'s "an exempt read must refuse a key" rule was restated as "must refuse OR drop it" with the drop checked on the parsed output.
- Regression/control: T6.1, T6.2 and T6.4 were red against scratch copies of the pre-fix `service.ts`, `project-config.ts` and `leader-events.ts` (8 of 12 cases failing); T6.3 was proven red by replacing `.strict()` with `.strip()` in a scratch edit, then restored. Neither proof used `git stash`.
- Remaining limit: the 0.16.0 answer rests on reading `v0.16.0` source and `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, not on a live 0.16.0 engine; the reported rejection text matches what that source produces.

- Observed: a red proof by `cp` swap needs to name the whole module graph, not one file. The counter file cannot be corrected without rewriting another run's evidence, so the round number is stated in the trigger and the `SELF_REVIEW` record instead.
- Observed: the fix invalidated a documentation sentence that was true before it. `docs/guide/12-cli-reference.md` said the flag door's copy is verbatim; once `defaults` is filtered on that door, the sentence is false, and nothing in the test suite would have caught it. It was found by grepping the guide for the behavior being changed, not by a failing check — worth doing explicitly whenever a guarantee is widened from one door to two.

The round's criteria are recorded under those IDs as the brief wrote them, and the collision is stated in the review-response comment rather than silently renumbered — a durable criterion ID that means two things in one PR is the kind of ambiguity a later reader resolves wrongly.

The skill's own words ("this task still owns its `xez/<id>` branch and must retain that SAME commit there") only make sense once the branch carries the reviewed bytes, and the `worktree-preflight.sh` comment about run `ba255b58` is the only place that hints at it. `git reset --hard 928ea2db` on the (empty, base-only) task branch was the reconciliation; the delivery push then fast-forwards the PR branch to the response commit. Worth documenting as an explicit step, because a reader who instead edits from the base gets a diff that reverts the whole PR.
- Observed: **the reviewer's unhandled-rejection claim reproduces inside a vitest worker, and the test still fails deterministically.** The review used a standalone `tsx` process to avoid collateral damage. In this run the same throw inside the worker produced the failure cleanly: the new case bounds its await with a 3 s race (below the suite's 15 s clock), so it fails on its own `"the call must answer"` assertion rather than a bare timeout, and vitest additionally reports the `Unhandled Rejection` with the full `callTool -> answer -> handleFrame -> LineFramer.onFrame -> Socket.emit` stack. No worker was lost and the rest of the file's 12 cases still ran. A bounded race is what makes that observable instead of a 15 s hang.
- Observed: **`npm run test:coverage:mcp` is contention-sensitive enough that one red run is not evidence of a regression.** Its first run in this step failed on two cases unrelated to the diff — `acceptance-parity.test.ts` `P-10` timing out at its own 90 000 ms budget, and `engine-leader-incidents.test.ts` failing as a suite on `ENOTEMPTY` in the `mcp-test-home` teardown. `P-10` alone passes in 694 ms, and the next full coverage run was green: exit 0, 77/77 files, 2404/2404 tests, per-file 80/80 thresholds satisfied. The coverage command runs the whole `src/mcp/` directory with instrumentation under the same worker cap as every other suite, so a reviewer should ask for a second run before attributing either failure to a diff.
- Regression/control: `T6.5` was proven RED in the foreground against a scratch copy of the pre-fix `service.ts` (the file as at `928ea2db`, `sha256 bb760883…`, swapped into the worktree and restored from a saved copy of the fixed file — never `git stash`): exit 1, `"the call must answer: a throw before the door must not reject callTool: expected 'no answer' not to be 'no answer'"`, plus the unhandled rejection naming `packages/xezar/src/mcp/service.ts:440`. Raw log `red-proof-prefix-service.log` in the run's evidence dir. Green controls: the two named suites 65/65, `npm run typecheck` exit 0, `mcp-api-doc.test.ts` 8/8 without `-u` (so no shipped description moved).
- Remaining limit: the fix bounds one seam, not the class. `serveConnection`'s `.then(...)` still has no `.catch`, so any *other* future rejection inside `answer` would still be unhandled; this step deliberately did not widen the change beyond the reviewed finding. The canonical gate list is the workflow's own `gates` step, not an author run.

  that drove two real cockpits side by side and waited 75 seconds could see that. The lesson is not
  "review harder": a frozen surface is invisible to any reader of one revision, so the kit's
  separate live-QA role is the control that catches this class and a faster pipeline without it
  would ship it.

  brief's value was pointing at it before the author started, not adding anything new.

  breaks were proven red this way: copy the file to the run's evidence dir, record its sha256,
  apply the break, run, restore, re-check the sha256. Two minutes, no shared stash stack, and the
  restore is verifiable rather than assumed. This worktree shares its stash stack with every other
  task on the machine, so the alternative was never safe in the first place.

### 2026-09-21 — #804 port-memory flake was a port-range edge, not lost output, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, claude-code (claude-opus-5) — real-task observed
- Input: the leader brief for #804, the failing gate log of run `c03361ad` and PR #782's rebuild of the same file. The brief framed the fix as "read the bound port from a signal that cannot be missed instead of chunked or delayed output".
- Observed: **the brief's framing did not match the evidence.** The helper already waits for the cockpit line OR the child's exit, and parses the port only after both pipes drained, so a delivered line cannot be lost. `first.port === undefined` in ~1 s therefore meant `serve` never printed the line. Twelve concurrent reruns did not reproduce it; reading `listenOnFreePort` did: `serve` only moves UP from a busy port, macOS's ephemeral range ends at 65535 (`sysctl net.inet.ip.portrange.last`), and a sentinel there makes `serve` exit 1 with "no free port". A preload that makes the test process's first `listen(0)` return 65535 reproduced the exact gate failure (`assert.ok(first.port)`, actual `undefined`) on the pre-fix copy every time. Linux's default range ends at 60999, which is why CI never showed it.
- Observed: **the bare `assert.ok(first.port)` introduced by #782 cost the diagnosis.** The gate log carried no exit code and no output, so the cause had to be re-derived from source. Every such assertion now goes through one helper that prints both.
- Remaining limit: the original incident's output was not recorded, so the 65535 edge is the one mechanism reproduced with the identical signature, not a proven identity with that run.
- Observed: **the round-1 re-check found a second way this test can fail for #804's reason, and the round's chosen rule cannot close it.** Accepting only sentinels at least `MOVE_HEADROOM + 16` below 65535 removes the ceiling band — sentinel 65533 with a neighbour holding 65534 reproduced RED on the pre-fix file and is GREEN on the response commit — but `serve` gives up after 50 binds (`PORT_SPAN`), so a contiguous busy window anywhere in the ephemeral range still strands the busy case. One of three concurrent `npm run test:unit` pairs at the response head failed with `no free port in 56013–56062`: 49 consecutive ephemeral ports busy under the machine's ambient load, mid-range, not the ceiling. The accept decision for a mid-range port is identical in both versions, so it is not a regression from this round; it is a pre-existing exposure that needs the owner's decision, not a silent widening of this round.
- Observed: **the review-response skill asks for a note in the reviewed run's primary evidence, and this run's write guard refuses every path outside its own worktree and its own evidence directory.** Correcting `RED_PROOFS` (the `:483` red-proof run was filtered to the busy test) was refused — "this isolated task must keep project writes and Git commands inside its worktree" — so the correction was carried in this run's own evidence and in the PR body instead. A skill that must annotate a peer run's evidence needs a sanctioned path, or its instruction is silently unfulfillable.

Fixed with the same do-while pattern (`refreshPromise`/`refreshQueued`), reusing a mechanism the class already trusted rather than inventing a second one. Red-proofed on a scratch copy (`.local/xezar/tasks/8fbddd2c-996e-4a22-b246-69d93787f518/red-proof/`, sha256-verified before/after, never `git stash`): the new test fails "expected 2 to be 1" against the pre-fix file, confirming the guard is load-bearing and not a test that passes either way.
- Observed: the DeepSeek probes that measured real OS delivery times (drops in 3–6 of 10 trials, delays to several seconds) were more useful than the PR's own latency claim — "within one 250 ms debounce window" was true of the debounce mechanism and false of what a reader would take it to promise. BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md and the changelog fragment now separate the two: coalescing is guaranteed, OS delivery time is not. Worth carrying forward: a debounce constant documents COALESCING WINDOW, never DELIVERY LATENCY, and a compatibility doc for a filesystem-watch feature should say so explicitly rather than assume the reader infers it.
- Observed: Nit 5 (the watcher start in `server.ts` had no `try` of its own) was correctly scoped as hardening, not a live defect — `startWorkspaceConfigWatcher` genuinely never throws today. Wrapping the call site anyway is cheap insurance against a future change to that contract reaching an uncaught exception inside a `listening` handler and silently gating the automations poller off (the flag on the next line would never run). Applied with the existing one-warning convention (`[xez] ...`), dead code under every current test — confirmed by re-running `config-watcher.test.ts` unchanged and green.

### 2026-09-21 — #671 flake: the per-dataDir todos watch tests awaited an OS event that may never arrive, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: the owner's rule 2026-09-20 21:02 (a flaky test is REDESIGNED on a deterministic signal from the system under test — never a widened timeout, a sleep, a retry, a re-run or a register), three reds of `scopes events to the written dataDir` and one of `unsubscribe stops delivery` in sealed gate attempts of runs `dd76265e` and `700f23bf`. Deliverable: one PR — the watcher seam, the two rebuilt cases, a red proof per named break.
- Observed: **the test's only signal was the OS, and the test file said so in its own comment.** `writeUntilDelivered` rewrote `todos.json` once per 300 ms debounce window and threw at 4 000 ms, with a comment citing #204: a write that lands while macOS is registering a fresh watch is DROPPED, not delayed, and nothing reports when registration is done. So the case was not slow under load — it was waiting for an event that had already been thrown away, and 4 000 ms was the only thing between the write and the assertion.
- Observed: **the old failure message cannot distinguish a broken watcher from a busy machine, and the new one can.** With BREAK-NEVER-FIRES-FOR-A-SIBLING-DATADIR applied to `todos.ts`, the pre-fix file (scratch copy, sha256 `b87482f6…`, never `git stash`) fails after 5 250 ms with `no change event for …/project-b/.local/xezar/todos.json within 4000 ms` — the exact text of the flake it was producing on green code. The rebuilt case fails in 4 ms with `AssertionError: expected +0 to be 1`.
- Observed: **there was no deterministic signal to await, so the smallest one had to be added.** The watch exposes no armed/ready event and `fs.watch` cannot report one; a store-level change signal (the #749 shape) does not exist here because the product emits nothing but the debounced `changed`. The seam is `setTodosWatchFactory` in `packages/xezar/src/todos.ts`: the test supplies the watcher, fires the raw event itself and advances the product's own 300 ms timer with fake timers, so neither the file system nor the wall clock is in the loop. The default factory is unchanged `fs.watch`, and a new case fails if the shipped path ever stops constructing a real watcher — the seam cannot hide a broken default.
- Observed: **the lost-write window is real but it is not a defect in `todos.ts`.** A write landing before `fs.watch` is armed is lost in the product too; the consumer is the cockpit Inbox SSE stream, which refetches authoritatively on reconnect and visibility, so the cost is one missed live refresh and never a wrong state. No product behaviour was changed to chase it.
- Regression/control: BREAK-NEVER-FIRES-FOR-A-SIBLING-DATADIR reds `A fires, B stays silent` only; BREAK-SHARED-EMITTER (one emitter for every dataDir) reds the same case on the cross-fire; BREAK-UNSUBSCRIBE-KEEPS-LISTENER reds `unsubscribe stops delivery` plus both teardown guards. Green proof: the file twice in ONE vitest invocation next to `server/todos-start.test.ts` and `server/start-run-todo.test.ts` — 4 files / 40 tests in 809 ms. `npm run typecheck` clean.
- Remaining limit: nothing exercises real `fs.watch` DELIVERY any more. That is deliberate — an OS delivery is exactly the unreliable signal this PR removed — and the default-factory case pins only that the real watcher is constructed, not that macOS reports through it.
- Note: `KNOWN_LOAD_FLAKES` in `.xezar/checks/ci-watch.sh` names CI JOBs (`Cockpit browser e2e`, `MCP per-file coverage`), never this test, and the leader guide's rerun line names no spec, so the brief's deletion step was a no-op and nothing was removed from either.

### 2026-09-21 — review-response round 1 on the `gateSlots: 2` gate-lease fixture (#812), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, pi — real-task observed
- Observed: the lease verb writes its `--status-file` on the `acquired` event, BEFORE it spawns the command. An order assertion built on the two commands' own output (`a-start b-start`) therefore rests on a process race however wide the measured margin: the status file is not a signal that the command has started. The case now reads "slot 2 taken while slot 1 held" from the two status files alone, which is race-free because holder A blocks on the FIFO until the fixture releases it.
- Observed: the "fixture could not start both runs" path called `lease_release_a`, whose `wait` is unbounded, on a holder that can be queued or wedged — so the failure path could hang for the verb's 20-minute lease bound. A scratch harness that pre-held both slots and extracted the real helpers verbatim showed the fixed path ending A by its saved pid in 1 s and naming `queued` in the FAIL line, against a 20 s watchdog control where the pre-fix release was still blocked.
- Observed: extracting helpers by line number from a script under review goes stale the moment the round edits that file. The scratch harness extracts by function header plus the first closing brace instead, and fails closed when a helper is missing.
- Observed: `pgrep -P <saved pid>` plus a `kill` per child is the pattern-free way to end a saved process tree; the kit contract test bans `pkill`/`killall` in kit scripts and passes on this change.

Per the owner's 2026-09-20 21:02 rule this is recorded as evidence for #671 and none of it was retried, widened or touched.
- Observed (kit friction): **this Bash sandbox refuses every standalone or chained `sleep`, including inside a single foreground call, and directs the caller to `Monitor` instead** — but a Monitor's notification does not resume a non-final xezar workflow step; only a synchronous foreground tool call does. The brief's own polling pattern (`sleep 120; tail -3 <log>`) is blocked outright here, and using `Monitor` to wait (as the sandbox's own error message suggests) ended the turn and the step engine treated it as a stalled/failed step. What did work: a `while` loop with short internal `sleep 10`s, capped just under the Bash tool's 600 s timeout, run repeatedly as separate foreground calls until the log carried the completion marker or (for the raw `vitest --sequence.shuffle` invocation, which has no `TEST_E2E_STATUS` marker) until the process PID stopped answering `kill -0`. A brief for a non-final agent step that waits on a 15–20 minute background command should say this pattern explicitly rather than the bare `sleep N` one, since the bare one is refused here.

### 2026-09-21 — Four review Majors on the #771 refresh fix, `address-review-findings` step `fix`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` REQUEST CHANGES on PR #789 (gpt-5.6-sol, run 171580e1, four Majors, each with a file, a line and a quoted safe fragment) and the leader's `## Leader disposition` accepting all four and authorizing one fix round. Deliverable: three source fixes with red proofs, a new contract test file, two new route/tool cases, the `AC_VERIFICATION` record, a written reconciliation of two sealed gate attempts, and the `## Review response` comment.
- Observed: **the first round fixed the throwing failure and missed the returning one.** `loadTeamSkills` wrapped the listing in a `try/catch` and reported `ok: true` whenever nothing was thrown — but `listRemoteSkills` answered `[]` for an unsafe ref, an unresolvable ref and a failed `ls-tree` without ever throwing, so the catch was decoration. The shape of the bug is worth naming: a per-item outcome derived from "did this throw" is only as complete as the callee's error style, and a callee that degrades by returning empty is invisible to it. The fix returns the distinction (`{skills, failure}`) instead of inventing an exception, because three existing call sites legitimately want the empty list.
- Observed: **a schema that only documents its discriminant is a schema that does not have one.** `{ok: z.boolean(), reason: z.string().optional()}` accepted `{ok:false}` with no reason and `{ok:true, reason:'failure'}`, and the mutual `contract-parity` assertion stayed green through both — because the route was typed through the same broad definition, so the two agreed on something wider than the truth. A type check cannot catch a schema wider than the route; only a direct `safeParse` probe can, which is why the new test is five `safeParse` calls and not a type assertion. The `z.strictObject` on the SUCCESS branch is the load-bearing half: a plain `z.object` would strip the contradictory `reason` and report success.
- Observed: **a scrubber applied on the refusal path and not the success path is a scrubber with a hole.** The MCP tool removed host paths from every `failed()` answer and forwarded the refresh's per-source reasons verbatim one branch away. Writing the test found a second half of the same hole immediately — scrubbing `reason` left the absolute path in `repo`, so the payload still disclosed it. A disclosure test should assert on the whole serialized payload, not on the field the finding named.
- Regression/control: the three catching tests are red against their own pre-fix scratch copies (sha256 recorded in `RED_PROOF`); the controls that pass both ways are `answers a refresh it cannot complete, and keeps serving the catalog` (the degradation guarantee) and the #744 catalog-version parity case.
- Observed (kit friction): the review's fourth Major was an EVIDENCE defect — two sealed gate attempts at one head while the PR cited one. Reading the run record settled it in minutes: the `gates` step's own window brackets exactly one attempt, and the other sits inside a `continue-1` step from a cancel-and-resume. The seal was never ambiguous; the PR body's citation was simply the wrong attempt id. Worth a product thought: a handoff that quotes `gateEvidence.attemptId` instead of a hand-picked one cannot make this mistake.
- Remaining limit: the listing reasons are this module's own sentences rather than git's, so unlike the fetch reasons they are not upstream's wording; and the MCP scrub maps only the project root and `$HOME`, so a clone cache relocated outside both (an explicit `XEZ_HOME` elsewhere) would still be named.
