### 2026-09-20 — F-05 of the #671 flake inventory: repo-git and settings-agents stop asserting on a git ref list the spec does not own, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (Claude) — real-task observed, live-verified
- Input: the owner's brief for PR D of the #671 fix plan, the leader's "Fix plan (leader, 2026-09-20)" comment, and the read-only inventory row F-05 (`.local/xezar/tasks/fcecce3c-…/671-flake-inventory.md`, plus the ordered fix list row 9). Deliverable: `packages/web/e2e/repo-git.e2e.ts`, `packages/web/e2e/settings-agents.e2e.ts`, one shared fixture helper, the pair removed from the leader guide's rerun line, a changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **the "flake" was deterministic, and the reason it never reproduced locally is the ref list itself.** Both specs attached to the shared test env, which serves the checkout the suite runs in, and asserted on that checkout's branches. `getBranches` (`packages/xezar/src/server/git.ts:103`) filters every `xez/*` name, so a checkout whose only ref is a task branch — CI's checkout — honestly answers `[]`. Verified live against a scratch repo whose only branch was `xez/scratch`: `/api/v1/repo` returned `info.branch = "xez/scratch"` and `branches: []`. On this laptop the worktree shares the primary repo's 90-plus refs (including `main`), which is exactly why the pair passed here and was filed as a load flake a re-run could clear. Eleven CI runs, one cause.
- Observed: **the red proof reproduces the recorded symptom exactly, on a scratch copy (`git show HEAD:…`, never a stash).** The only patch to each copy was its `beforeAll`: boot the fixture helper, then rename the fixture's sole branch to `xez/scratch`. `repo-git` then failed at the pre-fix line with `AssertionError: expected 0 to be greater than 0` (the recorded symptom, 4 other cases passing — the branch case is the only one that read the ref list), and `settings-agents` timed out on `…agents-base-branch…options.length ?? 0) > 1` after 25000 ms (the recorded symptom, 5 other cases passing). Both scratch copies were deleted before the green runs, because the filter is a path substring and `redproof-repo-git.e2e.ts` contains `repo-git.e2e`.
- Observed: **`settings-agents.e2e.ts` used to write THIS repository's own `.xezar/config.json`** and restore it byte-for-byte in `afterAll` (its own comment said so). Booting its own fixture moves that write into the fixture's data root, which `stop()` deletes — the shared env's repo config is no longer touched by the suite at all. That is a side benefit of "own the fixture", not a separate fix.
- Observed: **the brief named a command that does not narrow the browser suite.** `npm run test:e2e -- <spec>` forwards `<spec>` to `test-env-up.sh`, which refuses unknown flags, and `scripts/e2e.sh` runs the whole suite either way. The documented per-spec path — `sh scripts/test-env-up.sh` once, then `npm test -- --config packages/web/e2e/vitest.config.ts <filter>` — was used for all nine runs. Already recorded in `.xezar/docs/dogfooding.d/600f520c.md` from PR A; repeated here because the brief still named the other form.
- Observed: **the brief's PR-body phrase "does not close #671" would have auto-closed the issue it means to protect, so the body carries the same meaning without a negated closing keyword next to the number.** GitHub's scanner ignores the negation: PR #737's "Do not close #676" closed #676 one second after the merge (recorded in this inbox and in the leader guide's rule "never put a closing verb next to an issue number, not even to negate it"). PR #745's body therefore says "This PR leaves #671 open" and states why no negated closing keyword is used. Checked after the edit: no closing keyword is adjacent to any issue number in the body, and `gh pr view 745 --json closingIssuesReferences` answers `[]`.
- Observed: **the first version of that very explanation re-created the hazard for a different issue.** It quoted the incident as "Do not close #676" ... "auto-closed #676", and GitHub linked PR #745 to #676 (`closingIssuesReferences` named 676) even though #671 was never named next to a keyword. The lesson is narrower than "avoid negations": the scanner reads every closing keyword in the body, so an example ABOUT the hazard is itself a live instance of it. The sentence now names no issue number beside a keyword. Worth knowing for any future PR that documents this trap.
- Regression/control: the red proof is deterministic, not statistical — one renamed branch decides it. Against that, the committed specs ran three times each green (`repo-git` 5/5 ×3, `settings-agents` 6/6 ×3) while the shared env was serving `xez/5335cfe5`, i.e. in the CI scenario, because neither reads that checkout's refs any more. The `repo-git` branch case also gained a fixture guard (`expectedCurrent === 1`) so it cannot go vacuous: the fixture is an attached checkout on `main` and exactly one row is marked current. No `retries`, no sleeps, no widened timeout, no change to `ci-watch.sh`'s job-keyed `KNOWN_LOAD_FLAKES`.
- Remaining limit: the nine runs were on one macOS laptop against the local dry-run env, not on CI. The red proof shows the pre-fix assertion fails on an xez-only checkout and the rebuilt spec passes; it does not exercise CI's own checkout. The fixture helper boots one extra server per spec, so the browser job carries two more short-lived servers.

### 2026-09-20 — review response on PR #734 (#677 wave 2 B1)

- Input: the `## Code review` verdict by Claude Fable 5.1 on PR #734 (REQUEST CHANGES: 2 Majors, 3 Minors, 1 Nit, "every security property held"), the `## QA` comment and issue #735, PR head `0c114ec8` on branch `xez/2f4977da`, base `main`. One allowed fix round, six findings. Deliverable: the six fixes, 15 named breaks with recorded red runs, the regenerated tool reference, the contract narrowing with its BC entry, the phase record and this fragment.
- **A reversal is not finished until every surface that stated the old rule is changed.** The PR
  made the workspace settings writable and fixed the cockpit hint, but three leader-facing strings
  still said "refused": the tool description, the `action` description and the
  `discover_project` `workspace_limits` row, which a leader reads at session start. Two of them
  were copy; the third was an answer FIELD (`status: 'read-only'`) with two tests pinning it. The
  rule worth keeping: when a decision reverses, grep for the old decision's WORDS, not only for
  the code that enforced it — and expect at least one of the hits to be a value rather than prose.
- **`.strict()` on an outer object says nothing about its nested ones.** `{ resources: { maxParalel: 9 } }`
  was stripped and answered 200/`applied` — a success for a change that never happened — through
  BOTH doors, because both validate with the same contract schema. Fixing it in the MCP tool alone
  would have left the cockpit's own route silently dropping the typo. The fix belonged in
  `packages/contract`, and narrowing a shared schema is a backward-compatibility entry even when
  every current caller is type-checked.
- **A test title is a claim.** "needs an operation key, and repeating one changes nothing twice"
  asserted nothing about repeating: the receipt belongs to the generic MCP door, and the tool's own
  suite calls the tool directly. Two test harnesses in this repo (the tool suite and the A/B parity
  world) both bypass the door, so the claim could only be proven in `composition.test.ts`, over the
  real bridge. Worth knowing before writing a replay assertion: *which harness actually runs the
  door you are asserting about*.
- **"Observed through a second GET" is weaker than it looks.** The hot-apply case read
  `composerDefaults` and `skillsAutoUpdate` back through `/workspace/config` — the echo, not the
  consumer. Breaking each real consumer (the run-mode resolution in `task_create`, the updater's
  `effectiveSkillsAutoUpdate`) reds the new assertions and leaves the GET green, which is exactly
  the gap the reviewer named.
- **Red proofs without `git stash`, cheaply.** `git archive HEAD` into the run's own tmp dir plus
  one `npm ci` gave a scratch copy in about a minute; a small script applied one named break at a
  time and restored from a pristine snapshot between runs. Twelve breaks, each with its targeted
  vitest output kept in the run's evidence directory. Nothing was written into the author checkout
  or the primary checkout, and the shared stash stack was never touched.
- **An independent QA verdict and a code review can disagree about the same line, and both be
  right.** The reviewer measured the hosted-mode behaviour, judged it out of scope for this PR and
  wrote it up as a note; QA reached the same fact, judged it a blocker and filed it. Neither was
  wrong: it was an owner decision, not a technical question, and it came back as "allowed — a
  server admin may change limits remotely". What the round then owed was not code but a RECORD and
  a PIN: a test that asserts the permissive behaviour, so the next person who adds the 409 breaks
  a named test instead of quietly reversing an owner decision. A test that pins an allowance is
  worth as much as one that pins a refusal.
- **A parity finding can point the opposite way from the one you fixed.** The review found that
  both doors dropped a misspelt NESTED key; QA found that the ROUTE alone accepted an unknown
  TOP-LEVEL key the MCP door refused. Same schema, two directions, one fix — but only because the
  fix went into the contract. Fixing either door would have left the other asymmetry standing and
  looked complete from whichever side was being tested.

### 2026-09-20 — #698 tilde-aware fences in changelog-check.sh, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (deepseek-api, deepseek-flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #698 (the quoted PR #696 review finding m2, with its line citations) and the owner-authored bounded-fix brief; base `main` at `02fe3a6d`. Deliverable: the tilde-aware fence rule in both walks of `.xezar/checks/changelog-check.sh`, two catcher fixtures in `.xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh`, a scratch-copy red proof, `changelog.d/698.md` and this fragment.
- Observed: **the issue's "the pair fails closed" evidence covered only one of the two walks, and the other one fails OPEN.** The issue argues that every divergence it could construct resolves with the check as the stricter gate. That holds for the top-level heading walk, and it is why both fixtures there are noisy refusals. It does not hold for the `unreleased_region` awk: with a tilde-fenced `# Something` inside the `# Unreleased` region, the backtick-only walk stops the region at that line, so a bullet edited BELOW the fence never enters the region, the per-commit comparison sees no change, and the check exits 0 on a direct edit of `# Unreleased` — a silent pass, not a noisy refusal. Red-proved in a scratch repo at `.local/xez698/repo` (pre-fix exit 0 "no direct edit", fixed exit 1). Generalisation worth carrying: "the stricter of the two" is a per-rule claim, not a per-file one; a second rule in the same file needs its own divergence hunt.
- Observed: **the cp scratch copy is the right red-proof instrument for a shell check.** `cp .xezar/checks/changelog-check.sh .local/xez698/pre-fix.sh` before the first edit, `git hash-object` on the copy to prove it is the HEAD revision, then run both copies over the same fixture files. Nothing in the worktree is ever stashed, the fixtures stay byte-identical for both runs, and the proof is re-runnable by a reviewer in two commands.
- Observed (tooling, cost me time): **the shell guard's path scan reads a leading `~~~` as a home-relative path.** A command writing fixture text that begins with three tildes was refused with "names a path outside the worktree in the primary checkout", for both a `printf` argument and a heredoc body, while the same command with any other leading text passed. That is exactly the content this task's fixtures are made of, so the fixtures had to be written inside the worktree (`.local/xez698/`) rather than in `/tmp`. Not a blocker; a false positive a reviewer should know about.
- Regression/control: the two new fixtures are catchers — fixture (a) red against the pre-fix copy ("has 2 '# Unreleased' headings") and green against the fixed script; fixture (b) red ("has 0 '# 0.2.0 (' headings") and green. The controls pass both ways and are declared as such: the pre-existing backtick fence fixture and the repository's own `CHANGELOG.md` both exit 0 on both copies. `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` reported 669 passed / 0 failed with the two new cases in the run log.

Writing the evidence-dir sentence into only the seven read-only skills would have failed the catalog with "shared contract differs from the other self-contained roles". The fix is therefore the SAME conditional sentence in all twenty skills — a fragment when the workflow has a committing handoff step, the evidence dir otherwise — plus the same conditional in the project `systemPrompt`, which is the one place that decides. All nine are covered by the guard, because leaving `issue-triage` or `issue-create` on the unconditional sentence would have reproduced #721 on those paths.

The guard matches the path substrings (`/\.local\/xezar\/tasks\/<runId>\/dogfooding\.md/`) rather than a quoted form, so it reads either spelling.

### 2026-09-20 — #676 PR 1 of 3, review round 1 (address the code review), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude (Opus 5) — real-task observed

- Input: the reviewer's `## Code review` on PR #728 (REQUEST CHANGES, 3 Majors, 2 Minors, 3 Nits), the same spec and issue. Deliverable: all eight findings fixed in one round, each with its own red proof.
- **Every one of the three Majors was the same failure wearing three coats: a branch that reads "resumed fine" when nothing was resumed.** A backend that ignores `spec.resume` (OpenCode), a backend that refuses the id at runtime, and an account mismatch nobody tested. The round-1 lesson is the AGENTS.md fail-open rule read one level deeper: it is not enough to pair a silent pass-through with a test for the empty-input case, because the eligibility question itself can be answered from the wrong evidence. A recorded session id is evidence that an id was minted, never that the runner will reopen it. Decide from what the runner can do.
- **The reviewer's red proof was worth more than the author's eight.** The author's B1–B8 all broke code that existed; the reviewer's probe scripted a case the code had no branch for at all (`error: 'No conversation found with session ID: …'` on the resumed turn) and got `status=failed`, 2 spawns. A break-the-code proof can only pin what was written; a script-the-environment proof finds what was not.
- **A second execution inside one step is a closure, not a loop counter.** The runtime fall-back re-runs the step's own spawn-and-await once, so that body became a local `execute()` and the mutable turn state (`sessionError`, `lastTurnText`, `turnText`, `sawActivity`, `startTokens`) is reset between the two calls. What made it safe to bound was naming what must NOT be retried: a cancel, xezar's own memory-guard pause, and — the important one — a resumed turn that produced text or a tool call and only then failed.
- **A Minor that says "INFERRED, not executed" is a reading assignment, and it paid.** The reviewer's guess that Codex double counts tokens on a resumed thread was right, and reading four runners settled it in minutes: three accumulate from zero per session object, Codex reports the thread's cumulative total. The fix is one list beside the resume policy, and the test that pins it needed one new optional field on the shared scripted runner.

### 2026-09-20 — #676 PR 2 gate-attempt provenance, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (deepseek-api, deepseek-v4-flash) — real-task observed

- Input: issue #676 and the accepted spec `.local/xezar/tasks/edbf87fc-.../676-spec.md` § "PR 2 — kit: gate-attempt provenance" (PR 1 merged as `042a26cc`). Deliverable: `repo-gates.sh --producer <author|gates>`, `producer` recorded in the attempt, a seal refusal for `author`, the ten workflows' `gates` commands, docs, tests, a changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **the workflow definition a run executes is frozen at run creation, so a workflow-command change does not reach an in-flight run.** `RunManager` persists `workflowDef` on the run record and `runWorkflowSteps` walks that object; `reviveWorkflow` prefers it over re-reading `.xezar/workflows/`. The task's own note ("your gates step must pass `--producer gates`") assumes the workflow FILE governs, which is true only for runs created after the change. This run's persisted `gates` command is `.xezar/checks/repo-gates.sh --fast`, so its gates attempt would record `author` and the new seal would refuse it, failing the run at `evidence`. This is the same shape as `docs/lessons/changing-working-mechanisms.md` § "A replacement that ships OFF is not a replacement": the default path of an in-flight run silently lost its seal.
- Observed: **the red proofs were taken on scratch copies, never `git stash`, and the controls are named.** A `.local/xezar/tmp/` harness drives the real `gate-results.mjs` CLI over synthetic attempts: an author attempt is refused, a `gates` attempt seals, a legacy attempt with no `producer` field seals. Against copies carrying each break the matching assertion goes red — B9 (drop `--producer gates` from `bug-fix.yaml`) stops declaring the gates step; B10 (`record.producer ?? "author"`) refuses the legacy record; B11 (fold `author` into absent) lets the author attempt seal. B10 and B11 are the two directions of the same fail-open, which is why they are separate cases rather than one.
- Observed: **the seal refusal needed a third branch, not a second.** The first draft's natural spelling — "refuse anything that is not `gates`" — refuses a legacy record and would strand every attempt already on disk, which is exactly the "a fail-open helper needs a populated-input guarantee" failure in the other direction. The shipped rule is `record.producer === "author"`: absence, `author` and `gates` are three answers, and the fixtures pin all three.
- Observed: **`gate_parallel.test.mjs`'s `outerFixture` runs `repo-gates.sh` with no flag, so its attempts now record `author`.** Those cases assert the attempt record and the early-stop behaviour, never a seal, so they stay green — the check that the change is additive to the recording half. The infra sealing fixtures, by contrast, model the workflow's `gates` step and now drive `GATE_PRODUCER=gates`; the provenance fixtures drive `author`, the product default and the legacy shape explicitly.
- Remaining limit: the migration that keeps an in-flight run sealable (`gate_resolve_producer`: no flag + no `XEZ_TASK_ID` + the workflow file declares the flag → `gates`) is inferred from the process environment, because a check step has no run identity at all (`lib/common.sh`). It is one release wide and must be removed then; a person running `repo-gates.sh --fast` by hand in a migrated checkout is also classified `gates`, which is the residual fail-open and the reason the exception is temporary.

### 2026-09-20 — F-26 and F-29 of the #671 flake inventory: the run store treats a vanished data directory as a shutdown, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: the owner's rule 2026-09-20 06:55 (a flaky test is FIXED or REBUILT, never registered, retried, quarantined or given a wider timeout; no CI re-runs), the read-only inventory rows F-26 and F-29 (`.local/xezar/tasks/fcecce3c-cdf0-40b1-95dd-2a0b8001f7e5/671-flake-inventory.md`), issue #631 and the sealed gate attempt `f33a31c7` of run `44cca504`. Deliverable: one PR — the store fix, the fixture routing, the wait rebuild, a red proof for each.
- Observed: **the gate red named in the brief is NOT the same mechanism as the ENOENT logged in the same run, and reading the log settled it in one step.** `44cca504`'s attempt 0001 shows both: an `[xez] failed to save runs.json: ENOENT … project-alpha/.local/xezar/runs.json.tmp` attributed to P-14, and P-14 itself failing at 30 468 ms with `timed out waiting for both sessions to wait for a reply`. The log is a PREVIOUS case's A/B world firing its debounce 300 ms after its dispose — vitest attributes a late `console.*` to whatever is running. P-14's own failure is a fixed inner budget: `until(predicate, what, timeoutMs = 30_000)` polled every 25 ms inside a case whose own budget is 90 s. Two defects, one log line, and the brief's "same defect surfacing as a timeout" holds only for the ENOENT half.
- Observed: **the A/B fixture's `dispose()` did not await `contexts.disposeAll()`, which is async.** That is the concrete producer of the `project-alpha` ENOENT: the context teardown (manager dispose → store flush) raced the `rmSync(base)` two lines below it. `await` plus `close()` fixes the fixture; the store fix makes it harmless everywhere else.
- Observed: **the source's own teardown said "the store is closed" and called `flush()`, which does not close anything.** `flush()` cancels the debounce and writes, then leaves the store armed for the next `touch()`. `RunStore` now has a real `close()` (cancel, write, refuse later writes, idempotent, safe on a directory that has already gone) and `project-context.ts`'s `teardown` calls it, so the doc comment is true.
- Observed: **the fix scales to 312 call sites without touching them, and that is the argument for putting it in the store rather than in the fixtures.** `RunStore.open(…)` appears 312 times across 145 test files; a scan for "removes a directory holding a store without flushing it" found 17. Those 17 plus the two shared fixtures are routed through one helper (`runs/store.testkit.ts`, `closeStoreAndRemove`); the other ~128 are covered by the store's own vanished-directory branch, which is why the store change is the fix and the routing is defence in depth.
- Observed: **the red proofs are deterministic at the store layer and NOT reproducible end to end on an idle machine.** BREAK-STORE-VANISHED-DIR-LOGS (the pre-fix `saveNow` error path restored on a scratch copy, never `git stash`): 4 of 7 red, each `AssertionError: expected [ Array(1) ] to deeply equal []` with `+ "[xez] failed to save runs.json: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open '…/runs.json.tmp'"`, while both GUARD controls stayed green. BREAK-FIXTURE-NO-FLUSH (the helper's `close()` dropped): exactly 1 red, `expected false to be true` on `store.isClosed`. But with BOTH breaks applied, `acceptance-parity.test.ts` passed 51/51 with no ENOENT at all — the fixture race needs the load the recorded gate had, so #631's `EnvironmentTeardownError` is still not reproduced end to end. What IS reproduced, deterministically, is the late `console.*` the issue asked someone to find and silence.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/mcp/acceptance-parity.test.ts packages/xezar/src/mcp/stale-write.test.ts packages/xezar/src/runs` is 19 files / 442 tests green three times in a row (40.3 s, 40.5 s, 41.5 s); `npm run typecheck` clean. Nothing was added to `KNOWN_LOAD_FLAKES` and nothing removed from it: as PRs A and G of this series already recorded, that list names JOBs (`Cockpit browser e2e`, `MCP per-file coverage`), never the two tests this PR fixes, and the leader guide's rerun line has named no spec since PR D.
- Remaining limit: the wait rebuild removes the inner budget, so a genuinely stuck wait now fails as the case's own `Test timed out in 90000ms` instead of naming the condition. The `what` label is kept at every call site for the reader, but it no longer reaches a failure message — the same trade #749 made.

### 2026-09-20 — A red proof that stayed green, and a cache a unit test must never reach

- **The `key: maybeUndefined` guard needs to be asserted on the OBJECT, not on its JSON.** The
  AC-02 test for the new `tag` key checked `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(entry))`, and
  `JSON.stringify` drops `tag: undefined` — so the test was green against exactly the break
  AGENTS.md names as the recurring contract-parity defect, and `npm run typecheck` was green too
  (`string | undefined` is assignable to `tag?: string` without `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`).
  Only the named red proof exposed it. The rule that follows: an "absent key" assertion must run
  against the value as well as its wire form, or it proves nothing about the shape.
- **`xezCacheDir()` is `~/.cache/xez` and no env var moves it**, so a unit test for anything that
  reads the team-skills bare clone reaches the developer's real cache by default — non-deterministic
  locally, empty on CI. Pinning `setActiveStateLayout(projectStateLayout(<temp>))` puts the cache
  inside the fixture and is the same switch single-project mode flips, so the test doubles as the
  AC-13 evidence. Worth knowing before writing the test, not after.
- **A recording `git` shim earlier on `PATH` is a cheap, real proof of "no network call".** The
  module runs `git` through `execFile`, so there is no runner to spy on; a shell script that logs
  `"$@"` and then `exec`s the real git turned "we believe this is local-only" into an assertion that
  no invocation matched `fetch|clone|ls-remote|push|pull`. Emptying `PATH` in the same way is the
  injection for the "git is not installed" degradation.
- **A developer's own git config breaks fixture repositories.** `git tag v1.2.3` failed with
  `fatal: no tag message?` on this machine because the user's global config annotates tags. Fixture
  setup now pins `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, which is the
  difference between a test that passes everywhere and one that passes on CI only.

### 2026-09-20 — #677 wave 2 slice B3, `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Opus 5 (westagilelabs-priv) — real-task observed
- Input: the accepted spec `677-spec.md` § 2 item B3 and § 4 Q1, the merged slices B1 (#734) and B2 (#748) with their diffs, the `## Code review` comment on #748 (four minors, two nits — M1, M4, Nit 1 and Nit 2 folded in here; M2 and M3 left for B6 by the brief), and the leader's brief. Deliverable: `set_workspace_ui_state` and `import_skills` as real writes through `PUT /workspace/ui-state`, their records and docs, the four named breaks, and the B2 leftovers.
- Observed: **"the door decides the key set, the route decides the values" is what made the acceptance criteria observable at all.** B1's argument schema IS the route's contract schema, so no body exists that the door accepts and the route refuses — which is why B1 could honestly write "there is no B1 body the route rejects and the tool accepts". Copying that shape here would have made the spec's own named breaks 2 and 4 untestable: every over-long list would have been refused at the door and the route's bound, its 400 and its body cap would never have been reached through the leader's door. Taking the five key schemas from the contract's TOLERANT read-side shape instead — still the contract, never hand-written — leaves the values to the one route that owns them, and the two cases now fail when the route's validator or its `bodyLimit` is removed. The design question and the testability question had the same answer, which is not always true.
- Observed: **a test that errors either way is not a proof, and the control run is what says so.** The body-cap case first asserted only "the call errored". Applying its named break (the `use(…bodyLimit…)` deleted from the route) left it green: without the cap the over-sized body simply reaches the schema and is refused as a bad value, 400 instead of 413. Pinning the STATUS — 413, refused by the cap before the schema had an opinion — turned it red. A case that survives its own break looks exactly like a case that works.
- Observed: **some guarantees cannot be broken from inside, and saying so is better than inventing a proof.** The review's M4 asked for an assertion that a leader-supplied host path never reaches the audit trail, with a named break. The assertion is in (over the whole trail file, with a control proving the path really was written). The break is not: three separate layers refuse a path — the record carries a payload DIGEST, the contract's `AUDIT_ID_RE` bans `/` in every identifier, and the redaction policy strips path-shaped string leaves before hashing. Two attempts to push the value into the row (through `fieldNames`, raw and chunked) were both sanitised to `[]`, and the case went red on the NEIGHBOURING B1 assertion instead. Recorded as a pinned invariant, not counted as a red proof.
- Observed (the ripple, again, and larger): flipping three records to `serves` pulled in three inventory statuses, two hard-coded covered counts in two test files, the coverage map's record rows AND its case table (whose generated order made the P-46 row's position load-bearing), P-29's record list and refusal list, A-22's global-administration list, the four-door audit matrix (F10 needed a new MCP refusal once the preference bag stopped being one), `composition.test.ts`'s boundary example, and a 2026-09-11 sentence in the definition-of-done record that named `import_skills` as refused. Nothing let me claim the coverage without moving every record that states it.
- Regression/control: four red proofs, each on a scratch COPY of the file, never `git stash` (shared with every worktree on this machine), each restored and re-hashed: `operationId` dropped from both `ACTION_FIELDS` rows → the operation-key case red; the route's `jsonZodValidator` removed → the bounds case and P-46 red (2 files); both actions added to `AUDIT_MCP_READS` → `audit-inventory.test.ts` rule 1 and the four-door matrix red; the ui-state `bodyLimit` removed → the cap case red. Logs under `<evidence>/red-proofs/`. `npm run test:coverage:mcp` exit 0 with the per-file 80 %/80 % floor held.
- Process slip, recorded rather than smoothed over: the self-review round was applied and then counted, where the rule is to count first. The count is right (1 of 2); only its order was wrong, and a round that is applied and never recorded is exactly the failure that rule exists to stop.
- Remaining limit: no live MCP client drove any of it — every case runs in-process over the real app, unlike the #748 review, which drove a real `xezar mcp` bridge and found things a fixture would not have. The `fail(answer)` arm of both writes (a read-only home answering 500) still has no test, unchanged by this slice.

### 2026-09-20 — A badge that contradicted the two lines under it, and where a policy window belongs

- **A state word that means "one of four different things" will eventually contradict the screen.**
  `unknown` covered no-clone, never-fetched, diverged AND a check older than six hours, and the
  fourth case printed two known versions under a badge reading "Version unknown". Two code reviews
  and a QA pass all read that as correct — because it IS correct as a comparison result. Only the
  design review, reading the words as a person would, saw the contradiction. A verdict enum is UI
  copy the moment a surface renders it verbatim; splitting `stale-check` out of `unknown` in the
  zod schema cost one line and removed the contradiction at the source rather than in the renderer.
- **Where a policy window lives decides how many places can disagree about it.** The fix could have
  been a UI-side "is `fetchedAt` older than six hours" test. That would have put the passive-fetch
  window in two files, and the MCP door — which relays the same block to a leader — would still have
  said `unknown`. Keeping the six hours in `compareState` and letting the state word travel means
  both doors say the same thing and neither re-derives the rule.
- **A `refetchInterval` added "only for the cold-boot race" outlives the race.** The catalog poll
  was bounded by `state === 'unknown'`, and an unreachable source is `unknown` forever — so the
  bound was not a bound. Its replacement is a pure, exported function with an attempt cap, which is
  also the only reason the "poll continues past the bound" red proof could be written at all: a
  predicate inlined in a hook option has no seam to prove anything against.

### 2026-09-20 — #766 review response round 1 of 1 (the outbound health probe's edges), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed
- Input: the immutable `## Code review` on PR #766 (reviewer run `f915fb83`, Claude Opus 5, APPROVE with three Minors and two Nits against reviewed head `05baae9d`, branch `xez/1f89880e`) and the leader's `## Leader disposition`, which dispositions all five as FIX and says this is the one fix round the PR gets (owner rule 2026-09-19). Deliverable: `redirect: 'error'` on the probe, the probe-reach doc block, the `fetchHealth` test suite, the cache eviction, the second-read sentence, `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` § Instance mode, the changelog fragment, this fragment and the `## Review response` comment.
- Observed: **the function that touches the network was the one with no test, and that is not a coincidence.** Every case in three suites injected `probe`, so `fetchHealth`'s four failure branches and its redirect behaviour were unpinned — the reviewer could exercise them by hand and find both defects precisely because nothing held them. Nine cases against real loopback `node:http` listeners cost 683 ms of suite time and turn "I drove it once" into a standing guard. An injected seam is what makes a module testable and is also where its untested half hides.
- Observed: **`fetch`'s default `redirect: 'follow'` is a reach the code never states.** The identity check ("only an answer naming THIS project") reads as a containment guarantee, and a 302 defeats it without touching it: the answer comes from the second server while the row links to the first. The red proof is one line — delete `redirect: 'error'` and the 302 case reports `{ kind: 'named', bootProject: 'beta' }` where `{ kind: 'no-answer' }` was expected. Any outbound `fetch` a server makes on an address it did not choose should name its redirect policy, because the default one is a policy too.
- Observed: **"probes health over loopback" was prose, and prose is what the next reader trusts.** The code asks `lastListen.host` as written, which is correct — a `--bind-host` sibling must stay linkable — so the fix was to make the sentence true and pin the behaviour with a named test (`non-loopback-host-asked-as-written`), not to narrow the code to match the sentence. Narrowing to loopback would have passed every existing test and silently dropped a supported deployment.
- Observed (the DELIVERED-versus-branch conflict, sixth run to record it after `d3e1d44e.md`, `7841e6ae.md`, `4e308830.md`, `5a52ede7.md` and `2ce3d44d.md`): the skill's `DELIVERED` path and `worktree-preflight.sh`'s `branch.has-own-commits` still cannot both hold, and `worktree-git.sh push`'s refspec is fixed to this run's own branch. The compliant path taken here was to set this task's own branch to the reviewed head (`git reset --hard 05baae9d`, no merge of a newer `main`, so the PR's diff gains nothing it did not ask for), commit the fix through `worktree-git.sh commit`, and push `HEAD` to `refs/heads/xez/1f89880e` with a plain `git push`. Own commits are present, so readiness passes on `branch.has-own-commits` and no `DELIVERED` record is written — and, unlike the empty-branch shape the skill describes, the workflow's own `gates` step then runs against the very tree that was pushed, which is the stronger evidence.
- Regression/control: focused `npm test` over `instance-liveness`, `instance-field-api`, `contract-parity.projects`, `instance-mode-api`, `route-parity`, `typed-bodies` and `session-binding` is **7 files / 86 tests green**; `npm run typecheck` clean. The working file was restored from its copy and re-hashed to `019d3e34…` after each.

The tree settles it: the ~50 pre-existing specs use `querySelector` and `data-slot` by design, so a 66-file scan is red on arrival and would demand rewriting specs the same sentence forbids touching. The guard therefore scans the browser-test package's 16 files, and the narrowing is written down in the guard header, in `agent-browser.md` and in the PR body rather than left implicit — a silent narrowing is the failure mode this avoids.

The guard reads command text, not the filesystem, and a variable-expanded path defeats its worktree check.
- Observed: **the scratch-copy red proof is cheap and does not need the stash stack.** `rsync -a --exclude node_modules --exclude .local` into `.local/xezar/tmp/redproof`, one symlinked `node_modules`, and `vitest run --root <scratch> --project web <bare-file-name>` reproduced every break. Two details cost a round each: `--root` resolves the CLI filter against EACH project root, so `packages/web/src/foo.test.ts` matches nothing and the bare file name is required; and the four breaks were reverted by re-copying the one touched file rather than re-syncing the tree.
- Observed: **pinning the fail-open branch end to end found more than the unit case did.** The two guards' explicit empty-input throws were unit-tested, and then the whole 66-file set was moved away in the scratch copy: four tests across both files failed closed with "the glob matched no *.e2e.ts files — refusing to pass vacuously". A comment-vs-code scanner is exactly the fail-open helper AGENTS.md names, so both halves — the throw and the surrounding non-vacuity assertions — were proven rather than asserted.
- Regression/control: the four named breaks each went red on the scratch copy with the exact lines quoted in the PR body (`/Whole suite: 1 200 s/` unmatched, `says 39 *.e2e.ts files, the directory holds 66`, `guide-01-getting-started.e2e.ts: data-slot ×1`, and the empty-glob pair); with the locator break in place the runtime-ceiling guard stayed green, proving the scan and not the spec is what catches it. In the worktree the two new guards plus `e2e-file-parallelism`, `e2e-capture-include`, `design-guardian` and `e2e-guide-browser-retry` pass (53 tests) and `npm run typecheck` is clean. No spec file changed.
- Remaining limit: the scan sees one line at a time in one file. A locator assembled at runtime from pieces no single line contains is invisible, a `data-slot` reached through an imported helper is out of reach, and the scanner's `}`-inside-`${…}` nesting is documented as an approximation. The runtime ceilings are records, not enforcement: nothing yet fails a run that exceeds 60 s for one file, so the numbers bound a review judgement rather than a gate.

### 2026-09-20 — #424 Settings loading gutters and reading measure, `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, claude (opus) — real-task observed
- Input: PR #774 (head `d118f534`) as a completed candidate to supersede, the sol design review on it (run `c837179a`, Major B-1), the sonnet QA on it (run `55dc3d92`, PASS), the D-03 record in `docs/design-system/decisions.md`. Deliverable: the same Settings changes on a branch fresh from `main`, the design Major fixed, one new test with two red proofs, a live measurement, a changelog fragment and this one. Result: PR #780.
- Observed: **superseding a PR is cheaper than it looks when the carry-over list is explicit, and the cost is entirely in what is NOT carried.** `git apply --include=…` over `gh pr diff 774` re-applied eleven files in one command with no conflict. The judgement was in the two files the diff contained that the brief's list did not name — `designs/README.md` and `designs/design-system-air/README.md`, whose status lines say "#774 closed #424". Renumbering them silently would have been the easy move; they are reported as deliberately not carried instead, because a design status line naming the wrong PR is worse than one that is a round behind.
- Observed: **the design review found what a class-name test cannot: `p-list … md:p-group` on both states made the classes agree and the geometry still move.** The loaded pane is centred on a reading measure; the loading line was not. Measured on the running cockpit at 1280 px, the first content edge sat at 496 px pending and 564 px loaded — a 68 px jump under a test that was green. **A rhythm contract that compares only the gutter is half a contract**, and the half it omits is the one that decides where the content actually starts.
- Observed (the contract shape that survived the second half): the first design compared token SETS, and it was wrong in a way that passes. Ten sections carry gutter and measure on one element; Bookmarklets carries the gutter on its scroll container and the measure on the panel inside it. Those two spellings carry **identical tokens** and place the first content edge a whole gutter apart. The test therefore compares an ORDERED chain of roles (`gutter+measure:max-w-2xl` vs `gutter`, `measure:max-w-2xl`) against an explicit per-file table, plus a guard that every measure the table names is a real container of that section, so the table cannot drift into fiction.
- Observed (a substring bug in the test, found before any commit): the loaded-container filter used `className.includes('p-list')`, and `gap-list` contains `p-list`. `project-setup-section.tsx` reported three phantom offenders. A class-name scan wants whole-token matching every time; the original #774 test had the `\bp-list\b` boundary and the rewrite dropped it.
- Observed (forcing a pending state live, without a fixture): a full page load is a race — the section had loaded inside 1.5 s, so a wait for the loading node timed out. What worked through the documented harness: land on a project Settings section that does NOT read `/config` (`agent-config`), patch `window.fetch` to delay `/config` by 12 s, then CLICK the Agents nav link so the section mounts under the patch. A client-side navigation keeps the patch alive where a `goto` discards it. The control number in the PR body came from the same live page with the measure classes stripped off the node — a real computed-style reading, not an arithmetic estimate.
- Regression/control: the `no-arbitrary-spacing` allowlist is untouched at two rows, ceiling two; `design-guardian`, `design-system-drift`, the B7 retired-spelling scan, `skills.test.tsx` and all 25 Settings route suites stayed green. `tsx` needed `TMPDIR=/tmp` for its IPC socket — the task's own tmp path under `.local/xezar/tmp/<runId>/` is long enough to fail `listen EINVAL` on macOS.
- Remaining limit: `mcp-connection-section.tsx` gates its pending state on `health.isPending || projects.isPending`, both shell-wide, so it cannot be forced live in isolation; the source contract is the authority for that one section and the PR body says so. The live measurement covers Agents only.

  `XEZ_MOCK_ARGS_FILE` hook lets a test assert `--resume` reached the real CLI argv. Neither had to
  be built.
- **`git stash` is the wrong tool here and the skill is right to ban it.** The stash stack is shared
  with every other worktree on this machine, and eight applications of a break is eight chances to
  pop a peer's work. A `cp` of the one file under change is strictly simpler and has no shared state.
- **Two eligibility conditions could not be checked in one place, and saying so in a comment was the
  honest fix.** Five of the six conditions for the cheap return are decidable in the workflow loop;
  the agent-account (`profileId`) one is only known after `agentEnvForStep` resolves inside the step
  spawn. Rather than pretend otherwise, the re-check lives there with a comment naming why, and its
  fall-back emits a `note` — the spec's own warning was that a silent fall-back to a fresh session
  is the failure mode this whole path exists to avoid.
- **Forcing a negative case needed a store listener, not a new seam.** "No recorded session" and "a
  session on another backend" are states the engine never produces itself, so the test mutates the
  step record from a `store.on('run')` handler the moment the gate step starts running. That kept
  both breaks testable without adding a test-only hook to `run.ts`.

### 2026-09-20 — review response on PR #764, one Major plus four Minors and a Nit on the MCP agent-accounts slice, `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` comment by Claude Fable 5.1 (run `9463662f`) on PR #764, pinned to reviewed head `24fe2a0922699c61692a107423fc5b7806fbaf76`, REQUEST CHANGES with six findings, each carrying its own named experiment; the leader's brief fixing all six in one round and adjudicating Minor 2 (echo `configDir` only when the call sent one). What worked without weakening anything: reset this run's OWN branch to the PR head, author and commit there through `worktree-git.sh commit` (preflight passes — right directory, right branch), then push `xez/a1e5ea04:xez/e212dc2a` explicitly from this worktree, and record `DELIVERED`. The skill anticipates the delivery but assumes the fix lands on a branch this task never commits to; a task branch cut from the PR head is the shape that satisfies both the guard and the delivery, and it has the side benefit that the `gates` step runs against the same tree that was pushed.

- Observed (author-level correction inside the author phase): the first cut of the identity redaction matched **any** email-shaped run of route error text, which also matches an scp-style git remote (`git@github.com:org/repo.git`). A redaction that mangles honest text is its own defect, so the rule was narrowed to a double-QUOTED run carrying an email shape — what the service quotes back is what a person typed. The red proof stayed red on the same line after the narrowing, which is the check that the tightening did not quietly disarm it.
- Regression/control: five named breaks re-run on a scratch copy of this worktree (`.local/xezar/tmp/<runId>/red-scratch`, its own tree, never `git stash`) — the redaction, the unconditional `configDir` echo, the caller's-provider echo, `fieldNames` on `account.select`, and the per-route hosted check — each red on the assertion it was written for, plus the marker-only control green. Then green: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/mcp packages/contract .../contract-parity.requests.test.ts` (79 files, 2355 tests), `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:coverage:mcp` (exit 0, `tools/project-config.ts` 98.41 % lines / 83.60 % branches), `check-links.mjs`, `fenced-quotes.mjs` and `changelog-check.sh`. The canonical gate list is NOT run here: the workflow's `gates` step owns it, and per kit fact #756 it seals at the branch head before this response commit — so the gate evidence this run carries is a confirmation of that tree, not of the pushed head, and the PR's own CI run is what covers the pushed head.

### 2026-09-20 — A literal key inventory catches what a derived one cannot

Run `a2554a58`, `bug-fix` workflow, #677 wave 1 (the two settings request schemas move into
`packages/contract`, plus an MCP ↔ route request-parity harness).

- **A parity test built from derived schemas proves nothing about a new key.** The MCP's write
  schema is `setConfigInputSchema.omit({ maxParallel: true })`, so a key added to the contract
  flows into BOTH sides and a key-for-key comparison stays green while nobody has decided whether
  the leader may send it. Writing the accepted keys out as a literal list in the test is what makes
  the addition stop. The same literal list caught a mistake in its own first run — the draft
  claimed `PATCH /projects/:projectId` accepts a `name` key, and the contract has only
  `maxParallel` and `tags`.
- **What a deleted schema copy was load-bearing FOR was not what it was for.** Two things the
  `server.ts` copies carried are invisible to every type-level parity check in the repository: the
  custom `'must be at most 20000 characters'` message on `systemPrompt`, which is the literal wire
  text a cockpit toast renders, and the shape's KEY ORDER, which decides the field order inside a
  multi-issue `{ error }` string (the contract had `resources` and `agentDefaults` the other way
  round). Both were preserved deliberately and each got its own runtime assertion; a types-only
  harness would have shipped both changes silently.
- **The narrowing a spec cannot see until you grep.** The spec asked A3 to compare "the schema the
  MCP accepts" with "the schema the route accepts" for each write action. Only two routes really
  validate with a contract schema (`PUT /config`, after this PR, and `PATCH
  /projects/:projectId`); the workflows, automations and agent-config routes still declare their
  own. Comparing the rest against a contract schema no route reads would have been a false parity
  claim, so those actions are classified with the reason instead — and the test refuses to leave a
  NEW write action unclassified, which is the part that keeps the exclusion honest.
- **Red proofs on `cp` copies, never `git stash`.** The stash stack is shared with every peer
  worktree on this machine. Copying the two files to the run's own evidence directory and restoring
  from there cost nothing and cannot pop somebody else's work.

### 2026-09-20 — review round 1 on PR G (#671, F-20): the rebuilt `auto-resume` queue-hold case was still insensitive to the account-hold gate, and is now rebuilt to pin both gate sites — real-task observed, fixture-tested, pi
- Input: the leader's brief for review-response round 1 on PR #750, the full `## Code review` comment (reviewer Claude Sonnet 5, run `42577f20`), and the PR head `d06c17ba` on branch `xez/34b34a76`. Deliverable: the rebuilt case in `packages/xezar/src/workflows/auto-resume.test.ts`, an updated changelog fragment, this fragment.
- Observed: **the review's Major reproduced exactly, and it was measurable, not inferred.** Instrumenting `pump()` on the unmodified PR head printed `accountHolds()` empty on every sweep the case made, and a trace in `requeueWhileHeld` showed all five spawns with `held=false`. So the case's "the rest never start" was slot cycling with `maxParallel: 2`, exactly as the reviewer found — the hold was never consulted in the window the case observed.
- Observed: **why the old counts could not see it, traced rather than guessed.** `whenTrue` fires on the store's own `run` event, and `armAutoResume` publishes `autoResumeAt` inside `dropActive` BEFORE `releaseSlot()`; but `execute()` sets `status: running` / `startedAt` only after `await loadConfig()`, so a count of `startedAt` right after an awaited sweep is a race against the dequeue, not a fact. With the FIFO check broken the case still passed because the spawn-time gate (`requeueWhileHeld`) hands the dequeued runs straight back — identical counts, a different mechanism.
- Observed: **`requeueWhileHeld` is unreachable for a WORKTREE run when the dequeue-time gate works.** It is called at the top of `execute`, before `createWorktree`, so its window is only `await loadConfig`; a worktree run that the FIFO check leaves in `queue` never reaches it. Making the case sensitive to that gate therefore required the measured shape the sibling case uses — an IN-PLACE run parked on the repo-root lease (#438, holding no slot per #347) — so the rebuilt case now has one phase per gate site.
- Observed (flakiness found and fixed while strengthening, not present at the reviewed head): the first cut of the new phase A hung 2 runs in 6. Root cause: `whenTrue(() => started() === 2)` was registered while the second-account control run still held one of the two slots, so only ONE of the five started, and that one re-established the hold before its partner could — the predicate then never became exactly 2. Fixed by waiting for the control to reach `failed` before releasing the holder; 14/14 single-case runs green after that.
- Observed (second flake, same cause class): an exact-match predicate (`=== 2`) can also be missed if the value jumps past it before the listener registers. The in-place phase was made immune by waiting on `inPlaceQueued() === 4 || inPlaceScheduled() > 1`, where the second disjunct is the broken-gate outcome, so a regression fails a real assertion instead of hanging the case.
- Observed: **both reviewer mutations now turn the rebuilt case red, deterministically.** On a scratch COPY of `packages/xezar/src/workflows/run.ts` (copy aside, edit the copy in place, restore from the copy — never `git stash`): forcing `requeueWhileHeld` to never hold fails `auto-resume.test.ts:421` with `AssertionError: expected 2 to be 1`; forcing `pump()`'s FIFO check to never hold fails `auto-resume.test.ts:355` with `AssertionError: expected 5 to be +0`. Restoring the copy leaves the case green and `git diff` on `run.ts` empty.
- Observed (tooling): the shell guard again refused any command naming a path in the primary checkout, including `find` of the evidence root; the red proofs' logs and the aside copy had to live under `/tmp`. The kit's checks read the primary by design — a shell command of the agent's own still does not.
- Remaining limit: the red proof is two production mutations and the focused file, not the canonical gate list (the workflow's `gates` step owns that run). The in-place phase's second disjunct proves the broken-gate outcome on this machine; it does not enumerate every interleaving of the lease hand-off.

### 2026-09-20 — #720 the second-window automations-gate regression test could go falsely green against a removed guard under slow git, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #720 (labels `bug`, `testing`, `release-0.17.0`) filed by the owner from the `## Code review (re-check, round 1)` comment on PR #717, which quotes the reviewer's finding and a tried correction; the bounded brief scoping the change to `packages/xezar/src/server/automations-gate.test.ts` with no product source change. Deliverable: the `getRepoInfo` gate in the `vi.mock('./git.ts')` factory reshaped to read the real call before the park, the mock's doc comment and the second-window wait comment rewritten to be true, `changelog.d/xez-b96708a2.md`, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **the issue's own diagnosis was exact and the reproduction matched it.** At base `02fe3a6d` the `listProjects` gate reads its rows before parking (`automations-gate.test.ts` first mock) while the `getRepoInfo` gate parked first and awaited `original.getRepoInfo(...args)` — a `git` subprocess — after the resume, so the comment "everything between the resume and `automationProjects.set()` is microtasks" was false for that gate. With the second `server.ts` guard removed on a scratch copy plus a 300 ms slow-git stand-in, the pre-fix test passed 1/1 (the bug) and the fixed test failed 1/1 at the final `expect(automationHandle()).toBeUndefined()`.
- Observed (the brief's probe wording needs one correction): the brief says to inject the 300 ms delay "after `gitGate.resumed += 1`". Taken literally in the FIXED shape that leaves the case GREEN, because a delay behind the resume is not a slow git at all — it is just more of the same window the test already declines to wait for. Measured: fixed shape + delay literally after `resumed += 1` + guard removed → 1 passed. The delay only means "slow git" when it is on the real `original.getRepoInfo` call, which in the pre-fix shape happens to sit immediately after `resumed += 1` and in the fixed shape sits before the park. With the delay on the real call the probe behaves as the issue describes: pre-fix GREEN, fixed RED, and the fixed shape with the guard present still GREEN. Worth recording because a future run copying the literal wording would produce a probe that proves nothing.
- Observed: **a removed guard was caught by the named-break script on both automations cases under the delay.** `node packages/xezar/test/red-proofs.mjs automations-refresh-resurrects-removed-project` reported RED [0 passed / 1 failed] and `…-in-the-map` reported RED [0 passed / 1 failed] with the 300 ms slow-git stand-in in the test. The `-in-the-map` case is the one whose test is this file's second-window case; the first case's test is the first-window case, which was never racy — it went red for the same reason it always did.
- Regression/control: the focused suite is green three consecutive runs (`Tests 31 passed (31)` each, exit 0) and `npm run typecheck` is clean. The control that the probe does not simply break the test is the fixed shape with the guard present under the same 300 ms delay: 1 passed. Scratch copies of the pre-fix test (`git show HEAD:…`) and of `server.ts` were made with `cp` under the run tmp dir and every one was restored by `cp`; `git diff` shows `packages/xezar/src/server/server.ts` unchanged and only the test file modified, with no `git stash` used.
- Remaining limit: fixture-tested only. The race is timing-dependent by nature — the probe models a slow `git` with a fixed 300 ms delay, not a real loaded machine, and the reviewer's original 5/5-green-on-an-idle-machine observation is reproduced rather than stress-tested. No product behaviour changed, so nothing about the fix itself is in question; what is proved is that the regression test now fails when the second guard is removed even while `git` is slow.

### 2026-09-20 — an inferred flake mechanism was wrong, and the measurement took twenty minutes

Issue #723 (F-06 of the #671 flake inventory) carried a careful INFERRED mechanism for the
`design-debt-b6.e2e.ts` AC-6 virtual case: `VirtualFiles` measures `startMargin` once, the review
thread above the diff keeps growing, the mapping goes stale and the spec's 40 viewport steps run
out before the last file's rows mount. The row said so itself — "Not proven" — and asked for a red
proof. The measurement says the mechanism is something else entirely.

A probe spec (a scratch copy of the real one, `cp`, never a stash) logged the scroller's geometry
and virtua's mounted card set at every one of the 40 steps. `big.txt` is the FIRST file of that
diff, and `content-visibility` keeps every row of a mounted card in the DOM, so line 420 is present
the moment the card mounts — near the TOP of the list — and is gone from step 13 onward, when the
scroller reaches the bottom and virtua has unmounted it. The loop only ever moves DOWN. So the case
was decided entirely by its FIRST check, before any scrolling: if the thread's own scroll-restore
had already left the scroller past that first card, all forty steps could only make it worse. That
is a one-way ratchet, not a step count that ran out, and no bigger step count would have helped.

The red proof is then deterministic rather than statistical: force the scroller to the bottom until
the card unmounts, and from that one state the old body returns `false` after all 40 steps — the
reported `expected false to be true` — while the rebuilt body returns `true`. `startMargin` was
never stale in any sample: the virtualizer's container top stayed at 1044 px and the first rendered
slot tracked it throughout.

Three things worth keeping:

- **An inventory row's mechanism is a hypothesis with a run-id list attached.** The run ids were
  right and useful; the mechanism was not. The row was honest about which was which, and that
  honesty is what made it cheap to check — twenty minutes of probing against days of arguing.
- **"Scroll N times and then assert" cannot be repaired by raising N** when the thing asserted is
  unmounted by scrolling. The rebuild had to change what the wait waits FOR: content height still
  for ten animation frames, then the scroller parked at the virtual list's own measured start, then
  the card's own arrival.
- **`npm run test:e2e -- <spec>` does not narrow the browser suite to one spec.** `scripts/e2e.sh`
  forwards its arguments to `test-env-up.sh`, which rejects anything that is not `--force` /
  `--force-rebuild`, and then runs the whole suite anyway. Narrowing is
  `sh scripts/test-env-up.sh` once, then
  `npm test -- --config packages/web/e2e/vitest.config.ts <filter>`. Worth a line in the briefs.

### 2026-09-20 — the declared REFRESH phase shape, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi — real-task observed

- Observed (the fix): **a readiness rule that required a content claim from every round had no shape for a round that makes none.** Issue #670's merge-only refresh round failed `phase.criteria` because CRITERIA demanded a criterion ID and an `accepted-by:` line it could not honestly write. The repair is the smallest additive shape: a `REFRESH` record naming the refresh, the merged base sha and the evidence it refreshed, accepted in place of the content claim only while it carries none of its own. An absent `REFRESH` behaves exactly as before, which is what makes the change safe to land ahead of the two sibling treadmill fixes (#670's "Not in scope").
- Observed (a constraint the acceptance imposed on the design, not the code): the brief required **both refusal fixtures to pass against the pre-fix check as well as the fixed one**, which rules out a new `phase.refresh` predicate for the anti-bypass refusal — a `REFRESH` record that carries a content claim refuses pre-fix as `phase.criteria` (the claim-free CRITERIA is what fails) and post-fix as `phase.criteria` again, now naming the REFRESH record. Keeping the predicate on the accepted-criteria INPUT is what lets the guard be a genuine both-ways control rather than a new assertion, and the shape-integrity refusal follows the same predicate for the same reason.
- Observed (red proof, kit friction): `infra-tests.sh` copies its own `$SCRIPT_DIR` into every fixture, so there is no supported way to point the suite at a scratch copy of one check. The pre-fix proof was run by `cp`-ing the fixed check aside, installing the `git show HEAD:` copy, running the suite under an `EXIT` trap that restores the fixed file, and diffing afterwards. That worked and left the tree byte-identical, but a `--checks-dir` or per-check override on the suite would make the red proof a first-class operation instead of a careful in-place swap.
- Regression/control: pre-fix the new positive fixture fails on the real readiness path with `[phase.criteria] the CRITERIA record names no acceptance criterion` (671 passed, 1 failed); post-fix all four REFRESH fixtures pass and the suite is green (672 passed, 0 failed). The two required refusal directions (`refresh-is-a-bypass`, `no-refresh-no-claim`) are green in both runs, so the fixture that catches the bug is distinguished from the guards that only pin the behaviour that must not change.
- Remaining limit: the issue's run-store facts (`8f5ff4b5` failing with "check readiness failed") were not re-derived here — the run store was not read — so the reproduction is of the refusal mechanism, fixture-tested end to end, not of that exact run.

- Input: the immutable `## Code review (re-check)` comment from run `11063d9b`, reviewed head `f3d9e669`, and the leader's explicit disposition of one Major and one Minor. The Major identified an open-ended `OperationAnswer` classifier that let a future answer become a row-free replay; the Minor identified contradictory thrown-effect wording.
- Observed: an exhaustive status/error classification with `never` defaults turns the reviewer's synthetic new receipt answer into a compile-time failure. The existing real-door refusal and genuine-replay breaks remain the runtime guards for the two current branches; the MCP coverage floor remains the broader boundary check.
- Delivery shape: this task's own branch was reset to the immutable reviewed head, the fix was committed through the bound Git helper, and that exact commit was pushed to the original PR branch. This keeps the workflow's later gates on the same tree as the PR delivery; the cross-branch push remains outside `worktree-git.sh`, whose fixed refspec can name only this run's branch.
- Gate return: the canonical run exposed a deterministic macOS false signal in an unrelated watcher-isolation test: files created before `watch()` produced FSEvents registration backlog after the test's fixed settle delay. Proving both watchers live through observed deliveries before measuring isolation removed the timing assumption while preserving the cross-project assertion.

This replacement carried that counter forward and independently reproduced both required red proofs on scratch source before reusing the predecessor's inspected implementation.

The workflow's later gates still run on the unchanged task tree, so the PR's CI at the delivered head is the gate the re-review must read.
