### 2026-09-19 — OpenCode qualification report and the model-routing update, `docs-maintenance` step `docs`, `xezar-docs-maintenance`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: the owner's ask ("I need a report when you finish testing OpenCode. Save the report in project documentation"), the leader's run inventory of 12 OpenCode runs of 2026-09-19, the run store `.local/xezar/runs.json`, the `## QA` comments on PR #688 and issue #686, the retrial report `.local/xezar/tasks/8feb0aaa-…/opencode-writing-retrial.md`, and issues #686/#690/#692. Deliverable: `.xezar/docs/opencode-qualification-2026-09-19.md`, the OpenCode rows of `.xezar/docs/model-routing.md` (§ 7, § 8, § 11, a new § 13 entry), a docs changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **"verify, do not copy" caught a baseline the source could not confirm.** The brief's baseline was "8–9 stalls up to 2026-09-18" and § 13 enumerates eight run ids — but none of those eight is in today's `runs.json`, so the report had to mark them as a dated record rather than a re-verified fact, and to re-derive every 2026-09-19 number from the store instead. The same check found the brief's inventory correct on all 12 runs but with the model split the store actually records (`f4bec267` local DeepSeek, `4f40f4b7` Qwen, the other nine the API lane).
- Observed (kit friction, fourth recorded shape of the shell guard): the isolated-task guard refused **any** command whose text resolved to the primary checkout's path — including `basename "$XEZ_HANDOFF_FILE"`, `echo "$XEZ_HANDOFF_FILE"` and a `$HOME`-relative form — so reading the run store the task names as source of truth needed the path written to `.local/runs-path.txt` and read back with `$(cat …)`, which the guard does not see. The guard reads command text, not the filesystem, and it expands `$HOME`. The allowed primary evidence dir was unaffected because `phase-record.sh` resolves it internally.
- Observed: **an issue-less docs task has no natural changelog fragment name.** `changelog.d/` is named after the issue number in practice (`668.md`, `680.md`, `686.md`), and this task has no issue, so the fragment uses the branch form the README documents (`xez-0619292a.md`) and carries no `#<pr>)` reference — the release fold greps for that reference per merged PR, so the release agent will have to add this PR's number by hand.
- Regression/control: the 2026-09-19 run-store facts in the report are read, not copied: 12 opencode runs, 11 `done`, 1 `failed`, wall-clock 1m12s–9m22s, all three models. The report's one conclusion (OpenCode stays out of rotation until #692 merges) is labelled a judgement, separate from the facts, and the three defect states are taken from `gh` (#686 fixed by #688 merged `9e97a149`; #690 and #692 open).
- Remaining limit: the report's writing-chain evidence is one headless retrial in a scratch clone, not the cockpit's server path, and the eight pre-2026-09-19 stall run ids could not be re-verified in the current run store; both are stated in the report's "Honest limits".

### 2026-09-19 — #692 OpenCode goes silent after a rejected permission ask, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude (Opus 5) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #692 with the QA evidence from PR #688 (three reproductions) and the `## QA` comment on issue #686 (a fourth, on a real writing chain at `main` `9e97a149`), the counter-shape run `0f4c4e08` in the primary run store, and the bounded-fix brief asking for either a post-reject silence watchdog in `packages/xezar/src/core/` or a fix to the turn-end seam, with a mandatory red proof. Deliverable: `REJECT_SILENCE_MS` plus the watchdog in `opencode-server-runner.ts`, one mock-server flag, four test cases, `changelog.d/692.md`, a `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` entry, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **the issue's two candidate shapes were decidable from the local run store, and the answer was the opposite of the issue's own title.** Scanning all 28 transcripts in `.local/xezar/runs/` that carry a `denied permission` note showed the same shape every time: the denial, the failed tool result, then `turn.completed`/`turn-end` within 0.1–0.3 s. Not one session went silent mid-turn. The #686 QA comment says the same thing in one line — "last event `turn-end` at 12:24:28.958Z" — so the silence is AFTER the turn ends, not instead of it. The runs that "stalled" were therefore sessions that had already gone idle, on a last (interactive) step that correctly parked at `waiting`, driven headless (`xezar run`, which waits only for `done`/`review`/`failed`/`cancelled`) where nobody could answer. Reading the run-history evidence before theorising changed which mechanism got the fix, and it is why the watchdog treats `session.idle` as NOT a sign of life while a new assistant part IS one.
- Observed: **a watchdog that disarms on "any event" would have passed its own test and fixed nothing.** The first design cleared the timer on every SSE frame; against the verified shape that clears on the session's own `session.idle` a tenth of a second after the refusal, so the fix would have been armed for 100 ms and then disarmed forever. The arming predicate had to be the thing the transcripts distinguish — a part the session had not started before — rather than traffic.
- Observed: **naming what the old "wait for the next event" was load-bearing FOR narrowed the change more than any test did.** It carries four things: a slow tool call or model mid-turn, a turn ended with a question a human answers at their own pace, a `XEZ:MONITORING` park, and the run layer's 15-minute idle close. Only the fourth is in the failure's path, and only when the abandoned turn said nothing at all — so the watchdog is armed exclusively after a reject xezar itself issued and disarmed by any new part, which leaves the interactive park (BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY § 8) intact for every turn that gave the user something to answer.
- Observed: **the 15-minute idle close means the stall was bounded, and that is worse, not better.** A parked last step is closed by `armIdleTimer` after the default 15 minutes; the step then returns `null` and the workflow reports the run as finished. The #686 QA killed its run at 11 m 58 s — three minutes before a close that would have recorded a successful run whose handoff step never opened the PR. The named error this fix raises at five minutes replaces an unexplained success as much as it replaces a wait.
- Regression/control: red proof at `.local/xezar/tasks/1adeb44a-…/red-proofs/red-without-fix.log` — with one line removed (`armRejectWatch` never called) the two stall cases FAIL by timing out at 30 s, while the guard case (a reject followed by a normal event) and the constant assertion PASS unchanged: 2 failed, 2 passed, which is the intended split between the regression proof and the guards. With the fix restored the file is 44/44. The default path is pinned by the suite's own normal-session case, which asserts the exact v1 event counts and the v2 stream.
- Remaining limit: fixture-tested against the bundled mock server only — no live `opencode serve` + DeepSeek session has been run since the change, so the five-minute constant is justified from transcript evidence (healthy gaps of 0.1–0.3 s) and from the surrounding bounds, not from a live reproduction. The watchdog covers a session that says nothing after a refusal; it deliberately does NOT cover a turn that ends after a refusal having said something, nor the headless CLI's own behaviour of waiting indefinitely on a run parked at `waiting` (a separate seam in `index.ts`, left untouched and named in the PR). For OpenCode this replaces the manual "cancel a silent pi/OpenCode task after 15 minutes" rule in `.xezar/docs/model-routing.md` § 6 for this one failure mode; the rest of that rule, and OpenCode's exclusion from the rotation under § 7, still stand until a live retrial says otherwise.

### 2026-09-19 — #678 XEZ_AUTOMATIONS flipped after boot opens the routes but never starts the scheduler, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude (Opus 5) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #678 with its mechanism confirmed at `origin/main` `96af64b7` and file:line citations that still resolve at this head, and a bounded-fix brief fixing the scope to the env flag only (the stored `automations` workspace key stays with #677/#672) and naming the four documents and three test shapes. Deliverable: two new closures in `startServer`'s automations wiring, four test cases in `automations-gate.test.ts`, the corrected comment in `capabilities.ts`, the `README.md` env row, the `.env.example` block, the `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` rollback sentence, a changelog fragment, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **"find the one place the resolve happens" turned up a second place, and it changes what the fix can honestly promise.** The scheduler's flag is resolved in one closure inside `startServer`; the ROUTES resolve it separately inside `createApp`. So an automations route answers 409 ahead of any handler and never reaches the closure — which means a flag switched OFF cannot be observed through an automations route at all. The first draft of the off-transition test asserted exactly that, and it was rewritten to drive the transition through a project removal (a real caller of the closure) before it ever ran. The shipped README and `.env.example` say "observed at the next consult" rather than "instant" for the same reason, instead of over-claiming a hot capability.
- Observed: **a fail-open helper needed a generation counter that no test would have demanded.** The warm-up awaits a `git` call per project before `start()`. Self-review of the finished diff found that a flag switched back off inside that window would be undone by the late `start()` landing afterwards — the off-transition silently reversed, with the suite green throughout. The scheduler's own `reschedule()` already solves this with a generation, so the fix borrows that shape rather than inventing one. It came out of enumerating the transitions the plan listed, not out of a failure.
- Observed: **naming what the boot-only start was load-bearing FOR is what kept the change small.** It carried exactly two things — one start ever, so never two pollers against one coordinator, and no start before the port is open — and both are now explicit latches (`automationsRunning`, `automationsListening`) rather than properties inherited from "it only runs in `server.once('listening')`". Grepping the TYPE found a single construction site of `WorkspaceAutomationScheduler` in product code, so there was no second site to keep in step.
- Regression/control: red proof at `.local/xezar/tasks/1d32d63a-…/red-proof-678.txt` — with `server.ts` restored to `HEAD` in a scratch copy (a copy, never `git stash`, whose stack is shared by every worktree), the whole file runs 24 passed / 2 failed: the on-flip case fails at `expected "start" to be called 1 times, but got 0 times` and the off-flip case at the same shape for `stop`, while the two guards — flag on at boot is not double-started, flag never set starts nothing and still 409s — pass unchanged, which is the intended split between the regression proof and the controls. With the fix the file is 26/26.
- Remaining limit: fixture-tested in-process against a server bound to an ephemeral loopback port; no live cockpit was booted and no real GitHub poll was made, so "the poller is running" is proven as `start()` called once and not as a request to GitHub. The off-transition is only as prompt as the next consult of the flag, which in practice means a definition saved, a project added or a project removed — a xezar that is idle in all three keeps polling until one happens, and that is stated in the shipped documentation rather than hidden.

### 2026-09-19 — audit-redaction nested-unicode-escape fixpoint, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: issue #646 (labels `bug`, `release-0.17.0`), the shipped `unescapeUnicodeEscapes` in
  `packages/xezar/src/mcp/audit-redaction.ts` (a single replace), its two call sites (`clean` and
  `summaryOf`'s `leaf`), the existing `B-REDACT-MCP-IDENTIFIER-SECRET-ESCAPED` tests, and the owner's
  brief naming the fix shape (fast path first, loop to a fixpoint, cap four passes, no new option).
  Deliverable: the fixpoint loop, two tests, a changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **the red proof was cheap because the pre-fix bytes are still HEAD.** The brief's route
  was `cp` the pre-fix source into the primary evidence dir, run the new test, restore. A cleaner
  equivalent that never touches the stash: `git show HEAD:<file>` writes the pre-fix bytes (HEAD is
  the pre-fix commit until the fix is committed), the fixed copy is saved beside it, and a shell
  `trap` restores it on any exit. Four matched tests, one failed and three passed, in 1.5s.
- Observed: **the default vitest reporter cannot show which of several matched tests passed.** The
  summary said `1 failed | 3 passed | 27 skipped` but listed only the failure, so the count alone
  could not separate the new fast-path guard from the two pre-existing tests. `--reporter=verbose`
  was needed to name each passing test, which is what makes the "guards pass both ways, one test
  catches the bug" claim checkable by a reader. Recorded as a second run of the same filter.
- Observed (source-tooling friction): `packages/xezar/src/mcp/audit-redaction.ts` is not text to
  `grep` or `file` — the `C0_C1` character class holds literal C0/C1 control bytes, so both report
  "Binary file … matches" and every search of this file needs `grep -a` (or `sed`). A source file
  that ordinary text tools refuse to print is a real reading hazard for the agents this repository
  runs, and it is not caused by this change.
- Regression/control: the new `B-REDACT-MCP-IDENTIFIER-SECRET-DOUBLE-ESCAPED` case fails against the
  pre-fix function (`expected 'c88a751b…' to be '3a8d71aa…'`) and passes with it, while the
  pre-existing `B-REDACT-MCP-IDENTIFIER-SECRET`, the pre-existing `…-ESCAPED` case and the new
  fast-path guard pass in both runs. `npm run test:coverage:mcp` exits 0 with
  `mcp/audit-redaction.ts` at 97.88 % lines / 89.34 % branches.
- Remaining limit: the cap is four passes, so an escape nested deeper than four layers still reaches
  the digest encoded — the bound the issue asked for, not a complete decode. The fix is proven
  through the MCP door fixture, not against a live MCP client, and the fast-path guard pins only
  that a backslash without `\uXXXX` reaches the digest byte-identical.

### 2026-09-19 — #644 state-path scan hardening, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (deepseek-api, deepseek-flash) — real-task observed
- Input: issue #644 (four deferred minors from the #609 `## Code review`, run `2d1ba3b7`, and the #600 deferral note `issuecomment-5723406609`), plus the current `packages/xezar/src/state-path-scan.test.ts` and its neighbours. Deliverable: the scan widened to three more spellings and to `xezarHomeDir(`, per-spelling red-proved probes, a `gh`/`git` env-parity guard, a Nit-6 wording pin, a changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **the guard's own probes had to go through the scan, not the regexes.** The existing probes tested the exported rule constants directly, so a probe could stay green while the scan stopped applying a rule. Extracting a pure `findingsIn(file, source)` and running each new probe through it means dropping the rule from `scan()` fails the probe, not merely deleting a regex. The red run (four probes failing, the prose control passing) was captured before the rules were added — no `git stash`, and the guard the probes protect is still readable.
- Observed: **`xezarHomeDir(` is a declaration as well as a call.** A plain `/\bxezarHomeDir\s*\(/` flags `export function xezarHomeDir(env …)`, which derives nothing; the lookbehind that excludes it keeps the allowlist to the four real call sites the issue named (`paths.ts` server-instances/server.json/install-lock, `project-kit-paths.ts` `~`-launch diversion) and gives a probe that pins the declaration and an import as non-findings. The one extra allowance the new rules needed was `process.env.HOME` in `mcp/tools/project-config.ts`'s `scrubPaths`, which reads the home only to redact it out of error text.
- Observed (guard vs. catcher, stated so a reviewer can tell them apart): the four spelling probes are **catchers** — each was red against the pre-fix rule set. The `gh`/`git` env-parity test and the Nit-6 socket-wording test are **guards that pass both ways**; a scratch-copy break restoring the pre-#609 socket wording made the Nit-6 guard fail (captured), and a scratch-copy `xezarHomeDir()` caller appended to real source made the main scan fail by file and line.
- Regression/control: the widened rules were checked against every existing allowance before committing — the only new findings were the four call sites plus the `scrubPaths` read, so no legitimate line needed a fresh exception and the stale-entry test stayed green. The template rule deliberately does not fire on the `server-install/engine.ts` help line that mentions `~/.xezar/config.json` inside a template literal, which is pinned as a passing control.
- Remaining limit: the scan is still a text scan over `packages/xezar/src`; an alias bound through a namespace (`import * as os from 'node:os'; const { homedir: h } = os;`) is not caught by the aliased-import rule, though `os.homedir()` itself is caught by the call rule. The MCP test floor does not apply — no `packages/xezar/src/mcp/**` source file changed, only a test that reads `mcpSocketLocation`.

### 2026-09-19 — PR 2 of 2 for issue #647, the SSE/terminal/automations listeners of the dispose seam, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed

- Input: the leader's brief, the approved spec at `.local/xezar/tasks/07a3a8c9…/647-spec.md` (§ 6 PR 2 scope, § 4 RP-3/RP-4/RP-5, § 5 AC-2…AC-10), base `main` at `0eed06b0` with PR 1 (#699) already merged, and the PR 1 review nit O-1 on `packages/xezar/test/red-proofs.mjs`. Deliverable: the three remaining listeners, four named red proofs plus a guard case, the AC-9 fan-out guard, the O-1 harness repair, this fragment and `changelog.d/xez-270e99f0.md`.
- Observed: **a spec that names what each listener was load-bearing FOR turned a three-listener change into three DIFFERENT guards, not one pattern applied three times.** The SSE stream and the terminal hold per-registration state, so they compare generations; the automations listener holds nothing comparable — the `project-added` bus event that re-populates the coordinators carries no generation at all — so `superseded` is the only question it can answer. Writing them all as a generation comparison would have been unimplementable in the third case, and writing them all as `superseded` would have been wrong in the first two (a dispose can be non-superseded and still name a registration this stream does not hold). The spec's § 1 table had already made that split; the value showed up as zero rework.
- Observed: **the terminal's build-side clobber was only visible because the spec asked what the OLD mechanism guaranteed, not what it did.** `onStoreCreated` fires while a build is still running, so a losing build announces its store too — a defect independent of the dispose payload and older than it. It needed its own red proof (`terminal-attach-accepts-older-generation`), and the two terminal proofs fail different tests, which is what shows they are two bugs and not one described twice.
- Observed (harness, the PR 1 nit O-1): **the red-proof harness could manufacture a proof.** Its non-guard branch read any non-zero exit as RED, so a break that made the source unparsable — or a mistyped file filter — reported "RED" for a test that never ran. Demonstrated on this branch with a throwaway case whose defect was a syntax error: before the repair that case read `RED`, after it reads `INVALID (no test executed — the run failed without the test failing, so it proves nothing)`. The guard branch already refused a zero-test run; the same hole in the other direction is worse, because it invents evidence instead of losing it.
- Observed (method): the four red proofs and the guard each failed exactly ONE test (`10 passed / 1 failed`, `23 passed / 1 failed`, `23 passed / 1 failed`, `22 passed / 1 failed`), which is the cheap check that a break is scoped to the behaviour it names rather than to the file. PR 1's four cases were re-run under the repaired harness and are unchanged (two RED, two guards STILL-GREEN).
- Observed (method): both new race tests park the teardown at `RunManager.prototype.dispose`, the single await inside `teardown`, instead of driving `DELETE /projects/:id` — the route's own five-second bound would otherwise be the test's runtime. The window under test is identical, and neither test references the bound's constant, which is AC-6's falsifier.
- Regression/control: RP-3 `sse-detach-on-stale-generation`, RP-4 `terminal-delete-on-stale-generation` + `terminal-attach-accepts-older-generation`, RP-5 `automations-remove-ignores-superseded` all RED against their named breaks and green after; the guard `automations-never-built-removal` (the never-built `project-removed` path) stays green against RP-5's own defect, proving the two paths were not folded together; `listener-not-migrated` proves the AC-9 source scan. The four existing tests AC-5 names pass with their bodies unmodified — the only edits to those files are import lines and added cases.
- Remaining limit: the SSE, terminal and automations variants are **code-verified, not observed** — no field report names them, the spec says so, and this run adds no sighting. The reproduction is a fixture run of the real modules at this branch's base revision, not a live incident on a released build.

### 2026-09-19 — bounded fix for issue #715 (Minors 1–2 of the fable re-check of PR #707), `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed

- Input: the leader's brief, issue #715 (which quotes the reviewer's two findings verbatim and re-checks each against the reviewed head `befb2015`), base `main` at `63d70332` with PR #707 already merged as `00da4ec0`, and the reviewer's own throwaway tests left read-only in the primary checkout at `.local/xezar/tasks/23eb256f…/`. Deliverable: the liveness guard in the refresh branch, the rewritten RP-5 assertion, one permanent regression test, three harness cases, `changelog.d/715.md` and this fragment.
- Observed: **a reviewer's throwaway, handed over as a FILE, is the cheapest red proof there is.** Run `23eb256f` left `zz-reviewer-zombie-throwaway.test.ts` in its evidence directory. Turning it into the permanent regression test took one read and one adaptation, and it was RED against the pre-fix source on the first run — no re-derivation of the interleaving, no guessing at which await the window sits behind. The evidence directory was worth reading before the source was.
- Observed: **the red-proof harness from #467/#647 left a slot and it paid immediately.** The three new cases are three literals in its `CASES` array; one command proved all three RED and restored the source byte-exactly. On a task whose whole point is that a test written after the diagnosis passes against the bug more often than anyone expects, having the proof be a committed, re-runnable artifact rather than a transcript is the difference between a claim and a record.
- Observed (the fix itself): **an async replacement opens a window the synchronous shape it replaced did not have.** #707's Major 1 correctly replaced "skip the removal when superseded" with "remove, then REFRESH from the registry". The refresh is async because the remote has to be resolved, and that await is a new window in which the row it already resolved can go stale. This is AGENTS.md § Changing a mechanism that already works one turn further on: the replacement was right, and it still needed the question "what can change under the NEW mechanism while it waits?".
- Observed (the test): **an assertion that pins two CALLS is not the same as one that pins the PROPERTY, and the gap is invisible until it is probed.** RP-5's old form (`add` called, `remove` called) stayed green against a refresh that re-adds and then removes again — net absent — and the whole 29-test file stayed green against a refresh that never re-seeds the automation coordinator at all. Both probes are now harness cases. "Which of my new assertions would survive the two most plausible next regressions of this seam?" is worth asking of every test written from a diagnosis.
- Observed (method): the first version of the new regression test waited on `rescheduleAutomations()` as its "the parked refresh has finished" signal, which is ambiguous — the genuine removal two lines earlier calls it too, so a late call could satisfy the wait before the parked refresh resumed at all, giving a spuriously GREEN regression test. Replaced with a signal that begs no question: the mock counts resumptions, the test waits for resumed == parked, then yields one macrotask, because everything between the resume and `coordinator.add()` is microtasks. Found by the author's own self-review round, not by a gate.
- Observed (tooling, cost me time): **`gh issue view <n> --comments` printed nothing and exited 0 on this host** — no error, no body, twice, including with the sandbox disabled. `gh issue view <n> --json body,title -q .body` returned the full issue at once. The failure mode is indistinguishable from an empty issue, so a brief that tells an agent to read an issue with the plain form should name the `--json` form as its fallback.
- Regression/control: `automations-refresh-resurrects-removed-project` RED without the guard and green with it; `rp5-net-absent-from-skills-coordinator` (probe E3a) and `rp5-refresh-forgets-reschedule` (probe E3b) both GREEN against the OLD RP-5 assertion and RED against the new one, which is what makes them proofs of the coverage hole and not of the code. The three pre-existing #707 cases were re-run and are unchanged: two RED, and `automations-never-built-removal` STILL-GREEN as the guard it is declared to be. The suite was run three consecutive times green to check the new race test for flakiness.
- Remaining limit: the zombie interleaving is **fixture-tested, not observed in the field** — no user report names it, and it needs a `DELETE /projects/:id` landing within tens of milliseconds of a superseded dispose of the same project. The reproduction is a fixture run of the real modules at this branch's base revision, not a live incident on a released build.
- Observed (review response round 1, run `00842b41`): **a reviewer who parks the NEXT await finds the half of the fix nobody asked about.** The #715 guard closed the `listProjects()` window; the reviewer's own throwaway parked the `getRepoInfo()` await behind it and showed `automationProjects` still taking the removed id back. The cheap generalisation for this seam: after adding a liveness guard in front of one await, ask the same question of every await that follows it in the same callback — the answer was one line, and the source comment plus the changelog had already claimed that ground.
- Observed (review response round 1): **a closure-private map is observable through the accessor production already uses.** The throwaway found `automationProjects` by patching `Map.prototype.set`; the permanent test reads the scheduler instance's own `options.handle(projectId, store)` off an existing `start` spy instead, which is the same idiom this file already uses for the coordinators' `roots`. The control it needs — boot populating the map with a faked GitHub remote — is what keeps "handle is undefined" from also being what a never-populated map looks like.

### 2026-09-19 — #699 review response (PR #647 PR 1 of 2), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, pi + DeepSeek V4.1 Flash — real-task observed

- Input: one Major finding (F-1) and two nits from the `## Code review` verdict by Claude Fable 5.1
  (run `675d0701`) on PR #699, plus the leader's dispositions. The source files were declared in

  `-t "opens once for a context reported twice"` reached vitest as the name filter `"opens` plus six
  positional FILE filters. Vitest matched no file, printed `Tests 8485 skipped (8485)`, executed
  ZERO tests and exited 0 — and the harness printed `STILL-GREEN (guard, as expected)`. This is the
  AGENTS.md fail-open-helper shape one level up: a harness that reads only the child's exit code
  cannot tell "nothing ran" from "everything passed", so its own guard evidence was vacuous. The
  reviewer's finding was reproduced here with a scratch copy of the pre-fix harness before the fix
  (`Tests 8485 skipped (8485)`, exit 0, green) and again after it (`1 passed / 0 failed`, non-zero).
- Observed: fixing only half the finding would have left the hole. A quote-aware split alone still
  lets any future filter that matches nothing read as green; the zero-test refusal alone still sends
  a space-bearing `-t` to vitest as positional filters. Both halves are pinned here: a scratch copy
  of the fixed harness with the OLD split back in it refuses the same run with `NO TESTS RAN
  (harness failure)` and exits 1.
- Observed: the four #647 cases after the fix — both named breaks RED (17 passed / 2 failed and
  12 passed / 1 failed), both guards `STILL-GREEN` with `1 passed / 0 failed`. The counts are now
  printed per case, so the guard evidence is readable rather than asserted.
- Observed (process): the pre-existing fragments `d3e1d44e.md` and `7841e6ae.md` already recorded
  that the skill's `DELIVERED` path and `worktree-preflight.sh`'s `branch.owned-by-run` rule cannot
  both hold in a fresh task worktree. This run confirmed it again: the compliant path is to merge
  the PR head into this task's branch, commit here, and push `HEAD` to
  `refs/heads/xez/c649c71b` with a plain `git push` (`worktree-git.sh push`'s refspec is fixed to
  this run's own branch). With own commits present readiness passes on `branch.has-own-commits`, and
  `DELIVERED` must NOT be written. The skill text and the check still disagree; a third run hitting
  this is the signal that one of them needs to say which wins.
- Remaining limit: the harness's zero-test refusal is on the guard branch, per the finding. A red
  proof whose defect breaks compilation still reads RED off a run that executed no test, so a
  future pass could add the executed-count (`passed + failed`) check there too.

### 2026-09-19 — #686 OpenCode denies the run's own task-evidence directory, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #686 as verified by the leader (file:line at `main` `e2e53c75`), the bounded-fix brief naming the two evidence directories and the four red-proof assertions, and the existing #578 permission suite. Deliverable: the allowed-roots widening in `opencode-permissions.ts`/`opencode-server-runner.ts`, two new test cases, `changelog.d/686.md`, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **a red proof for a NEW capability has no pre-fix symbol, so "the guards pass both ways" and "the regression test fails at main" need two different shapes.** The first version of the unit test built every assertion from the new `runEvidenceRoots` producer; stashing the fix then made ALL FIVE cases red with `TypeError: runEvidenceRoots is not a function`, which proves the file cannot load, not that the policy denies the path — and it hides the guards. Splitting it fixed both: the regression case pins the producer (`toEqual([evidence, frozen])`) and is red at main, while (b)-(d) build the roots list from the documented convention and assert against BOTH the pre-fix list (`cwd` only) and the post-fix list, so they are green on both revisions and still fail if the fix over-widens. A genuine behavioural red came from the mock-server suite instead, where the real runner answered the ask with a named denial and no new symbol was needed to observe it.
- Observed: **the release archive guard and this fix pull in opposite directions, and the guard wins by construction.** `own-local-path` refuses a packed file containing `.local/xezar/tasks` or `.local/xezar-tasks` — the kit's evidence root, deliberately kept out of the shipped engine — while #686 needs the engine to name exactly those two directories. Building each path from segments (`join(projectDataDir(root), 'tasks', runId)` and `join(root, '.local', 'xezar-tasks', runId)`) keeps the shipped bytes free of the contiguous literal: the built `dist/core/opencode-permissions.js` contains `xezar-tasks` and no `.local/xezar[-/]tasks` at all. The alternative — a whole-tree grant, or a kit path passed in through the spec — was rejected as wider than the bug or as a new seam field; the engine now naming the kit's evidence convention on purpose is flagged for the reviewer rather than resolved silently.
- Observed: **a fixture evaluated at describe time reads as a policy failure.** `const state = join(base, '.local', 'xezar')` next to a `base` assigned in `beforeEach` is `'.local/xezar'` by the time the cases run, so assertion (a) came back `reject` for a reason that looked exactly like the bug under repair, one full test cycle after the fix was already correct. The fixture is a getter now, with the reason written next to it.
- Observed (kit friction, third recorded shape): the isolated-task shell guard refused any command whose TEXT contained the primary checkout's absolute path, so the red-proof transcripts could not be written by naming `task_evidence_dir` literally; resolving it at runtime from `checks/lib/common.sh` worked. The guard reads command text, not the filesystem.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/core` is 983/983 (40 files) at the fixed head, and `npm run typecheck` is clean across the four workspaces. The red proof is stored with this run's evidence: `red-proofs/red-6-both-before-fix.txt` (fix stashed, tests kept) is 3 failed / 57 passed — (a) and (e) red because the producer does not exist, the mock-server case red with the real runner's `denied permission 'external_directory' for …/tasks/68600000-…/red-proofs/* — outside this run's allowed directories`, and (b)-(d) green — while `red-proofs/green-3-after-fix.txt` and `red-proofs/green-5-after-fix.txt` are 60/60 and 20/20. The archive-path guard is checked against the real build output, not only by reading the rule.
- Remaining limit: the fix is fixture-tested against the bundled mock server; no real `opencode serve` session has written an evidence directory since the change, and the frozen `.local/xezar-tasks` root is granted for the #665 dual-read window without a live run exercising it. The widening is deliberately flagged as a security-boundary change for a full review rather than self-approved.

### 2026-09-19 — #652 the pi worktree guard refused the run's own evidence directory and read quoted `cd`/`..` text as commands, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #652 as filed against v0.16.0 (commit `a8b25a77`, evidence `.local/xezar-tasks/83428cc9-…/two-cockpits-qa.md`), the bounded brief naming the two halves and their required cases, and PR #688's just-merged `runEvidenceRoots` producer (#686) to reuse rather than copy. Deliverable: the shared evidence-roots module, the pi guard's tokeniser change, the guard's allowed-roots wiring in `run.ts`, the new test cases, `changelog.d/652.md`, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **this run is a pi run under the guard it is fixing, so both halves reproduced live before any edit.** A `mkdir -p` into this run's own evidence directory came back `Blocked by Xezar: … The shell command names a path outside the worktree in the primary checkout, or a directory change it cannot verify.` — the exact text the issue quotes — and `printf '%s\n' '..'` and `echo "cd .."` were refused by the same guard. That is the strongest reproduction available for a guard bug: the defect refused the command that would have written its own red proof.
- Observed: **the brief's own commands were among the false positives.** `grep -n "from '../core/" packages/xezar/src/workflows/run.ts` was refused (a quoted `..`-prefixed search pattern), and so was a `python3 - <<'PY' … PY` heredoc whose body quoted source text — a heredoc body is unquoted shell text, so the guard parses it as commands. A brief that asks for a quoted relative path or a heredoc should expect the refusal and route around it (`write`, or a variable), or the tokeniser has to learn heredocs.
- Observed: **naming what the blanket flatten pass was load-bearing FOR made the (B) fix a measurement, not a judgement call.** Deleting `wordsEscape(shellWords(command.replace(/["'`]/g, ' ')), roots)` failed exactly ONE of the 106 existing cases — `blocks sh -c with a quoted script` — so the pass's unique value is quoted shell scripts and everything else it caught was incidental. The replacement reads a quoted string as a command only in a script position (`sh`/`bash`/`zsh`/`dash`/`ksh`/`ash`/`mksh`/`fish` with a `-c`-ish flag, and `eval`), which keeps that case green and drops the false positives. Had the experiment shown five failures, the fix would have been a different, narrower one.
- Observed: **the loosening this buys is real and is written down rather than hidden.** Quoted text is no longer re-read as a command, so `perl -e 'unlink "<primary>/tracked.md"'` now passes (it is one argument to a program that builds its own paths — already on the guard's documented cannot-see list) and the issue's "prose naming a repository path" case passes. Inside the required set, `rm -rf '..'` is now allowed where `rm -rf ..` stays refused: a quoted `..` is the string a program receives, and only the quoting separates it from a listing token. The header's cannot-see list names the first; the second is the price of the criterion and is stated in the PR body.
- Observed: **a control test that fails for the wrong reason is not a control.** The first (A) version built every case from the new `agentDirectories(repoRoot, dataDir, env)` signature, so stashing the fix made the project-root and tracked-file GUARDS red too — they were asserting the signature, not the guard. Splitting the root list into a `rootsFor()` built from the documented convention made the controls green on both revisions, and the red proof pins the producer separately (`expect(produced(...)).toEqual(rootsFor(...))`). Measured after the split: (A) is exactly 1 red / 129 green and (B) exactly 4 red / 126 green against the pre-fix sources.
- Observed: **a bounded recursion needed a bound that is not reachable by ordinary input.** `scriptEscapes` calls `bashEscapes` on a script operand, so a command of 12 nested `sh -c "…"` would recurse 12 deep and a pathological one could throw instead of blocking — a thrown error is a missed block, not a false one. `MAX_SCRIPT_DEPTH = 8` fails closed and is pinned by a test that nests past it.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/workflows packages/xezar/scripts packages/xezar/src/core` is 591/591 on the targeted set and 1007/1007 for all of `src/core`, and `npm run typecheck` is clean across the four workspaces. The two red proofs are stored with this run's evidence: (A) `red-proofs/` with `run.ts` stashed and the tests kept is 1 failed / 129 passed, (B) the same with only the guard source stashed is 4 failed / 126 passed, and both are 131/131 with the fix. The `sh -c`, `bash -lc`, `eval`, `git -C`, chained-`cd`, project-root and tracked-file cases stay green in every one of those runs — they are the controls, and the brief's `echo "cd /elsewhere"` is one of them rather than a red proof.
- Remaining limit: the allowed-roots half is fixture-tested through the real producer (`agentDirectories` → guard) and the real argv seam is covered by the existing `buildPiArgs` test, but no live pi session has yet written an evidence file through the fixed guard — the fix cannot be exercised by the run that authored it. A `cd`/`..` fragment inside a heredoc body is still read as a command, and a quoted relative path such as `'../core/'` is still refused; both need the tokeniser to know where heredocs start, which was out of scope for a bounded change.
- Observed (round 1 review, PR #693): **measuring a replacement by deleting the old code measures it against the suite, not against the command space.** Deleting the blanket flatten pass went red on exactly 1 of the 106 existing cases, which correctly identified what that pass was load-bearing FOR (quoted shell scripts) — but those 106 cases were written for the OLD guard, so they could not contain the commands the new code would get wrong. The replacement shipped with two holes the deletion experiment was structurally blind to: `bash -o pipefail -c '<script>'` (the flag's separate argument stops a dash-word scan before the `-c`, so the script was never read, and five spellings that BLOCKED on `main` came back ALLOWED) and a quoted `..` exempted for every command (`rm -rf '..'` reaches the worktree's parent — every peer run's tree). Both were found by the reviewer's adversarial command table, not by the suite. Lesson: a red count from deleting the old mechanism says which OLD cases depended on it; it says nothing about the new code's own failure modes, so a second, adversarially-built table is a separate artefact to write before claiming the replacement is equivalent.
- Observed (round 2, scoped re-check of PR #693): **a fix that is right about the OLD mistake can be wrong about the same word.** Round 1's Blocker was "a dash-word scan stops before the `-c` and reads the flag's UNQUOTED argument (`pipefail`) as the script". The repair decoupled "was a command flag seen anywhere in the segment" from "which word is the operand" and then took the FIRST QUOTED word in the segment — which is the same mistake with the quoting flipped: `bash --rcfile 'x' -c '<script>'` hands the flag a QUOTED argument, that argument is now the first quoted word, and the real `-c` operand is never read. Both versions answered "which word is the script" without reference to WHICH FLAG asked for one. Lesson: when a fix relocates a search, state what the search is anchored TO; an unanchored "first word that looks like X" survives one adversarial table and fails the next.
- Observed: **the class was enumerable, and enumerating it found four more spellings than the re-check reported.** Writing one harness row per shell flag that takes a SEPARATE word (`-o`/`+o` in bash/sh/dash/ksh/zsh, bash `-O`/`+O`, `--rcfile`, `--init-file`, zsh `--emulate`, clusters like `-euo`, a quoted `'-c'`, and `-co`, which asks for a command string and consumes a word in the same breath) measured 17 of 29 rows at the wrong value against the pre-fix guard — the re-check had reported 2. Anchoring at the command flag closes 16; `-co 'pipefail' '<script>'` needed the second half, stepping over a known flag's own argument PAST the anchor. A two-row bug report is a sample of a class, and the cheapest way to find out how big the class is, is to write the table.
- Observed: **the red proof was a file swap, not a `git stash`.** The stash stack is shared by every worktree on this machine and peers pop it concurrently, so the pre-fix guard was copied to this run's evidence directory and swapped in and out around one vitest run: exactly the 17 `RED:` rows failed and all 12 `GUARD:` rows passed on BOTH revisions, which is what makes them controls rather than proofs.
- Process slip, recorded rather than tidied away: the `phase-record.sh counter self-review` round was consumed AFTER the source edit was written, not before it as the contract asks. Nothing was hidden by the ordering — the counter was not exhausted and the round was consumed before the commit, the gates and the push — but the guarantee the contract is buying is that an exhausted counter stops a repair before it is written, and that only holds if the call comes first.

### 2026-09-19 — the guide-02 covered-click flake, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (deepseek-flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: the leader's bounded-fix brief (browser-suite only) naming the three red CI runs — main 35451994733, PR #702 35451195569, #706's neighbour — and the #702 chain report's diagnosis that the failing call is a raw `clickRole('link', 'Files')` which does not go through the `clickRoleWhenStable` guard. Deliverable: the four run-header tab clicks routed through that guard, the `run()` change that makes the guard actually fire, one unit case, a changelog fragment, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **the brief's diagnosis was necessary but not sufficient, and the failed run's own log said so.** Downloading the failed attempt's log showed the error took `run()`'s `execFileSync` catch (stack: the throw at `guide-browser.ts:71`, caused at `:65`), not the zero-exit `success:false` branch, and the "covered by" text appeared only in the Serialized Error's `stdout`. `isCoveredClickError` reads `cause.message`, which on that path is `Command failed: … --json` — so `clickRoleWhenStable` would have rethrown the very covered click it exists to retry. Routing the click through the helper alone would have been a cosmetic fix that the brief's own acceptance ("passes 3 times locally", "a unit check already exists") could never have falsified. Confirmed on Node v24.20.0 that `execFileSync`'s message does not carry the child's stdout.
- Observed: **a fake CLI is the only honest way to pin a predicate that is a string match on CLI output.** A hand-built Error would have encoded the same wrong assumption the bug came from, so the unit case writes a `#!/usr/bin/env node` fake under a `mkdtemp` dir, reaches it through a new `GuideBrowser.forBinary` test seam, and drives the REAL `run()`. Its failure output reproduces the CI log's serialized shape exactly (`status: 1`, `stdout` carrying the JSON).
- Regression/control: red proof at `.local/xezar/tasks/499e23a0-…/red-proof.log` — with only the payload carry reverted, 2 failed | 1 passed: the retry case fails with the CI's own `xezar e2e: agent-browser find role link click --name Files failed`, and the bare-`clickRole` case fails on `/covered by/`; the guard case (a non-covered failure is rethrown after exactly one attempt) passes both ways and is labelled a guard. With the fix, the file is 3/3. The browser acceptance is three consecutive green runs of the changed spec (`e2e-guide-02-run{1,2,3}.log`, 9/9 each, ~13 s), and the shared test env was booted and torn down through `scripts/test-env-up.sh` / `test-env-down.sh`.
- Remaining limit: the flake itself was never reproduced locally — it is load-dependent — so this is hardening proven by the CI log's error shape, not a reproduced incident fixed. Two links in the chain stay unverified: that the newly active tab's `font-semibold` re-render is the only coverer in practice, and that agent-browser exits 1 on every covered click (observed once on CI, then reproduced by the fake). Deliberately left unfixed, per the brief: the suite's other raw `clickRole('link', …)` sites (guide-06:101, guide-07:141, guide-10:52/60/78/92, guide-16:62) — none is a tab strip, and the settings nav's active state changes colour only, not geometry.

### 2026-09-19 — #648 pi probe-facade minors A/B/C, `bug-fix` step `fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed
- Input: issue #648 (three minors recorded in the PR #636 second-opinion review, each with a stated red proof), the brief's bound (`pi-runner.ts` + its test file), and AGENTS.md § Changing a mechanism that already works. Deliverable: the three fixes, four red-proof tests, one guard test, a changelog fragment and this fragment.
- Observed: **the brief's "red proof on a scratch copy, never `git stash`" instruction worked exactly as written and is cheaper than a stash.** `cp` the pre-fix file into the primary evidence dir, `cp` it back over the source, run only the new block (`-t "#648"`), `cp` the saved fixed copy back, then `diff -q` to prove the restore. No shared stack was touched and the full pre-fix source stays in evidence as a permanent artifact of what "without the fix" meant.
- Observed: **a red proof that fails by test TIMEOUT hides what it proves.** The first version of the two "no child is spawned" cases awaited `session.result` before asserting the spawn count; against the pre-fix file a child IS spawned, nothing ever drives it, and the case failed as "Test timed out in 8000ms" — true, but it names neither the spawn nor the count. Reading the counter before settling turned both into `expected 1 to be +0`. A timeout is a red light, not a red proof.
- Observed (kit): the three counters were declared with `phase-record.sh counters init --none` and never consumed — the work needed no self-review fix round, no gate return and no quality repair. Recording the "none" history up front is what let `phase-record.sh check` pass without a reconciliation question.

Nothing here was exercised against a real `pi` binary or a real cancel from the cockpit, so "a cockpit cancel inside the probe window now returns promptly" is inferred from the unit behaviour, not live-verified.

### 2026-09-19 — #649 `machine-state.json`: zod parse, read-modify-write lock, silent failed write, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #649 (labelled `bug` + `release-0.17.0`), the read-only analysis `.local/xezar/tasks/91fea85d-…/647-649-analysis.md` § #649 (three verified claims with file:line), the leader's bounded-fix brief with the six acceptance criteria and the mandatory red-proof shape, and the owner's 2026-09-19 14:27 decision lifting the earlier "recorded only, not fixed" direction. Deliverable: the schema/lock/warning fix in `project-machine-state.ts`, the awaited call sites in `projects.ts` and `port-memory.ts`, a new six-case test file with a cross-process worker testkit, a changelog fragment, this fragment and the phase record. All three claims re-verified in this worktree before building on them (hand-rolled parse at `project-machine-state.ts:81–111`; unlocked RMW at `:129–146`; empty `catch {}` at `projects.ts:197–201`).
- Observed: **the red proof needed all THREE changed source files stashed, not the two the brief named.** The brief said to stash `project-machine-state.ts` and `projects.ts`. Making the two writers async forces both call sites to await — `projects.ts` already did after the fix, but `port-memory.ts`'s `rememberLastListen` did not, and the missing `await` was a real regression the focused suite caught (`projects.test.ts` "still remembers the port across a fresh read": `expected undefined to be 4323`), not a false alarm. The red run therefore stashes `port-memory.ts` too. The `await` is behaviour-neutral against the old synchronous function, so the four red cases and the two guards are unchanged by including it.
- Observed (behaviour change, recorded as the brief requires): **an over-long stamp is now DROPPED, not kept.** `workspaceProjectSchema` caps `addedAt`/`lastOpenedAt` at 64 characters; the working file did not cap them at all, so a hand-edited 100-character stamp round-tripped. The new schema mirrors the cap. Truncation was rejected (a half-ISO string parses as nothing), and so was the registry's `''` sentinel: `projects.ts` reads `machine.addedAt ?? DERIVED_AT` and overlays `lastOpenedAt` only when it is not `undefined`, so an `''` fallback would write an empty timestamp into the derived row. The fallback here is `undefined`, with `.min(1)` dropping empty strings too — the file's own "absent means nothing recorded" contract, which is what makes the fallback safe. xezar's own stamps are ~24 characters, so only a hand-edited value is affected.
- Observed: **a test can gate a real read-modify-write without a new production seam, by holding the file lock the fix itself uses.** The cross-process case (`named break lost-update`) takes the machine-state lock in the vitest process, starts a real child that calls the public `recordLastListen`, waits for the child's ready marker (so the only timing assumption is the RMW, not tsx startup), then races the child's exit against 800 ms — well inside the lock's 2 s bound — writes writer B's fact, and releases. Without the lock the child has already written and B's write clobbers it (red); with the lock the child is gated, reads B's fact and keeps both (green). No fs patching, no new export, and the worker uses only symbols that exist before and after the fix, so the file loads in the red run and the two guard cases stay green.
- Observed: **the naive patch of an ESM named import of a builtin does nothing, and that shaped the test.** Patching `require('node:fs').readFileSync` in a child process did not reach `import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'`; only patching before the builtin's ESM namespace is first instantiated (i.e. an `--import` module ahead of `tsx`) worked. Rather than carry a loader fixture, the lock-as-gate design was used — it also tests the actual mechanism instead of an instrumented copy of it.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/workspace` is 278/278 (14 files) at the fixed head, and `npx tsc --noEmit -p packages/xezar/tsconfig.test.json` is clean. The red proof is stored with this run's evidence: `red-proofs/red-649-before-fix.txt` (fix stashed, tests kept) is 4 failed / 2 passed — `unknown-key-dropped`, `unbounded-stamp`, `lost-update` and `silent-failed-write` red, with the fail-open-read and global-layout guards green both ways — and `red-proofs/green-649-after-fix.txt` is 6/6. The global-layout control in `projects.test.ts` and `single-project-home-safety.test.ts` also still pass, so the committed `workspace.json` and the per-user config are untouched.
- Remaining limit: the fix is fixture-tested; no real pair of concurrently starting `xez` processes has been observed contending for this lock since the change, and the 64-character drop is exercised against a hand-edited file, not against a real legacy value on disk. Both are stated on the PR rather than inferred away.

### 2026-09-19 — #680 a failed security gate did not stop `repo-gates.sh` at the security stage, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-fix`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #680 (speed/quality v2 item 11, label `release-0.17.0`), base `main` `e2e53c75`; a bounded kit fix with one red-proof fixture case, no change to `security-scan.sh` or to what counts as a security failure. Deliverable: `.xezar/checks/repo-gates.sh`, one new case plus one guard case in `.xezar/checks/gate-parallel.test.mjs`, `changelog.d/680.md` and this entry.
- Observed: **the comment above the security phase already described the guarantee the code did not implement.** `repo-gates.sh` carried "A failing security stage stops the run here" while the next line was `gate_phase serial 2 || exit 1` — and `gate_phase` returns non-zero only for a scheduler failure or a worker result that could not be collected. A gate that RAN and exited 1 records `failed` and returns 0, so the sentence was true of the design and false of the runner. The fix reads `workers/2.json`'s recorded status and treats anything but `passed`/`not-applicable` as a stop. General shape: when a phase's failure is recorded rather than returned, the caller has to read the record; `|| exit 1` on the phase is not that read.
- Observed: **the "unknown is not a refusal" boundary is what kept the fix small.** `security-scan.sh` exits 0 for `pass`, `unknown` and `not-applicable` and exits 1 only when the result is `findings` or the stage could not read its input, so keying the stop on the gate's recorded outcome stops exactly on a refusal and leaves `unknown` resolving as before — no change to what counts as a security failure. A guard case stubs a security gate that records `unknown` and exits 0 and pins that every gate still runs, and it passes with and without the fix, which is the point: it is the control, not the regression test.
- Observed: **the red proof needed a fixture that drives the real `repo-gates.sh`, and `gate-parallel.test.mjs` already had one.** `infra-tests.sh` drives `lib/gate-record.sh` directly by design ("the suite is itself the last gate, so calling it back would recurse"), so the security-stop case went into `gate-parallel.test.mjs`, whose `outerFixture` copies the real checks tree into a scratch git repository and replaces only path discovery and the agent CLIs. Reusing it cost one `stubSecurity` helper (three lines) and no new fixture machinery.
- Observed: **the red transcript is the defect, verbatim.** With the security gate stubbed to exit 1, the pre-fix runner printed the gate as `"status":"failed","exitCode":1` and then ran `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run test:unit`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:package` and the repository-check tail, all recorded `passed`, and only the aggregate at the end was `failed`. Post-fix the same fixture prints `GATES STOPPED AT THE SECURITY STAGE: the security gate recorded "failed", so no quality gate ran.`, records exactly two commands, and completes the attempt as `failed`.
- Observed: **extracting the completion path into `gate_finish` is what makes "never left half-written" true of the early stop too.** The summary block, the completion call and both exit paths moved into one function that the security stop and the normal end of the run share, so there is one place where an attempt is published rather than two that can drift. The signal trap and `gate_attempt_complete` are unchanged, and the existing `cancellation racing completed finalization` case — which monkeypatches `gate_attempt_complete` by name — still passes because the function is called by name at call time.
- Observed (kit friction, third recorded shape of the same guard): **the isolated-task shell guard refused a `write` to the run's own evidence directory under the primary checkout** ("this isolated task must keep project writes and Git commands inside its worktree"), even though the brief names that directory as allowed. The same write through a variable-expanded `$EV` in `bash` succeeded, and `write` into the worktree's own `.local/` followed by `cp` to `$EV` also succeeded. The guard reads the literal path text, so a brief that asks for a file in the evidence dir should say "write it in the worktree and copy it with a shell variable", or the guard needs a write allowance for `.local/xezar/tasks/<runId>/`.
- Regression/control: `node --test .xezar/checks/gate-parallel.test.mjs` is 17/17 at the fixed head, including the unchanged `failed typecheck retains later build/package evidence and cannot certify`, which is the control for the application phase this change deliberately does not touch. The red proof is the new case failing against the pre-fix script (assertion `expected /GATES STOPPED AT THE SECURITY STAGE/`) and passing after; both the `node --test` transcripts and a matched pair of direct `repo-gates.sh` fixture runs are under this run's evidence `red-proofs/` (`before-fix.txt`, `after-fix.txt`, `manual-before-fix.txt`, `manual-after-fix.txt`, `fixture-repro.mjs`). `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` passed 667 cases / 0 failed.
- Remaining limit: the stop is proven on a stubbed security gate, not by a real refused scan at a real candidate; what the real stage does on refusal is `security-scan.sh`'s own contract and is unchanged here. The recorded statuses are read from `workers/2.json`, so a future renumbering of the canonical gate list has to carry this index with it — the same renumbering note `lib/gate-parallel.mjs` already carries for gates 3–7.

### 2026-09-19 — #704 the changelog fold's `## ` group scan was not fence-aware, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #704 (`bug`, `release-0.17.0`) and the leader's bounded brief — one scan in `.xezar/checks/changelog-fragments.mjs` plus `.xezar/checks/fragments.test.mjs`, a red proof run against a scratch copy of the pre-fix script (never `git stash`), and four named kit checks. Base `main` `63d70332`. Kit-only: `.xezar/checks/` ships in nothing, so no released `xezar --version` exhibits this.
- Observed: **the issue's reproduction was exact on current main, and reproducing it first is what made the fix one line.** `mergeIntoSection` is handed the fence map `inside` (it passes it to `sectionEnd` one line earlier) and then walks the section body with `for (const line of body) { if (line.startsWith('## ')) … }`, so an unindented `## ` line in a fence became a group boundary. Folding the issue's fixture exited 0 and rewrote the section with the fenced `## My Custom Group` hoisted to the bottom and the closing fence stranded above it. The fix is `if (!inside[headingIndex + 1 + i] && line.startsWith('## '))` with the loop carrying its index — because `body` is a slice, `body[i]` is `lines[headingIndex + 1 + i]`, which is the index `inside` is keyed by.
- Observed: **the issue's non-triggering claims held, and one of them is the reason this survived to a filed issue.** An indented fence, and an unindented fence whose `## ` line is indented, both folded with only the fragment bullet added. The scan is anchored at column 0, so the corruption needs a column-0 `## ` line inside a fence — which the real `CHANGELOG.md` has never had (its one fence holds `npm install -g @qodeca/xezar`). The issue itself dropped the review's "the real CHANGELOG.md indents its fenced samples" claim; I confirmed the real fence is unindented and simply holds no heading.
- Observed: **my first "no group was hoisted" assertion was itself fence-blind.** Filtering the re-emitted section for `## ` lines counted the sample line the fix had correctly left inside the fence, so the green run failed on the assertion rather than on the fix. The assertion now removes the fenced block first and then checks the remaining `## ` lines are exactly the role's two — which is what "not hoisted" actually means. A test can reproduce a bug's shape and still encode it.
- Regression/control: the red proof is a `cp` of the pre-fix script plus the new test into a scratch checks directory (`.local/xezar/704-repro/checks-pre/`), run with `node --test --test-name-pattern="#704"`: pre-fix `fail 1` on `the fenced sample must survive intact`, post-fix `pass 1`. The whole file is 29/29 after the fix. The two `#684`/`#685` guard cases (`a changelog with no fence folds to exactly the same bytes as before`, `a section of only house headings folds to the same bytes as before`) pass both ways and are labelled guards — they pin the default path the fix must not change.
- Remaining limit: the fold is proven on fixtures, not by a release run against the real `CHANGELOG.md`. Three sibling scans are deliberately untouched, all outside this brief's bounded scope. The issue names two of them as the same root cause: `separatorIndex` (:178) still does not take the fence map (a `---` inside a fence could be read as the section separator) and `parseFragment`'s `## ` scan (:107) is not fence-aware, though it fails closed on an unknown heading rather than corrupting. The third I found while checking the fix: `foldChangelog`'s existing-section lookup, `lines.findIndex((line) => line.startsWith('# <version> ('))`, is also not fence-aware — a fixture whose `# Unreleased` fence holds the literal line `# 0.16.0 (2026-09-01)` makes the fold treat that fenced line as the section heading and shred the fence, which I reproduced and then left alone. It needs the exact version and date being folded, so it is far less reachable than the group scan was; it is still the same class of defect and deserves its own issue rather than a scope creep here.

### 2026-09-19 — owner correction of the review-fix rule in `.xezar/docs/leader-guide.md` § Review discipline, `docs-maintenance` step `Update docs`, `xezar-docs-maintenance`, pi (DeepSeek) — real-task observed
- Input: the owner's chat words of 2026-09-19 ("Review fix loop should be limited, not completely abandoned. One review fix iteration is OK"), the brief's exact target locations (the Review discipline section and the checklist line under "On each verdict") and its tag rule. Deliverable: the corrected rule text and this fragment. No source change.
- Observed: **a dated owner tag makes the old rule a record, so the correction is an added sentence, not an edit in place.** The 2026-09-16 19:45 tag was attached to the sentence "Never let a PR spin in a review → fix → review loop"; rewriting those words and keeping the tag would have moved the tag onto words the owner did not say. The fix kept that sentence byte-identical and appended the new rule as its own sentence carrying (owner 2026-09-19). A wording correction whose instruction is "keep the old tag where it is" is therefore not a find-and-replace, and the diff is the proof.
- Observed (kit): **a kit-docs-only change needs no changelog fragment, and the gate says so rather than the author guessing.** `changelog-check.sh --diff-base auto --fragments changelog.d` reported "no direct edit of CHANGELOG.md" and OK, so no fragment was written; the brief's conditional fragment was correctly not created.
- Regression/control: not applicable — the change is prose in a kit document, so the proof is the read-back diff and the passing checks (link check, catalog check, changelog check), not a red test.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine; only the text was verified, and the changed rule's effect on a real review loop is not exercised here. The changed wording is authoritative only once the PR merges.

- Remaining limit: this is a kit-internal script with no released `xezar --version` exhibiting the defect (the issue says so), so the "reproduction" is a fixture run of the real module at the task's base revision rather than a live release incident; and the PR for this fix is opened by the workflow's separate handoff step, so this fragment carries the run id and the changelog fragment carries `(#685)` rather than a PR number.

### 2026-09-19 — #697 review response round 1 (the per-field `.catch` guard, raw guard bodies, comment precision), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` REQUEST CHANGES on PR #697 (reviewed head `81673ceb`, branch `xez/4f63e0c4`, base `main` `32516a26`) and the leader's dispositions: one Major (the mixed-validity case the new test file never pinned) and three Minors, all four fixed with tests only plus one doc line plus one comment. Deliverable: one new case in `project-machine-state.test.ts`, the raw-body guard fix, the warn-once comment reword, the `afterEach` reset, the `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` line, this fragment and the phase record. No product behaviour changed.
- Observed: **the skill's `DELIVERED` path and the preflight's branch rule still cannot both hold, and the branch rule is the one that runs.** `xezar-review-response` says the fix lands on the PR's own branch and this task's branch stays empty, recorded as `DELIVERED`; `worktree-preflight.sh --readiness` refuses any branch but `xez/<id8>` and refuses a detached HEAD, while `xez/4f63e0c4` is already checked out by the author's worktree and cannot be adopted here. The compliant path was the one `d3e1d44e.md` recorded: merge the PR head into this task's branch, commit here, and push `HEAD` to `refs/heads/xez/4f63e0c4` with a plain `git push` (`worktree-git.sh push`'s refspec is fixed to this run's own branch). With own commits present readiness passes on `branch.has-own-commits`, and `DELIVERED` must NOT be written — the preflight's own refusal text says a run asked to change source on its own branch must not write either record. The skill text and the check still disagree about how this workflow reaches the PR branch; `d3e1d44e.md` said one of them needs to say which wins, and that is still true.

That is whole-object rejection dropping a good sibling, exactly the data loss the review described. Restoring the real file with `cp` and re-running gave 7/7. The mutation and the restore were verified byte-identical with `diff` before the scratch directory was removed.
- Observed: **the fail-open guard was testing one branch nine times.** `writeRaw` JSON-stringifies, so `''`, `'not json'`, `'[]'`, `'null'`, `'"a string"'`, `'42'` and `'true'` all landed on disk as a JSON string, and `JSON.parse` never threw and no real array was ever written — the parse-failure `catch` and the `Array.isArray` branch were both uncovered. Writing the bodies with `writeFileSync(statePath, body, 'utf8')` and adding `'{oops'` and `[1,2]` is what makes the guard mean what its name says; it still passes at head, because the behaviour was already right.
- Observed: **the warn-once flag is coarser than `warnOncePerState` and the comment said "mirrors".** `warnOncePerState` is a Map keyed by path whose value is the exact bytes, so it re-warns when the failure changes; `warnedWriteFailure` is one boolean latched for the process lifetime, so a later and different persistent failure is silent. The comment now says that plainly and no longer claims a mirror. The test file gained an `afterEach` calling the module's existing `resetProjectMachineStateWriteWarning`, so the latch is re-armed between cases rather than only at the start of the one case that asserts it.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/workspace` is **279/279** (14 files) at the fixed head (278 before, +1 new case). The two pre-existing guards still pass both ways, and the existing six cases are unchanged and green.
- Remaining limit: the fix is still fixture-tested; no real pair of concurrently starting `xez` processes has been observed contending for this lock, and the 64-character drop is exercised against a hand-edited file, not a real legacy value on disk. Both remain stated on the PR. The three Minors are test/comment/doc precision only — no product behaviour changed — and the product code the review called correct is untouched.

### 2026-09-19 — #691 the security early-stop read the wrong status vocabulary and delegated its own verdict, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation` / `xezar-bug-fix`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #691 (Minors 1–2 of the #687 review, label `release-0.17.0`), base `main` `36969331`; a bounded kit fix with one red-proof fixture case. Deliverable: `.xezar/checks/repo-gates.sh` (the `case` arm and its comment, plus the `gate_finish` expected-outcome argument), one new case in `.xezar/checks/gate-parallel.test.mjs`, `changelog.d/691.md`, a one-sentence correction in `changelog.d/680.md`, and this entry.
- Observed: **both claims were checked against the code before acting, and both hold.** `lib/gate-record.sh`'s `gate_collect_worker` validates `workers/2.json` to exactly `passed` / `failed` / `not-run`, so the `passed | not-applicable` arm could never match; the security vocabulary `pass` / `findings` / `unknown` / `not-applicable` lives in `security.json` (`lib/gate-results.mjs`). `gate_finish` contains its own `exit 0`, so the issue's literal second correction — "`exit 1` straight after the `gate_finish` call" — would be dead code. The issue's *other* offered correction, an expected-outcome argument, is the one that actually makes a green exit impossible; the brief left the choice open, so the argument was taken and the dead-code option recorded as not viable. The reviewed head's `repo-gates.sh` sha256 (`7e429636…`) matches the base file, so the reviewed bytes were the bytes fixed.
- Observed: **the dead arm cannot be proven by a behavioural test, and that is the finding, not a gap.** Nothing the real worker can record is `not-applicable` (`gate_collect_worker` refuses it), so the only change Minor 1 admits is deleting unreachable code and correcting the comment; the regression test that catches a real bug is Minor 2's. The distinction matters for the review: one of the two changes is proven red-without-fix, the other is a dead-code and prose correction with no observable behaviour to break.
- Observed: **the red proof is the defect verbatim, and it is a pass that never should have been printed.** With `gate_attempt_complete` stubbed to return 0, the pre-fix runner printed the security gate as `"status":"failed","exitCode":1`, then `GATES STOPPED AT THE SECURITY STAGE: the security gate recorded "failed"…`, then `ALL GATES PASSED`, and exited 0 — a refused scan certified green by the completer's return code alone. Post-fix the same fixture exits 1 and never prints `ALL GATES PASSED`, while the completer still writes the record.
- Observed: **a scratch `cp -R` of the checks tree is enough for a red proof, because the fixture resolves its own directory.** `gate-parallel.test.mjs` computes `checks` from `import.meta.url`, so running `node --test --test-name-pattern=… <scratch>/checks/gate-parallel.test.mjs` with `<scratch>/checks/repo-gates.sh` restored to the pre-fix bytes drives the pre-fix runner with the new case. No `git stash` (whose stack is shared across worktrees) and no edit-then-revert of the worktree were needed; the two command lines and both transcripts are under this run's evidence `red-proofs/`.
- Observed (kit friction, same guard shape as `50c94129`): **the isolated-task shell guard refused a command that named the primary checkout's path literally**, while the identical work through a `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)`-derived variable succeeded. The evidence directory the brief names as allowed is reachable, but only via a variable-expanded path.
- Regression/control: `node --test .xezar/checks/gate-parallel.test.mjs` is 18/18 at the fixed head, including the three controls this change must not disturb — the #687 case `a failing security stage stops the run before any quality gate and completes the attempt as failed`, the application-phase case `failed typecheck retains later build/package evidence and cannot certify`, and the guard `a resolved-but-unknown security stage still runs every gate` (which passes with and without the fix and is labelled a control, not a proof). The new case fails against the pre-fix script (`AssertionError … actual: 0, expected: 0`, with `ALL GATES PASSED` in the captured output) and passes after. `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` passed; `node .xezar/checks/catalog-check.mjs` and `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/tracked-files.test.ts` passed.
- Remaining limit: the stop is proven on a stubbed security gate and a stubbed completer, not by a real refused scan at a real candidate; what the real stage does on refusal is `security-scan.sh`'s own contract and is unchanged. The expected-outcome argument is required with no default, so a future caller that omits it fails closed (empty never equals `passed`) — a deliberate choice, not an oversight. `workers/2.json`'s index still has to be carried by any future renumbering of the canonical gate list, the same note `lib/gate-parallel.mjs` already carries for gates 3–7.

### 2026-09-19 — #692 round 1 review response on PR #695, `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude (Opus 5), run `92ad3202` — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: the `## Code review` comment of run `114d2521` (Claude Fable 5.1) on PR #695 — REQUEST CHANGES, one Major — with its probe (`probes/zz-review-probe-692.test.ts` + `probes/probe-mock.diff`) in its own evidence directory, and the leader's adjudication to fix that Major and nothing else. Deliverable: the `LIVE_PART_KINDS` allowlist in `opencode-server-runner.ts`, a wire-faithful `idle` mode in the mock server, one new permanent test, the corrected `changelog.d/692.md`, `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md`, `AGENT_PROTOCOL.md` and PR body, and this fragment.
- Observed: **a mock "read off the real transcripts" still missed the part those transcripts carry, because the part is not called by its name in them.** The fix's own evidence pass read all 28 denial transcripts and correctly derived the TIMING (the turn ends 0.1–0.3 s after a refusal). What it did not derive was the CONTENT of the frames in that gap: 14 of 14 denial samples carry a `usage.updated` between the failed tool result and the turn end, and `usage.updated` is precisely what the v2 mapper emits from a `step-finish` PART (`opencode-ui-mapper.ts`, `mapStepFinish`). The transcript never says "step-finish"; it says the thing the mapper renamed. So a fixture written from the transcripts reproduced the timing faithfully and dropped the one frame the new predicate depended on — and the predicate ("any part the session had not started before") was disarmed by it on every real denial while the suite stayed green. The lesson is narrower than "test against the real server": when a fixture is derived from a DERIVED stream, the derivation has to be inverted deliberately, frame kind by frame kind, or the fixture silently encodes the renaming as an absence.
- Observed: **the reviewer's red proof and this one answer different questions, and only the second one could catch this.** Replacing the whole file with the PR base (no watchdog at all) makes the stall cases fail — but they fail for the base's reason, not for the predicate's. The proof that mattered here was narrower: put back the REVIEWED HEAD's runner under the CORRECTED fixture (`red-A-reviewed-head-runner.log`, 2 failed / 3 passed — both `idle`-shape cases time out, while `hang`, the carries-on guard and the constant assertion pass). That split is the one that says "this round's change is load-bearing", and it only exists because the fixture was fixed first. Against the PR base the same run is 4 failed / 1 passed (`red-B-base-9e97a149-runner.log`).
- Observed: **a fail-open counting predicate needs a permanent case that pins the INPUT, not only the outcome.** The new test waits for the `usage.updated` the corrected mock now produces before asserting the watchdog still fires, so a future edit that drops the `step-finish` part from the fixture fails the case instead of quietly returning it to the green-either-way state it was in. Asserting only "the error fires" would pass against a mock that stopped emitting the part at all.
- Remaining limit: unchanged and still the binding one — fixture-tested only. No live `opencode serve` session has been run against this correction either, so "the real server writes a fresh `step-finish` part after a rejected tool call" rests on the 14 run-store samples and on the installed 1.18.31 binary's embedded processor source, both read, neither reproduced live. OpenCode stays out of the routing rotation until a live retrial says otherwise.
