### 2026-09-19 — #650 the single-project `workspace.json` carried the machine's multi-project keys, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #650 (labels `bug`, `release-0.17.0`) and the owner's widened-scope comment on it, the affected release 0.16.0 at base `fc461704`, and the bounded-fix brief. Deliverable: the layout-aware projection in `packages/xezar/src/workspace/config.ts` (shared with `import-global.ts`), four test cases plus two adapted existing assertions, `changelog.d/650.md`, the `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` and guide updates, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **the reproduction matched the issue byte-for-byte, and it located the write where the issue said it was.** A fresh project-layout boot on the pre-fix source (`createProjectStateFiles` then `runMigrations`) wrote exactly `browseRoot: "~/"`, `projectsDir: "~/xezar/projects"` and `projects: []` into `.xezar/workspace.json`; `createProjectStateFiles` itself writes `{}`, so the culprit is the merge-write re-serializing the fully parsed config, reached through migration 001. With the fix the same script writes none of the three and still materializes `schemaVersion`, `resources` and the rest.
- Observed: **the brief, the issue's Expected and the issue comment's acceptance addition agree on a fresh start, and disagree only on a stored row — resolved in favour of the acceptance text.** The comment's Table A says the writer should "drop the empty default … keep reading a row for this folder", while its own additions and the brief both say the writer writes no `projects`. The fix omits all three top-level keys on every project-layout write and leaves the reader untouched, which satisfies both acceptance lists (a fresh start has no row to keep) and leaves SP-3.1's matching-row read exactly as it was; the one consequence — a 0.16.0 clone's committed row is dropped by the next write — is stated in `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md`. Recording it because "which of two documents governs the writer" was the only genuinely ambiguous point in this task.
- Observed (kit friction, another shape of the shell guard): the isolated-task guard refused a `cat >> … <<'EOF'` append whose heredoc merely *contained* a foreign absolute path as test fixture data (`/Users/other/source`), not a command argument. The guard reads command text, so a test that models another machine's home cannot be appended through the shell; it had to be written with the editor. The same guard also refused `ls -d` on the primary evidence directory by literal path, while the same directory resolved through `.xezar/checks/lib/common.sh` worked — which is the documented path and is worth saying again for the next run.
- Observed: **a source-text guard that pins an exact call expression is brittle to a legitimate signature extension.** `config-lock.test.ts` asserted the literal `withWorkspaceConfigLock(path, () => mergeWriteLocked(path, mutator))`; adding the project-layout argument broke it. It is now a regex that still requires the lock to wrap `mergeWriteLocked` and accepts both the old and new call shape, so it passes with and without the fix — the guard's intent (no writer bypasses the lock) is unchanged and it no longer fails for a reason unrelated to its subject.
- Regression/control: red proof at `.local/xezar/tasks/a7652982-…/red-proofs/red-without-fix.log` — restoring a scratch copy of the pre-fix `config.ts` and `import-global.ts` (never `git stash`) and re-running the focused suite gives 5 failed / 62 passed, and every failure is a new or adapted assertion about the three keys. The two guard cases that must pass both ways did: the new global-layout guard and the adapted `config-lock` guard. With the fix restored, `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/workspace packages/xezar/src/single-project-isolation.test.ts packages/xezar/src/state-path-scan.test.ts` is 300/300, and `npm run typecheck` is clean.
- Remaining limit: fixture-tested and reproduced at the module/CLI level, not against a live 0.16.0 binary upgrading in place; the "0.16.0-written file" case is a hand-built file with the exact three keys rather than a file produced by the shipped 0.16.0 binary. The `projects`-omitted choice also means a committed registry row for the folder is dropped by the next write; that is the issue's stated Expected and is documented, but it is a behaviour change for a clone that carried one and has not been exercised end to end.

### 2026-09-19 — #684 the changelog fold inserts the new release section inside a fenced code block, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, pi (deepseek-flash) — real-task observed, fixture-tested
- Input: issue #684 as verified by the leader (file:line at PR #683 head `51b73638`), the bounded-fix brief naming `isTopHeading` and its two call sites, `changelog-check.sh` :81–83/:162–173 as the rule to match, and the mandatory red proof plus the byte-identical no-fence guard. Deliverable: the fence-aware helper in `.xezar/checks/changelog-fragments.mjs`, three cases in `.xezar/checks/fragments.test.mjs`, `changelog.d/<pr>.md`, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **the issue's reproduction is exact, and the byte diff is the proof.** Folding `0.2.0` into a `# Unreleased` section holding a fenced `# not-a-real-heading` put the heading, the `## 🐛 Fixes` group and the `---` separator all between the opening and closing fence markers, then left the fence content and its closing marker below them (`red-proofs/fence-path-before.txt` vs `fence-path-fixed.txt`). The fix moves exactly that block below the closing marker and above `# 0.1.0`, and the byte diff of the fence-free default path before vs after the fix is empty (`default-path-before.txt` / `default-path-fixed.txt`) — the guard case alone would not have shown that, so the diff was run as well.
- Observed: **the brief's two fence instructions do not describe the same rule, and the fold now implements the wider one.** `changelog-check.sh` toggles on lines starting with ``` only (`case "$line" in '```'*` and the awk `/^```/`); it has no `~~~` handling at all, so "``` or ~~~ fences, the same rule `changelog-check.sh` already applies" cannot both be literal. The helper tracks the opening marker, so both kinds toggle, a `~~~` line inside a ``` block is content rather than a closer, and an unclosed fence runs to EOF — which is what the check's single boolean does for backticks. Verified on a `~~~` fixture and a mixed one; the check's verdict is unaffected because a buried non-Unreleased, non-dated `# ` line changes nothing it counts. The check remains backtick-only, and that asymmetry is a real, pre-existing gap in the check rather than something this bounded fix closed.
- Observed: **"match its behaviour exactly" and "nothing else changes" leave two same-class sites fence-blind, and the brief's own enumeration is what decides that.** `lines.findIndex((line) => line.startsWith('# ' + version + ' ('))` (the already-created-section path) and `mergeIntoSection`'s `## ` group parsing both still read fenced lines as structure, so a fenced `# 0.2.0 (` or `## 🐛 Fixes` inside the version section would still be misread. Only `isTopHeading` and its two call sites were in scope, and widening the helper to those sites would have been an unrequested behaviour change with no red case behind it; they are recorded as remaining limits instead of fixed silently.
- Observed: **a test whose NAME covered the harm while its assertion covered only the model** (PR #696 review, Major M1). The case `a fence that opens and never closes is content to the end of the file (#684)` asserted only that the new section's heading index followed the fenced text — an ordering that holds just as well WHILE the section sits inside the still-open fence, which is the harm the fix exists to prevent, and the fold exited 0. Renamed to what it pins (`a file that ends inside an open fence is refused, not folded into (#684)`) and joined by a second case on the reviewer's own fixture, which asserts the refusal, the untouched bytes and that no `# 0.2.0` was written anywhere. AGENTS.md § *Changing a mechanism that already works* names exactly this — keep the guard tests that pass both ways, but know which is which; a name is not evidence, and a green-either-way assertion reads as coverage without being any.
- Regression/control: `node --test .xezar/checks/fragments.test.mjs` is 24/24 at the fixed head; with the fix stashed and the tests kept it is 22 passed / 2 failed — the fenced fixture and the unclosed-fence case — while the byte-exact no-fence guard stays green on BOTH revisions, which is the point of keeping it. `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` is 667 passed / 0 failed (it runs `fragments.test.mjs` unconditionally as its own `-- changelog and dogfooding fragments --` case), and `node .xezar/checks/catalog-check.mjs` is `CATALOG OK`. The red transcript is stored with this run's evidence at `red-proofs/red-before-fix.txt`.
- Remaining limit: the fold is proven on fixtures through the real script; no release has folded a real fragment against a CHANGELOG containing a fence since the change, because `CHANGELOG.md` carries none today (the issue says so, and the fenced fixture is the closest the repository can get without editing `# Unreleased`). The `~~~` marker kind is covered by a fixture and by the mixed-fence case, not by any real changelog in the tree, and `changelog-check.sh`'s backtick-only fence walk is left as it was.

The brief's "keep them green" was read as extending the committed case to cover the eleven-id table, which also makes the new `README`/`..foo`/`x` refusals sit next to the separators-and-dot-segments refusals they belong with.

The pre-fix transcript is stored at this run's evidence `red-proofs/red-1-prefix-new-cases.txt` (4 failed / 31 passed, with the failing ids named), the fixed pair at `red-proofs/green-2-after-fix.txt` (224 passed). The pre-fix module was copied with `cp` to `.local/red-proof/`, never `git stash`, whose stack is shared by every worktree of this repo, and both modules were sha256-diffed back into place.

### 2026-09-19 — issue #703, the injectable OpenCode attach-check bound, `bug-fix` step `Reproduce, diagnose and fix`, pi (DeepSeek) — real-task observed

- Input: issue #703 (OPEN), a follow-up Minor 3 from the code review of PR #700 (`c5a6bbdb`), read with `gh issue view 703 --json`; the owner-authored task brief for run `b33161ec` fixing scope (`leader-delivery.ts` and its test file only), the `heartbeatMs` pattern, the unchanged 10 s default, the one never-answering-server case and the scratch-copy red proof. Deliverable: one internal option threaded into `LeaderDeliveryOptions`, one new regression case plus a never-answering fake server in `leader-delivery.test.ts`, `changelog.d/703.md`, this fragment, the phase record and one focused commit.
- Observed: **every claim in the issue held against main `63d70332`, and the code needed no reinterpretation.** `OPENCODE_ATTACH_CHECK_MS = 10_000` at `leader-delivery.ts:146`, passed to `AbortSignal.timeout` inside `checkOpenCodeAttach`, composed with the adapter's own close signal in `#call` via `AbortSignal.any`; and a grep for the never-answering bound found only `pi-link.test.ts`, so nothing pinned it. The fix is the reviewer's own correction, verbatim: the option goes beside `heartbeatMs` and the call site resolves it with the same nullish default.
- Observed: **the red proof is a bound assertion, not a compile error, and that is worth recording.** vitest transpiles TypeScript with esbuild and never type-checks, so the pre-fix module accepted the unknown `opencodeAttachCheckMs` key in the test's options object, ignored it and waited the shipped 10 s default: the case failed with `AssertionError: expected 10003 to be less than 5000` after 10 013 ms. The green run finishes the same case in 161 ms against the injected 150 ms bound. A test written to prove a *timeout* seam therefore proves itself by *failing a time assertion* when the seam is missing — the run's own `npm run typecheck` lane is what covers the type surface the red run cannot.
- Regression/control: `npm test -- packages/xezar/src/mcp` passes 70 files / 2214 tests; `npm run test:coverage:mcp` passes with `leader-delivery.ts` at 99.33 % lines, 91.79 % branches, 97.62 % statements and 95.58 % functions, above the 80 % per-file floor; the existing default-path cases (closed port, wrong project, successful attach) are the guards that pass both ways and pin the unchanged shipped behaviour, and the new case is the regression that is red without the option.
- Remaining limit: the bound is verified with a fake server on loopback, not against a real paused `opencode serve`; and the phase record's own scope is one task worktree, so the fragment reports the seam and the test, not a live-attach measurement.

### 2026-09-19 — PR #707 review response round 1 (issue #647, PR 2 of 2), `address-review-findings`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed

- Input: the ONE `## Code review` comment on PR #707 (Claude Fable 5.1, run `4587970a`, REQUEST CHANGES — one Major, two Minors, two Nits), the leader's disposition to fix all five in this round, the reviewer's throwaway test and the approved spec. Deliverable: the five fixes with red proofs, three new permanent tests, the updated red-proof harness cases, this fragment and the changelog fragment.
- Observed: **a spec table and a spec rationale disagreed, and only the reviewer's "what was the old mechanism load-bearing FOR" experiment found it.** § 1's automations row said "ignore the whole removal when `superseded`"; § 2(1) item 4 said the listener is "the only exit after a drift rebuild". Both readings compile, both pass the suite as written, and the author implemented the row. The leader ruled for the rationale, and the resulting behaviour is neither of the two: a superseded dispose REMOVES and then re-adds from the current `listProjects()` row. Skipping keeps a wrong registration after a drift; removing without the re-add drops a live one. The lesson is that the two failures sit on opposite sides of one line, so "which branch wins" was the wrong question — the fix had to answer both.
- Observed: **the throwaway that found the Major became the permanent test almost unchanged, but its assertions had to be inverted.** The reviewer's version asserts the head's behaviour (`add` NOT called with the new root) to demonstrate the defect; the permanent one asserts the fix (`remove` called, then `add(id, rootNew)`, never `add(id, rootOld)`). A verdict's throwaway is a reproduction, not a regression test, and copying it verbatim would have pinned the bug.
- Observed: **an existing regression test's literal assertion could not survive the fix, and the net-effect assertion it was really about could.** RP-5 asserted `remove` was NOT called for a re-added project. Under remove-then-re-add that is false while what RP-5 exists to pin — the live project stays in both coordinators — is still true, now expressed as `remove` followed by `add(id, sameRoot)`. Rewriting an assertion during a review response is the shape that hides a regression, so the change is called out explicitly in the response comment rather than left in the diff.
- Observed (method): **the first version of the new SSE test passed against its own defect and had to be rewritten before it proved anything.** It emitted the disposed project's usage row and the boot row in ONE snapshot and read until the boot marker; SSE ordering meant the leaked project's event could still be in flight at the assertion. Emitting the two rows as two ordered snapshots — the dead project first, the boot row as the control that proves the fan-out ran — made it RED against the review's probe A. The harness is what caught it: the case reported `STILL-GREEN (proves nothing)`, exactly the outcome the repaired label below exists to make legible.
- Observed (harness, Nit 1): the other half of the PR 1 nit O-1 was real. `vitest -t <nothing>` exits 0 with `24 skipped`, and the proof branch called that `STILL-GREEN` — the label says a defect survived a test when no test ran. Demonstrated both ways on this branch (`.local/xezar/tasks/bdeb39e5…/red-proofs/nit1-zero-test-label.txt`): the shipped harness prints `STILL-GREEN (proves nothing) [0 passed / 0 failed]`, the repaired one `INVALID (no test executed …)`.
- Observed (guard design, Minor 2): a source-text scan that classifies some spellings and skips the rest is the fail-open helper AGENTS.md names — the four shipped listeners all matched, so the hole was invisible. Counting every `onContextDisposed(` in a file and requiring the count to EQUAL the classified sites moves the failure from "silently skipped" to "this scan cannot classify this call", and it does not depend on the regex ever being complete. It did need one carve-out the count check itself revealed immediately: the hook's own TYPE signature in `project-doors.ts` is an occurrence and not a call.
- Regression/control: five red proofs, each failing exactly the test it names — `automations-superseded-skips-refresh` RED 27/2, `automations-disposed-listener-does-nothing` RED 0/1, `sse-disposed-listener-does-nothing` RED 11/1, `ac9-scan-blind-to-a-spelling` RED 0/1 — plus the guard `automations-never-built-removal` STILL-GREEN against the Major's own defect. The Major also has an explicit run against a byte copy of the pre-fix `server.ts` (red) and the same command after restoring the fix (green), and Minor 2 an explicit old-scan-green / new-scan-red pair. Evidence: `.local/xezar/tasks/bdeb39e5…/red-proofs/`.
- Remaining limit: still code- and fixture-verified only. Nothing here was observed on a live cockpit, and the drift + overlapping-request shape the Major describes has no field report — it is reproduced with the real modules in a fixture, as the reviewer's own was.

### 2026-09-19 — #647 PR 1, the generation-aware context-dispose seam, `bug-fix` step `fix`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Opus 5 — real-task observed

- Input: a leader-approved spec (read-only, written by an earlier run) naming the exact seam change,
  the construction sites to grep, the two named breaks and the acceptance criteria. The skill's own
  "read run/history evidence first, then reproduce" step was already spent by that spec, so this run
  started at "implement and prove", not at "diagnose".
- Observed: the spec's § 4 "grep the TYPE, not the field" was the load-bearing instruction. `rg
  'ProjectContext[^sEDSF]'` found the one literal a field-name grep would have missed
  (`server.ts:1224`, the boot context), and making `generation` REQUIRED turned that into a compile
  error rather than an `undefined` that reads as 0 — confirmed by deleting the field and watching
  `tsc` fail with `TS2741` at exactly that line.
- Observed: the existing red-proof harness (`packages/xezar/test/red-proofs.mjs`) documented a
  `kind: 'guard'` concept in its header comment that the runner never implemented — every case was
  scored "RED or failure". Adding the two guard cases the spec asked for required implementing it.
  A harness whose comment describes a feature it does not have reads as done.
- Regression/control: both named breaks went RED against their owning test file; both guard cases
  (door dedupe, re-add waits for the old door) stayed green against those same defects, which is what
  makes them guards and not proofs. Two reviewer experiments from the spec were run as controls and
  both failed as designed: deleting the boot literal's field (typecheck red) and flipping RP-1's
  expected `superseded` to `false` (test red, so the flag is computed and not a constant).

  three — workspace SSE, terminal, automations/skills coordinators — still take the one-argument
  listener and still carry the same race; they compile because the payload is additive, which is
  exactly the "a replacement that ships OFF" trap AGENTS.md names. PR 2 owns them, and the fan-out
  guard added here is what keeps that visible rather than silent.

### 2026-09-19 — #683 review response round 1 (the fold's own guard test, and a refusal that could not be repaired forward), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` REQUEST CHANGES on PR #683 (reviewed head `51b73638`, branch `xez/e3c4f765`, base `5b172942`) and the leader's adjudication naming Major 1, Major 2 and Minor 3 as in scope and Minor 1 / Minor 2 as filed (#684, #685); `origin/main` `e0c66b0f` (#666, #682) merged into the branch before any edit. Deliverable: `changelog-check.sh`, `fragments.test.mjs`, one new fixture case, this fragment and the changelog line already in `changelog.d/668.md`; no product code.
- Observed: **the skill's `DELIVERED` path and the preflight's branch rule cannot both hold in a fresh task worktree, and the branch rule is the one that runs.** `xezar-review-response` says the fix belongs on the PR's own branch and this task's branch stays empty, recorded as `DELIVERED`; `worktree-preflight.sh` — which readiness runs in strict mode — refuses any branch but `xez/<id8>` and refuses a detached HEAD, while the PR branch is already checked out by the author's worktree and cannot be adopted here. The only compliant path was to commit on this task's own branch and push `HEAD` to `refs/heads/xez/e3c4f765` with a plain `git push` — the same conclusion the 2026-09-19 #634 entry reached from `worktree-git.sh push`'s fixed refspec. With own commits present readiness passes on `branch.has-own-commits`, and `DELIVERED` must NOT be written: the preflight's own refusal text says a run asked to change source on its own branch must not write it. The skill text and the check disagree about how this workflow reaches the PR branch, and one of them needs to say which wins.
- Observed: **a rule that reads HISTORY stays red after the branch repairs itself.** The direct-`# Unreleased`-edit refusal walked the branch's `--first-parent --no-merges` commits and refused on the first one that touched the region, so reverting the edit and moving the bullet into the fragment the refusal names — the repair it asks for — was still refused. The only escape was amend/squash/rebase plus a force-push, which the message never said and which this workflow forbids; an agent would have fixed forward, stayed red, and spent its two gate-repair returns on a branch it had already corrected. The rule now refuses only when a per-commit edit exists AND the region at HEAD still differs from the region at the diff base. General shape: when a check objects to a state, it has to ask whether that state is still there, not whether it was ever reached.
- Observed: **the change's own guard test asserted the existence of the file the change deletes.** `changelog.d/668.md` is folded away by the release role this pull request adds, and `fragments.test.mjs` asserted it existed — so the next release went red for doing exactly the right thing, in the role whose repair budget is tightest. The case now asserts what survives the mechanism's whole lifecycle (both folders with their `README.md`) and that `--check changelog.d` parses whatever is present, including the README-only directory a fold leaves behind. This is the shape AGENTS.md § *Changing a mechanism that already works* warns about: the new mechanism was correct and the tests were green today.
- Observed: **the red proof for that guard test cost one scratch tree, because the case derives the repository from its own location.** Copying `.xezar/checks/` plus a README-only `changelog.d/` into a scratch directory and running the reviewed suite there reproduced the failure verbatim with no change to this checkout, and dropping the corrected suite into the same tree turned it green — same directory, so the two runs differ only in the file under test.
- Observed (kit friction): **the isolated-task shell guard refused a command whose text contained a directory change into the evidence directory** (`(cd "$scratch" && find …)`), reported as "a directory change it cannot verify", while the same listing through `find "$scratch" … | sed` ran. It is the third recorded shape of the same textual guard (absolute foreign paths, the phrase naming the primary checkout, and now a `cd`), and it is cheap to work around once you know it reads the command text.
- Regression/control: `node --test .xezar/checks/fragments.test.mjs` is 21/21 at the merged head. The two red proofs are stored with this run's evidence: the reviewed suite red on the post-fold tree (`red-proofs/01-major1-original-red.txt`, 19 pass / 1 fail, exit 1) and green after the fix on the SAME tree (`red-proofs/02-major1-corrected-green.txt`, 21/21, exit 0); the new repaired-branch case red against the reviewed per-commit-only rule (`red-proofs/03-major2-reviewed-rule-red.txt`, `1 !== 0` on `edited directly by …`) and green against the corrected rule (`red-proofs/04-major2-corrected-green.txt`). The merge-of-`main` guard case and the release-fold case stay green in every run. The new check against the merged branch itself exits 0 (`diff base e0c66b0f4bb5 — no direct edit of CHANGELOG.md`, `1 fragment(s) parsed`, `1 "# Unreleased" heading(s), in order`).
- Remaining limit: the repaired-branch rule is proven on fixtures, not by a real author repairing a real direct edit — #682 and #653 carry such a commit, and their own gate runs are what will exercise it. Minor 1 (the fold's fence-awareness) and Minor 2 (an unknown heading sorted above `## Highlights`) are untouched and filed as #684 and #685.

### 2026-09-19 — the #651 OpenCode attach check, `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed

- Input: issue #651 with its QA evidence rows (T3a–T3d, run `83428cc9`, 0.16.0 `a8b25a77`), the leader's written decision in the task brief (fix the code, not the guide; fail closed; reuse `checkSession()`; no UI redesign), base `main` `fd419584`. Deliverable: the attach-time check, its regression tests with red proofs, the guide sentence, and the two fragments.
- Observed: **the bug had a shape AGENTS.md § Changing a mechanism already names, and the rule that found every site was "grep the TYPE, not the field".** `checkSession()` existed, was correct, and was reachable from exactly one path. Grepping `OpenCodeReactionAdapter` across the packages — rather than `baseUrl` or `sessionId` — answered "how many attach sites are there" in one step: one construction site in `leader-delivery.ts`, and an MCP door that refuses OpenCode earlier with `client-needs-address` and never reaches it. Without that grep the MCP door would have looked like a second site needing a second check.
- Observed (kit friction, none): `phase-record.sh check` answered the readiness question before the workflow asked it, `counters init --none` was accepted first try, and the isolated-task guard did not fire once in this run — the primary checkout was only ever read by the kit's own checks.
- Observed: **the red proof cost five unrelated test edits, and that is the signal, not the noise.** Five existing `LeaderDelivery` cases attached OpenCode to a closed port (`http://127.0.0.1:1`) purely to get "a leader attached", plus one sweep in `leader-mcp-only.test.ts`. Every one of them was, in miniature, the defect under test: the suite had encoded "an unreachable address attaches fine" as a convenience. They were moved onto a real fake `opencode serve` (now `fakeOpenCodeSession` in the testkit, shared by both files), and the one case that is genuinely about a leader going away was reshaped to attach live and then stop the server — which is the scenario it always claimed to be.
- Regression/control: three refusal cases (`session-not-found`, `wrong-project`, unreachable) and a fourth (a refused second attach keeps the working leader) were run against the pre-fix modules restored from `HEAD` into a scratch copy — never `git stash`, whose stack is shared with every other worktree on this machine — and all four failed `expected true to be false`; the fifth case, the DEFAULT-path guard, passed against the pre-fix module too, which is what makes it a guard rather than a second red proof. The adapter-level case failed `TypeError: adapter.checkTarget is not a function`.
- Remaining limit: no live reproduction against two real cockpits. The original incident is evidenced by the issue's QA rows only; what this run reproduced is the code path and its refusals under an automated fake `opencode serve`. Fixture-tested, not live-verified.

### 2026-09-19 — #668 changelog and dogfooding fragments (the two append-only files stop conflicting), `feature-implementation` step `Implement`, `xezar-implementation`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: issue #668 and the leader brief's eight-point design; base `origin/main` `5b172942`; no product code, kit only. Deliverable: `changelog.d/` and `.xezar/docs/dogfooding.d/` fragments, a direct-`# Unreleased`-edit refusal and a fragment parse in `changelog-check.sh`, and a fold the `release` role runs.
- Observed: **a fold that is prose in a skill cannot be red-proven, so the mechanical half has to be executable.** The design asks `infra-tests.sh` to prove "the fold produces the expected section and removes the fragments" and "a dogfooding fragment is folded above the newest existing entry without touching it". Nothing in the kit could run the skill's prose, so the deterministic part moved into two small helpers (`changelog-fragments.mjs`, `dogfooding-fragments.mjs`) that the release skill invokes; the skill still classifies the PRs and writes the Highlights prose. The line between "role judgement" and "mechanical fold" is what made the requirement testable at all.
- Observed: **the refusal had to be defined by what the release fold does, not by the branch's name.** The release task runs on an ordinary `xez/<id8>` branch, so "non-release branch" cannot be read from the branch. The rule is instead about the section: a diff that leaves `# Unreleased` in place and changes its text is refused; a diff that removes the heading is the fold and is allowed. That also keeps the check independent of who is running it.
- Observed: **comparing base against HEAD made a plain merge of the base a false red.** The first version flagged any difference in the `# Unreleased` region, so a task that merged `main` (bringing another branch's Unreleased edit) was refused for an edit it never made. The guard now inspects only the branch's own `--first-parent --no-merges` commits plus the working tree against HEAD; the regression test is a `--no-ff` merge of a side branch that edited the section. This is the shape `security-scan.sh`'s empty change set already cost a re-run for: a check whose input includes somebody else's work must not attribute it to this branch.
- Observed: **the fold's create path appended at the end of the file when no `# Unreleased` heading was left.** The index guard that skipped line 0 excluded a changelog that starts with a dated heading, so the new section landed after every older one. Both defects were found in one self-review pass, before any commit, and each has its own case now.
- Observed: **the diff guard's one fail-open point needed its own test.** `--diff-base auto` cannot refuse when no base resolves (a shallow CI checkout has no `origin/main`), so it prints "the direct-edit rule was NOT checked" instead of passing quietly — and a case in `fragments.test.mjs` pins that exact line, per AGENTS.md's fail-open rule ("a fail-open helper needs a populated-input guarantee, or it lies"). The gate run always has a base (`GATE_BASE_SHA`), so the loud path is the bare-checkout one.
- Observed: **the tests that pin CHANGELOG.md pin prose, not the `# Unreleased` heading, and this change never moves that prose.** `mcp-api-doc.test.ts` has two cases (`documents every Channels eligibility condition…`, `documents the claude-code-channel-not-advertised recovery…`) that read `CHANGELOG.md` at the repo root and assert phrases; both pass unchanged, because the fragments are new files and the `# Unreleased` section is not touched by this pull request. They will also pass after a release folds the section, because the fold moves the bullets and never rewrites their text.
- Observed: **the change's own pull request uses the mechanism it adds** — `changelog.d/668.md` and this file — and the release role is the only thing that folds them. Nothing was appended to `CHANGELOG.md` or to the ledger by hand.
- Regression/control: `git stash push --include-untracked` of the three check files (reverting `changelog-check.sh`, removing both fragment CLIs) turned `node --test .xezar/checks/fragments.test.mjs` from `20 pass / 0 fail` to `0 pass / 20 fail`; the direct-edit case failed `2 !== 1` on `changelog-check: unknown argument --diff-base`, and the fold case on `Cannot find module …/changelog-fragments.mjs`. Restored, the focused suite is 20/20; `repository-checks.sh`, `catalog-check.mjs`, the 34-case kit contract, `tracked-files` and the two `mcp-api-doc` changelog cases are green. `infra-tests.sh` runs the suite and passed 649 cases / 0 failed.
- Observed (kit friction, first time recorded): **the isolated-task shell guard refused an absolute-path fixture command.** A `bash` call that created a nested git fixture under the task worktree with `git -C "$WT/.local/…"` and then ran `bash "$WT/.xezar/checks/…"` was refused as naming a path outside the worktree, while the same commands with worktree-relative paths ran; the guard reads the command text, and an absolute worktree path is indistinguishable from a foreign one to it.
- Remaining limit: the fold is proven on fixtures, not by an actual release task; the next release run is what exercises `changelog-fragments.mjs --fold` against the real CHANGELOG.md. The direct-edit refusal is enforced in the local gate (`repository-checks.sh` inside `repo-gates.sh`), which is where a task is stopped before its pull request exists; CI's `Xezar infrastructure fixtures` job runs it on a shallow checkout with no base, where it says so rather than checking.

### 2026-09-19 — #667 integration CI wait as a token-free check step, `feature-implementation` step `Implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Input: issue #667 with an accepted mechanism (split `integration.yaml` into merge → bounded check → report) and a brief naming the exit contract for the new check. Base `5b172942`. Kit-only; no engine change.
- Observed: **the brief's exit contract and the engine's check-step semantics could not both hold, and the conflict is invisible in the YAML.** The brief asked the watch script to exit non-zero on a red CI run. `run.ts` ends a run on a failed check step that has no `onFail`, and `onFail.retry` may only name an EARLIER step — every step earlier than the watch is earlier than the merge, so any retry would re-run the merge. A red-exits-non-zero script therefore means the report step never runs: no flake adjudication and no `XEZ:ASK` at the one moment integration needs a question. Resolved by making an OBSERVED terminal conclusion exit 0 (success, cancelled and failure alike, each recorded verbatim) and reserving non-zero for the states in which there is nothing to report — a missing or malformed target, an unobservable GitHub, and the deadline. The departure is stated in the script header, in the PR body and here rather than resolved silently.
- Observed: **naming what the old step was load-bearing FOR changed the design.** The single agent step was not just "the thing that waited": it kept an agent SESSION open for the whole wait, and a session is the only thing the leader's `execution_control` `send_message` can reach. A check step has none, so the rescue channel disappears during the wait. What replaces it is the hard bound — the script ends at 45 minutes and the report agent starts immediately — which is a different guarantee, weaker in kind and stronger in bound, and is the sentence the workflow header now carries.
- Observed (kit friction): a fixture helper that assigns globals must be called plainly, never as `$(helper …)`. Written the usual way — `wt="$(ciw_fixture success)"` — the subshell discarded the stub and evidence paths, every one of the sixteen new cases drove the previous case's fixture, and all sixteen failed identically for one reason that looked like sixteen. The rule is now written above the helper.
- Observed (tooling): `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` takes just over five minutes on this machine, so each red/green cycle of a fixture section costs a full suite run; debugging the script itself against a hand-built throwaway repository first was what kept that to two runs rather than six.
- Regression/control: sixteen new fixture cases (green, red-with-known-flake, red-with-real-failure, an empty failed-job list that must not read as "all flakes", cancelled-superseded, cancelled-unexplained, four malformed or missing targets, the deadline, an unobservable GitHub, and the workflow's own step order) proved red with `.xezar/checks/ci-watch.sh` stashed and green with it restored. The red-proof failures are quoted verbatim in the pull request body.
- Remaining limit: every case here is fixture-tested against a stubbed `gh`; no real integration run has used the split yet, so "the wait costs no tokens" is a property of the mechanism (`run.ts` spawns a check with no agent session) rather than a measured saving. The exit-contract departure above is a judgement this step made under its own authority and is flagged for the reviewer, not accepted.

### 2026-09-19 — #634 review response (seal the merge-refreshed head without changing content), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, pi (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) — real-task observed
- Input: the `## Code review` APPROVE on PR #634 (reviewed head `50f406d5`, branch `xez/d093a187`) with two Minor findings, both "no action needed", the second being the stale gate seal itself; `origin/main` `96af64b7`. Deliverable: no product change, a sealed full gate run at the head that ends up on the PR branch.
- Observed: **the brief's merge step was already satisfied by the base, so it fast-forwarded instead of creating a merge commit.** `git merge --no-edit 50f406d5` moved the task branch `96af64b7 → 50f406d5` ("Fast-forward", 2 files) because the PR head is itself a merge of the current `origin/main` (`96af64b7` is `50f406d5`'s second parent). No `MERGE_HEAD` existed, so the brief's "a plain `git commit` is allowed for the merge commit itself" fallback never applied, and the merge alone produced no new head — the run's own dogfooding commit is what makes the pushed head new. Kit friction (recommended): a review-response brief should say what to do when "bring the PR head into your own branch" is a fast-forward.
- Observed (kit friction, source reading): **`worktree-git.sh push` cannot deliver to the PR's own branch, which is the one write this workflow exists to perform.** Its refspec is fixed to the run's own branch (`refs/heads/$BRANCH:refs/heads/$BRANCH`), while the handoff skill tells a review-response run to use it "for a named task ref"; a delivery to `xez/d093a187` therefore needs a plain `git push origin HEAD:refs/heads/xez/d093a187`. Same shape as the recorded `worktree-git.sh commit` / `MERGE_HEAD` friction: the guard is right about the checkout and wrong about the ref.
- Regression/control: no product source changed. `git diff --stat origin/main...HEAD` is the PR's two files plus this entry, and `git diff 50f406d5 -- CHANGELOG.md packages/xezar/src/mcp/stale-write.test.ts` is empty, so criterion C1 (reviewed content unchanged) is proven by an empty diff rather than by re-reading the files. The canonical `repo-gates.sh --fast` run and its seal at the pushed head are the workflow's own `gates` and `evidence` steps.
- Remaining limit: the review's finding 2 (gate evidence freshness) is closed only when the seal's head equals the PR branch's live tip; that depends on the handoff step pushing `HEAD` to `xez/d093a187` rather than to the task branch, and on nothing else changing afterwards.

### 2026-09-18 — #644 model-routing document to version 3 (DeepSeek V4.1 Flash as a normal lane), `docs-maintenance` step `docs`, `xezar-docs-maintenance`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed

### 2026-09-18 — #636 review response round 1 (pi `--mcp-config` capability probe), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed
- Input: the code review on PR #636 head `32134c52` (REQUEST CHANGES, M1), a second-opinion review handed to the task through the brief rather than posted on the PR, the `## QA` comment for the same head (PASS, no findings, nothing to fold) and the leader's adjudicated list M-A/M-B/m-1..m-4; `origin/main` at `326a7ebe` merged in first.
- Observed: **a capability probe answered for the wrong process because nobody asked which process was going to run.** The probe was correct about the binary and the agent home and still wrong about the child, because pi also resolves an extension from the project folder. The general shape is worth naming: a probe that spawns a peer must be handed the SAME three inputs the real child gets (binary, env, cwd), not the two that were convenient. Re-derived here against real pi 0.85.1: the same binary answers `yes` (1029 ms) for the adapter-equipped agent home and `no` (159 ms) for an empty one.
- Observed: **removing a cache was cheaper than keeping one correct.** Once the answer depends on the folder, a folder-keyed cache is cold on the common case (one task per folder), and any expiry window is a window of exactly the two failures the review reported. Dropping it removed the invalidation problem instead of solving it, and paid 0.2–0.4 s at a session start that was already spawning a CLI.
- Observed: **making a synchronous spawn asynchronous broke 21 tests in three suites that the brief had recorded as deferrable hygiene.** The `vi.mock('node:child_process')` hooks in the pi suites match on nothing, so they intercepted the PROBE's spawn too, and the harnesses then drove a child that did not exist yet. A deferred-hygiene item can become load-bearing the moment the thing it touches changes shape; the honest move was to adapt those three suites in the same round and say so in the response comment rather than report the brief's framing unchanged. The durable fix in each suite is a spawn hook that signals when it fired, so a test waits for the spawn instead of assuming it already happened.
- Observed: **keeping the `AgentRunner` seam synchronous over an asynchronous spawn costs exactly one optional member.** A facade answers everything except `pid`, which feeds resource telemetry through a single registration site; `AgentSession.onProcessStart?` keeps that site single and also covers the runner's own one-shot restart, so telemetry never tracks a pid that is already gone. Two second-order effects only surfaced on self-review, not from the finding list: the doomed first attempt's opening `turn.started` would have been emitted twice, and a message handed to the facade during the probe window would have died with that attempt.
- Regression/control: the three source files stashed with a tagged `git stash push` → **10 of 29** isolation cases red (`expected undefined to be '…/task-folder'`, `expected [ [ '--mode', 'rpc', …(2) ] ] to have a length of 2 but got 1`, `You must provide a Promise to expect() when using .resolves, not 'boolean'`), the "extension present" guard and all 11 controls green both ways; restored → 64/64 across the three pi suites, 252 across the golden-fixture and protocol set, and `repo-gates.sh --fast` all eight commands passed (542 files / 11250 tests, unit 129, security `pass`).
- Remaining limit: the per-folder direction of the finding (a project-local adapter making the same pi answer `yes` in one folder and `no` in another) is taken from the reviewer's measurement; this round reproduced only the per-agent-home directions against the real binary, because proving the other one means installing an adapter into a project.

### 2026-09-18 — #590 review response round 3 (superseding PR #619), `address-review-findings` step `Address findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code — real-task observed
- Input: the owner-granted third round after a comprehensive research pass on a click race (`.local/xezar-tasks/9a994a7d-.../click-race-research.md`), PR #590's own round-2 scoped re-check finding, and the branch's `quality-repair` counter state; PR head `4110cda`, base `origin/main` at `4b955fbc` (17 commits behind).
- Observed: **a repair counter tracked per response round was never actually recorded by either predecessor round.** Response rounds 1 (`a86ab91f`) and 2 (`fcde3951`) each repaired the same review finding — exactly what the `quality-repair` counter exists to cap at two — but neither round called `phase-record.sh counter quality-repair` at the time; each round's own `COUNTERS` file independently declared "no predecessor run, starts at zero." Chaining `counters init --predecessor` from round 2 would therefore have silently under-counted to 0 of 2, letting a third repair proceed as if it were the first. The two rounds were still real and countable: their PR comment headers ("Response round 1"/"Response round 2") and the campaign log (`timeline-2026-09-17.md:342`, `README.md:23`, "#590 has spent round 2 of 2") independently confirm the count. Reconciling the `COUNTERS` file by hand from that outside evidence — rather than trusting the tool's own automatic predecessor chain — is what made the counter refuse correctly. A response-round workflow that wants this counter enforced automatically needs to call it at THE POINT a round is dispatched, not leave it to be reconstructed three rounds later.
- Observed: **the counter refusal is a decision fork, not a dead end, and the skill already named the other branch.** `phase-record.sh counter quality-repair` refusing (2 of 2 EXHAUSTED) does not mean "stop" here — the skill's own text anticipated exactly this outcome for a response-round task and named the fallback (a superseding PR from the task's own fresh branch, carrying the reviewed head plus the fix, original PR left open). Following that fork produced a real PR (#619) with a real gate run against its own pushed head, rather than either bypassing the counter or abandoning an owner-granted round.
- Observed: **the merge that carries a PR's approved head across 17 catch-up commits from main is where "keep both" needs to be judged per file, not applied uniformly.** Of 4 conflicts, `CHANGELOG.md` was a trivial keep-both (two independent bullets); `docs/testing/coverage-gaps.md` required re-ordering (main had rewritten several rows this branch's own row sat between); the add/add on `guide-browser.ts` needed every method from both sides read in full before writing a merged file, because neither side's diff alone showed which helper methods were genuinely independent (`isChecked`/`isDisabled`/`waitForRoleGone`/`hasPlaceholder` from main) versus which one this round's own fix would delete anyway (`waitForUncoveredRole`/`boxesOverlap`, superseded by the new `clickRoleWhenStable`).
- Regression/control: RED attempt (guard reverted, full file, no `-t`, capped at 6 runs per the brief) did NOT reproduce the race — reported honestly as a negative result rather than omitted, consistent with the reviewed failure rate being 2 of 3, not 3 of 3. GREEN: 12/12 across two fresh instances (6 same-instance, 6 after a clean reboot). `npm run typecheck` clean; `e2e-file-parallelism.test.ts` 2/2; `repo-gates.sh --fast` full 8-command canonical list passed against the real pushed head.
- Remaining limit: the exact JSON error shape agent-browser emits for a covered click was not captured live this round (the race did not reproduce), so `isCoveredClickError` matches the human-readable `covered by` phrase from a previously-recorded real occurrence (round 2's own scoped re-check) rather than a JSON field — documented in the code comment as evidence-based, not verified live this round.

### 2026-09-18 — #600 PR2 (the isolation edge, the source scan, the machine-shaped keys), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code (Opus 5) — real-task observed
- Input: the owner-accepted slicing spec for #600 § "PR2", the leader's answers to Q2 and Q3, and PR1 (#605) already merged; base `c86c7d68`, head `b6ff536`.
- Observed: **a source-text scan needs a stale-entry test as much as a new-call test.** The obvious half — "fail on a `homedir()` outside the allowlist" — leaves an allowlist that only grows: an entry whose call site was deleted silently pre-approves the next line that happens to match it. The second assertion, that every allowance was consumed, is what keeps the list an allowlist. Matching each entry on file AND exact source line (with a count for genuine duplicates) is what makes a changed call site re-justify itself rather than inherit an old reason.
- Observed: **"a `.xezar` literal" is the wrong pattern; "a `.xezar` PATH literal" is the right one.** A naive `\.xezar` match over stripped source produced eleven findings that were help text, a `[mcp_servers.xezar]` TOML key, `metadata?.xezar` and a package identifier. Requiring the literal to BEGIN a quoted string (`'.xezar'`, `'.xezar/skills'`) drops every one of them and still catches the spec's named break. A guard with a noisy default gets an allowlist full of noise, and then the allowlist means nothing.
- Observed: **the environment leak recorded on 2026-09-17 was a missing pin, and it is fixed.** `config-api.test.ts` pinned `HOME`, `XEZ_HOME`, `CODEX_HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` but not `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, which `agentHomePaths` reads FIRST — so any agent process carrying that variable (this one does) read the developer's real Claude settings and saw a `defaultModels.claude` no case wrote. Proven by re-running the file with the variable unset (17/17). One added pin, identical behaviour where the variable is unset.
- Observed (kit friction, not a defect): **`phase-record.sh check` validates criterion ids against `^(AC-|DP-)` only.** The accepted analysis numbers its criteria `SP-2.1…SP-2.6`, and the leader's brief names those ids, so the record had to write `AC-SP-2.1:` to satisfy the check while keeping the id a reader can trace. Two near misses cost a round each: `AC-SP-2.1 (issue DC-1):` fails, because the regex needs the colon immediately after the id. Worth considering whether the namespace should admit an analysis-assigned prefix, or whether the message should say what shape it wants.
- Observed: **a lazily resolved path is not a style preference in this feature.** The skills-update lock was first resolved in the service constructor. It happens to be built after the boot installs the layout, so the value would have been right today — and it is exactly the "path resolved at the wrong moment" bug the whole slice exists to prevent. Self-review caught it; no test would have.
- Regression/control: five named breaks, each red alone and restored — the scan (`join(homedir(), '.xezar', 'scratch')` in an unrelated module, red naming file and line), the skills cache (`bareDirFor` reverted), the semaphore clamp (`Math.min(committed, hostDerived)`), the kit check (`singleProjectMode` constant false, 5 of 6 new cases red), and the MCP socket dir. `bash .xezar/checks/infra-tests.sh` 602/602. Canonical gates green at head `b6ff536`, security stage PASS. Guard tests that pass both ways (SP-2.3, SP-2.5, the global-layout pins) are labelled as guards in the evidence rather than counted as proofs.

### 2026-09-18 — #453 batch B8 review response round 1 (PR #602), `address-review-findings` step `respond`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code — real-task observed
- Input: the three verdict comments on PR #602 (code review APPROVE, QA PASS, design review FAIL with one blocker) and the leader's dispositions of B-1, NB-1, NB-2 and NB-3; head `54541a8`, base `7b595bb`.
- Observed: **a source-string test can retire a ledger row against a live defect.** The only proof for G-10 was `expect(src).toContain('const returnFocus = useReturnFocus(confirming !== null)')`. It was green, the code review was green, and the behaviour was still broken on the surface the PR body itself named. What a string assertion cannot see is WHICH element the hook captured: with a Radix menu open, `document.activeElement` is the menu container, and the menu unmounts as the dialog opens, so the captured target was disconnected by close time and focus silently fell to `<body>`. A claim about focus is a claim about the rendered document; it needs a rendered assertion or it is not evidence.
- Observed: **a fix for "the opener is wrong" belongs at the site that KNOWS the opener.** The hook's `document.activeElement` capture is correct for its original site (a plain button that stays mounted) and cannot be made correct generically. Passing the opener explicitly from the control that opened the MENU leaves the desktop path untouched and needs one optional parameter.
- Observed: **#584's hit-test-and-wait recipe does not port to every control.** Ported into this spec to click the phone kebab, it timed out on two of three cases in one run: below `md` the run header is not sticky, the thread restores its own scroll position underneath it, and the fixed top bar covers what ends up at the top, so the control cannot always be brought fully clear. Opening the menu with the keyboard (focus the trigger, press Enter) needs no hit test, reproduces the defect identically — what breaks the hook is the open menu owning focus, however it was opened — and is the more honest journey for a keyboard-focus finding. Three consecutive green runs after the switch.
- Observed: **a red proof has to be re-taken when the TEST changes, not only when the source does.** The first red proof was taken against an earlier draft of the spec; the shipped spec was re-proven red against the same targeted break (2 failed / 19 passed, both the focus-return cases) before the fix was restored.
- Regression/control: targeted break = the pre-fix hook call, cockpit rebuilt each way. Red 2 failed / 19 passed with `focus landed on body instead of the kebab`; green 21/21 three times (17.7 s, 16.3 s, 23.5 s). `npm run typecheck` clean; the four unit suites 72/72.

### 2026-09-17 — #537 review response round 1 (PR #555), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code — fixture-tested, pi resolver parity observed

- Input: REQUEST CHANGES at `beb157a` with three Majors (file-tool path spellings, shell `cd`/`git -C` variants, primary root read from the `.git` file) and three Minors; the author run was out of quota, so this run answered on the same PR branch.

The reviewer's probe method – run the guard next to the installed pi's own `resolveToCwd` – found the real gap; a guard test that builds its own fixture paths could not. Two further gaps surfaced while fixing: Node's JS `realpathSync` keeps the spelled letter case on a case-insensitive disk (`realpathSync.native` does not), and the guard blocked the run's own handoff and temp folders because xezar places both under the primary checkout.
- Change: the guard now mirrors pi 0.85.1's path normalisation, uses the native realpath, receives the primary checkout and the run's granted folders from xezar as flags, and treats the shell check as best-effort with that limit written in the code, CHANGELOG and compatibility record.
- Regression/control: 33 new or changed focused tests failed against `beb157a` and pass with the change; the probe log shows six primary-checkout spellings that pi writes and the old guard allowed, all blocked now, with in-worktree and home-cache spellings still allowed.
- Remaining limit: the shell check cannot see variables set inside a command, command substitution, `eval` or scripts; the file-tool mirror is pinned to pi 0.85.1's resolver and must be re-checked when pi changes it. No live pi tool call with a model was run.

### 2026-09-16 — #446 (the 130 px progressive-history failure), `bug-fix` step `investigate`, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Codex — real-task observed

- Input: three unrelated PR heads failed the same live-tail assertion at exactly 130 px on GitHub's 2-core runners; #133/#177 established that repeated geometry must be measured rather than budgeted away. Base `c48fffe`, run `deb5deb4`.
- Observed: the fixed cost was a second parallel response, not a sticky header or transcript row. The newest history page paints first; compact current-state context later mounts the expanded Plan dock and collapsed Agents dock. A one-variable 500 ms delay on only `/history-context` grew the route dock from 156 px to 287 px (Plan 70.5 px, Agents 40.5 px) and left the actual viewport 130 px off-tail. The thread observer watched only the rows, so dock-only growth had no publisher to re-run `toBottom()`.
- Change: the same observer also watches the optional route dock. Its existing intent rule remains load-bearing: a stuck reader follows either growth source; an explicitly parked reader does not move. The e2e sampler still records every frame from the first destination row, but only declares settlement after this fixture's context-owned docks exist. A single delayed fetch makes the runner ordering deterministic without changing the 80 px product threshold.
- Regression/control: with only `observer.observe(dock)` removed, the deterministic spec failed under concurrent `npm run test:unit` pressure with `expected 130 to be less than 80`; restored, the same case passed repeatedly. The unit regression follows a pinned reader through 130 px of dock growth; its control proves an unpinned reader stays at 1,200 px.
- Lesson (recommended): when one shell scroller owns content and a sticky in-flow dock, observing only the transcript is not observing scroll geometry. Name every independently loading region that can change `scrollHeight`, then apply the existing reader-intent rule to its resize instead of teaching a test to wait past the defect.
- Remaining limit: the deterministic delay is fixture-only and verifies the exact historical ordering in real Chrome; GitHub CI on the draft PR remains the runner confirmation. Panel transcripts deliberately have no route dock and retain content-only observation.

### 2026-09-16 — #467 PR 3 (the CLI activity renderer), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code — real-task observed

- Input: the approved terminal design (#482, `designs/cli-terminal/`), the owner's spec § 11 AC-06…AC-13, and PR 2 (`cli-settings.ts`) already on main; base `c0ce42a`.
- Observed: **writing `\uXXXX` into a source file through a tool whose input is JSON silently produces the real control byte.** Every escape in a sanitizer's regexes, a renderer's ANSI constants and three test files arrived as literal ESC and C1 bytes, and the file then failed to parse at RUNTIME rather than at write time — `SyntaxError: Invalid regular expression` from the built JS, not from the editor. The fix that made it stop recurring was mechanical: after writing any file that mentions an escape, run one script that re-escapes C0/C1/DEL/U+2028/U+2029 and fails if any remain. Same trap in the edit direction: an `old_string` containing `\b` or `\u001b` is decoded before the match is attempted, so it never matches — those edits have to go through a script file, not an inline argument.
- Observed: **a red-proof harness that restores with `git restore` silently does nothing for a file the change ADDS.** The first full pass reported 16 of 17 breaks red; every one of those numbers was worthless, because the first case left a syntactically broken `sanitize.ts` on disk and every later case then failed for that reason instead of its own. `git restore` reads the index, and an untracked file is not in it. Restoring from the bytes held in memory, and asserting the file matches afterwards, is the only form that cannot lie. The corrected pass found one case genuinely green-either-way.
- Observed: **that one green-either-way case was the point of the whole exercise.** `duplicate-summary` deletes the "did the status actually move" guard, and the test still passed — because the `running` branch has a second guard of its own, so the one status the test exercised was correct by accident. Every ENDING status had no such guard, and for `failed` the deleted line was also the only thing between a token refresh and a second `countFailedTask()`. The test was widened to cover them; it is now red. A green-either-way test is not a small problem — here it was hiding an inflated failure count in the session summary.
- Observed: **a real pseudo-terminal found two defects that every fixture missed**, because both only exist at a width: the session summary was never fitted to the terminal (its counts line ran 12 columns past a 40-column edge), and the boot event reported `start=0` for a `--port 0` start, where nothing was asked for. Node has no pty, so the helper is 40 lines of Python (`packages/xezar/test/unit/pty-capture.py`); it sets the window size BEFORE the child execs and starts it in its own session, so the Ctrl-C it sends can only ever reach that one process group. The suite skips where python3 is absent rather than passing.
- Observed: **the gate run is what distinguishes a stale assertion from a regression, and the two looked identical at first.** Three tests failed on the same change: two pinned the pre-#467 wording of a warning the approved design replaces (stale, and updated), and the third had found a real hole — a plain output line carries the event name and its fields and NEVER the human message, so a warning whose whole content was in its message arrived saying nothing. That is a design property worth stating once: any line that must survive into a log file has to put its content in a field.
- Observed: **a scope boundary can be a string.** § 6.1's compact banner is part of the approved design and is not shipped, because the `cockpit → <url>` spelling is parsed by eight test files, two of them inside `packages/xezar/src/mcp/` — a directory this wave's coordination put off limits. The departure is written into the design's § 14 with its reason rather than left for the reviewer to discover.
- Remaining limit: fixture-tested and live-verified against the built CLI (captures at 80 and 40 real columns, piped, and quiet). **Not verified: human design QA and the design verdict**, which are the `needs-design` gate; and `npm audit` for the one added dependency, recorded as unknown.

### 2026-09-16 — PR #502 / #468 PR 5, REVIEW RESPONSE (`address-review-findings`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code) — real-task observed

- Input: the `## Code review` comment on PR #502 at head `3e713c5` (REQUEST CHANGES, one major, two minors, one nit). Author run `a73071e0` was already done and its worktree reclaimable, so the reclaimed-owner recipe applied: own branch off `origin/main`, `git merge` the PR head, work, then one named-ref push to `xez/a73071e0`.
- Observed: **a comment can assert a guarantee no line of the module delivers, and every test in the file can pass anyway.** `new-issue-dialog.tsx` carried *"The pre-fill is offered, never persisted"* above two effects — one seeding state from the search text, the next persisting that state unconditionally. There was no flag between them; `grep` for `touched`, `dirty` or `pristine` in the feature's two modules returned nothing. The existing AC-6 case asserted only that a brief SURVIVES, which is true of the bug and of the fix alike. This is exactly the class `AGENTS.md` § *Changing a mechanism that already works* names, and the only thing that separated the two halves was a test written for the sentence the comment made, not for the behaviour on screen.
- Lesson (recommended): **when one effect SEEDS state and a later effect PERSISTS it, the guard is an authorship flag, never a value comparison** — and the flag has to start `true` for state restored from the store, or the persist effect reads the restored draft as a seed and deletes it on mount. Clearing on close looks equivalent and is not: it loses the words of somebody who typed and then pressed Escape. Both wrong shapes were run as named breaks here and each turned a different one of the three new cases red.
- Observed: two of the three new cases were **green against the unfixed code**, so the red proof had to come from breaking the FIX rather than from the original defect — flag never raised (the edit case red), unconditional seeding that also marks touched (the untouched case and the stored-draft case red). Reporting which case is a proof and which is a control is the difference between three tests and one test plus two guards.
- Observed: the reviewer's own probe reproduced byte for byte in this checkout (`expected [ 'worktree lease' ] to deeply equal []`), which made the first case cheap to write and left no argument about whether the defect was real. A review that ships the failing assertion text is worth more to the responder than a paragraph of prose about it.
- Remaining limit: gates and the two named suites are green on the pushed head, but the browser legs stay unclaimed — `needs-qa` and `needs-design` were in flight against the pre-fix head while this ran, and the gate step of this workflow runs against the responder's own unchanged tree, which is a no-op confirmation and not a re-gate of the pushed content.

### 2026-09-16 — #469 P1 (the local development-process contract), `feature-implementation` step `implement`, `xezar-implementation`, Claude Code — real-task observed

- Input: the accepted analysis and owner decisions on #469 (three depth levels; code-security evidence whenever code or security capabilities apply; a capped author self-review beside the existing independent reviews). Documentation and shared skill prose only; base `52442a7`.
- Observed: **the shared skill tail is a 19-file byte-equality contract, so editing it by hand is the wrong tool.** `catalog-check.mjs` compares everything after `## Shared contract\n` for string equality across every maintained role, so one stray space in one file fails the catalog with a message that names the file rather than the drift. Editing all 19 through one Node script with anchor strings that must each match exactly once made the equality a consequence of the method instead of something to re-verify. Mixed punctuation is the trap: the tail uses ASCII apostrophes, and typographic ones inserted by drafting had to be normalized in a second scripted pass.
- Observed: **a contract page nearly overloaded an enforced record name.** The first draft gave the acceptance-criteria mapping the name `VERIFICATION`, which already means "this run verified an existing revision and made no commits" and is what lets readiness accept an empty branch (`xezar-testing`, `xezar-handoff-draft-pr`). Renaming it `AC_VERIFICATION` and listing `BLOCKED`, `DELIVERED` and `VERIFICATION` as the three records that already have teeth keeps a documentation PR from silently redefining a check's input. Lesson: before naming a record in prose, grep the kit for that name.
- Observed: **kit prose pins are narrower than they look.** `xezar-contract.test.mjs` pins the gate list against `.xezar/pipeline/config.json`, the 18/19 role counts, the `.xezar/docs/` file list and the shared-contract equality — not SDLC or CODE_REVIEW wording, which greps confirmed no test asserts. Checking that first turned "which strings am I allowed to change" from a worry into a two-minute answer.
- Observed: the depth record needed no `.xezar/pipeline/config.json` key: depth, maturity and counters are per-task facts, and that file travels to every checkout, so a key there would be the same false promise `maxParallel` already is.
- Remaining limit: adapted and fixture-tested only. This PR is prose; the executable security stage, the readiness/AC input checks and the durable counter validation are the sequenced follow-up, and nothing here has yet been exercised by a task that had to obey it.

That red was free evidence and is quoted as the count red-proof.
- Observed: **a new `xezar-*` skill is silently optional until it is listed in `MAINTAINED_SKILLS`.** Until then `catalog-check.mjs` treats it as a custom skill, so dropping its shared contract does not fail the catalog even though the shared tail is present. The name had to be added for the maintained-role negative control to go red, which is the honest shape: the list, not the prefix, is what makes a role maintained.
- Observed: **the repo's own tracker doc and the shared skill's GitHub mapping disagree about filing.** `.xezar/pipeline/trackers/github.md` `create-issue` interpolates the body into `--body "<body>"` and `search-issues` reads `--state open` only; the shared `references/trackers/github.md` requires `--body-file`, `--state all` and separate `--label` arguments. The wrapper states the precedence in its own text rather than editing the pipeline file, which a parallel task owns this wave.
- Observed: `task_create` defaults a **skill** source to autonomous when the project is configured `source-dependent` (`packages/xezar/src/mcp/tools/task-create.ts`). That is exactly the case the upstream IF-07 row calls out, so the wrapper repeats explicitly that a `task_create` skill source is not a filing grant.

Every surface now names `POST /api/v1/p/<projectId>/mcp/leader` with `project.id` from `discover_project`, and says OpenCode also sends `baseUrl` and `sessionId`. Red-first: with `bridge.ts`, `leader-delivery.ts` and `leader-events.ts` reverted to `ae4b0e8`, 3 pins failed. ### 2026-09-13 — PR #404 merged with #403 (Claude Code Channels wake joined with the Codex app-server attach), `address-review-findings` step `address`, `xezar-review-response`, Claude Code — real-task verified

- Evidence: run e15fd875, own branch `xez/e15fd875` on `main` = `cc39b8e`, merge commit `3a227b8` (parents `cc39b8e`, `38032d4`), pushed to the PR branch `xez/bf8ac01c`. Gate log, MCP coverage summary and the conflict table in the primary evidence directory under `address/` and `gates/`.
- Observed: **`worktree-git.sh commit` cannot conclude a merge.** Its strict preflight fails `gitstate.clean` while `MERGE_HEAD` exists, which is exactly the state a resolved conflict leaves behind. The only commit the guard refuses is the one the merge needs. The merge commit was made with plain `git commit` in the worktree the same preflight had just verified (right CWD, right branch); every later commit went through the guard. Lesson (recommended): a review-response task that merges `origin/main` into a PR branch should expect this and record the plain commit in the ownership-migration note. Candidate kit change: let `gitstate.clean` admit `MERGE_HEAD` when the index has no unmerged paths, or give `worktree-git.sh` a `merge-continue` verb; not changed here (outside this task's brief).
- Update (2026-09-15): the merge-continue proposal is recorded as deferred in `enhancement-ideas.md`; no Git guard changes are part of #447.
- Observed: **the integrator table in the other PR's body was worth more than `git merge-tree`.** #403's "Merging with #404" table named the nine files, the rule for each ("union", "flip `LEADER_CLIENTS['claude-code']` to `direct`", "keep every `#changed()` call") and one file that is NOT a conflict but must change anyway. All nine matched the real conflict list, and the not-a-conflict file (`mcp-leader-control.tsx`) is where the merge would have quietly shipped a control that still called Claude Code pull-only. Lesson (recommended): a PR that knows it conflicts with a sibling should write that table; the merger reads it before the first hunk.
- Observed: **one hidden collision only the typecheck found.** Both branches added a `withCode` helper — #403 as an export of `mcp-copy.ts`, #404 as a local function in the section; git merged both without a conflict marker and `tsc` reported the duplicate. Lesson (real-task verified): after a conflict-free auto-merge of a file both sides edited, run the typecheck before the tests — it is the cheapest check that sees the whole program.
