### 2026-09-11 — the empty-branch refusal fired on an honest QA run, repaired in PR #322's handoff step at the owner's request, Claude Code — fixture-tested; `VERIFICATION` is real-task verified by `1176f24c` (update 2026-09-15)

- Evidence: run c5a99f15 (`testing-and-verification`, QA re-verification of PR #311 at `e175140`). Readiness gave two refusals: `scope.not-blocked` (it had written `BLOCKED` for an owner decision – correct) and `branch.has-own-commits` (its branch `xez/c5a99f15` was the base commit `4c179f0` – wrong: QA of another branch makes no commit by design). Both refusals came from the kit on `main` (#315 and earlier), not from PR #322.
- Observed: **git and the workflow cannot tell a QA run from a test-writing run that wrote nothing.** Both use `testing-and-verification`; both reach readiness with HEAD in the base and a clean tree; check steps get no task brief (`lib/common.sh`: the engine exports nothing task-specific to a check step). The #312 entry's scope check ("all end in a draft PR or a release") missed that one workflow has two uses.
- Change: the task declares it. A `VERIFICATION` file in the primary evidence directory with `verified: <40-hex sha>` (must resolve to a commit) and `findings: <where>` lets the empty-branch check pass in readiness and both evidence modes. Absent: the refusal is unchanged. Malformed: `scope.verification-record`. `BLOCKED` is checked first. The testing skill says when to write it and when never to; the handoff skill says a verification-only run pushes nothing and opens no PR.
- Regression/control: with the old `worktree-preflight.sh`, the new section was 8 red out of 10 (490 passed, 8 failed overall); "no record is still refused" and "BLOCKED still stops it" passed both ways. With the change: 498 passed, 0 failed.
- Remaining limit: the record is the agent's word, the same trust `BLOCKED` already relies on; a test-writing run that wrote nothing and then wrote the record would pass. The `verified:` sha must exist, which stops a bare switch but not a false claim. A verification run still pays for a gate run on its own head, which is the base.
- Update (2026-09-15): run `1176f24c` completed readiness, gates and evidence with no own commits. Its recorded `check-output` says “a verification-only run, by its VERIFICATION record”; the run’s `dogfooding-handoff.md` confirms evidence verification exited 0.

### 2026-09-11 — #317 and #320 (the workflow proceeds when it should have stopped), `bug-fix` step 1, `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Code — fixture-tested; #317's stop is real-task verified, #320 remains unqualified (update 2026-09-15)

- Evidence: run b86c6066's implement step. The cockpit thread reads as a prose question, but the Claude session transcript shows the turn ended on an `XEZ:ASK` line: the engine strips the marker from the thread and, in a non-final step, never parsed it. The #312 entry below and this task's brief both called it "prose, no `XEZ:ASK`"; the transcript is the better source.
- Observed: **the agent contract already separates "finished" from "waiting".** Every agent step is told to end with `XEZ:DONE` when its goal is achieved and to end plainly only when waiting on the user. Measured over this machine's history (`runs.json` + Claude and pi session transcripts, 75 non-final agent steps with a transcript): all 68 that really finished ended with `XEZ:DONE` (66 Claude, 2 pi). The other 7 were an `XEZ:ASK` (2, b86c6066 among them), an `XEZ:MONITORING` step still waiting on its own review (1), a turn that stopped mid-work (1), a turn waiting on a background review with no marker (1), one cancelled and this task's own running step. Every one of the 5 finished-but-not-done cases had been marked `done`. Codex and OpenCode had no non-final steps in the history, so their compliance is unknown.
- Change (#317): `runAgentStep` returns `unfinishedStepReason(lastTurnText)` for a non-final step, so a step whose last finished turn does not end with `XEZ:DONE` fails with a message naming the marker it did end on, and the run stops before readiness. The last (interactive) step is untouched. No flag.
- Change (#320): `--readiness` refuses a dirty tree (`gitstate.committed`) with the sealer's own predicate, `task_tree_is_dirty`, and says to commit and re-run. Evidence: 4ac055cb's refused seal was one uncommitted `.xezar/docs/dogfooding.md` edit, which the engine's finalize autosave committed afterwards; cc0a24f2 and cecc8f86 were refused the same way.
- Regression/control (#317): with `run.ts` reverted to `HEAD`, the new test's three stop cases were red (`expected 'done' to be 'failed'`); the `XEZ:DONE` control and the last-step-still-waits guard passed both ways. Seven older tests assumed a non-final dry-run step with no marker was done (six with the mock, one with a fake runner that ended no turn); each now ends its step with `XEZ:DONE`.
- Regression/control (#320): with the old `worktree-preflight.sh`, the infra fixtures were 484 passed, 4 failed – exactly the four new refusal cases; the plain-preflight, read-only-role and committed-tree controls passed both ways. With the fix: 488 passed, 0 failed.
- Observed: the red proofs swapped the one source file for its `HEAD` copy and back (`cp`, `git show HEAD:<file>`), not `git stash`: the stash stack is shared by every worktree and peer sessions use it.
- Remaining limit: the engine change reaches a task only through a cockpit built from this commit; a cockpit already running keeps the old step loop. A stopped step fails rather than waiting, so answering it means Continue and then finishing the remaining stages by hand (`resume-complete.sh`). A step that ends with `XEZ:DONE` while unfinished still passes; that is the agent misreporting, not the engine guessing.
- Update (2026-09-15): run `7832ffe3` has a persisted failed `investigate` step with the error “the agent ended its turn without the XEZ:DONE completion marker”. The run later completed through Continue; that does not erase the observed stop. #320’s real readiness refusal is still not established here.

### 2026-09-11 — #307 (five false test signals), `bug-fix` workflow — real-task verified

- Observed: **both gate repairs were spent on the task's own scratch tests, not on any fix.** Flake reproduction wants probes that run under the real vitest config, so they sat in the tree as `*-scratch.test.*`. Attempt 0001 failed 47 tests, all scratch; attempt 0002 failed typecheck and 45 tests on one scratch file that an interrupted proof script had copied back in, and that `git add` had staged. The real suite was green both times.
- Lesson (recommended): a reproduction probe that must live under `packages/*/src` is copied in by the script that runs it and removed by that same script on the next line, never left for a later cleanup. Check `git status --short` for `scratch` before ending an investigate step, and commit the fix before starting a long proof run, so an interrupted run can only leave a dirty tree, never a committed probe.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine; the kit has no guard that refuses a `*-scratch.test.*` file, and adding one is a separate decision.
- Update (2026-09-15): the scratch-test guard proposal is filed as #313.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #250, `feature-implementation` workflow, implement step (`xezar-implementation`) — fixture-tested, real-task observed

- Goal: make N-03 stale-write rejection reachable through MCP (A-13). The design was decided by the leader before the task (route-side compare-and-apply on an OPTIONAL `expectedVersion`, REQUIRED in the MCP tools); the step implemented it. Base `b404e44`, then `269fd79` (#247 composition) merged mid-task. Node 24.20 / npm 11, macOS.
- Observed: **the cockpit typecheck reads the SERVER's route table from `dist`**, through `@qodeca/xezar/app-type`. Running `npm run typecheck -w @qodeca/xezar-web` right after a route change checked the OLD table and passed; only `npm run build:server` first makes it check the change. `npm run typecheck` at the root does this through `pretypecheck`, a workspace-only run does not. Lesson (recommended): after changing a route's request type, rebuild the server before believing a cockpit typecheck.
- Observed: Hono makes a validated `json` a REQUIRED client argument, so adding an optional field to a route that took no body would have turned every bodyless cockpit call into a compile error. A typed-optional variant of the JSON validator keeps the wire and the runtime unchanged. Worth knowing before any "just add an optional field" change to a bodyless route.
- Observed: **a red-proof run can silently test the fix.** Under zsh an unquoted `$FILES` does not word-split, so the copy-aside loop swapped nothing and the "red" run was green. It was caught only because the result looked wrong. The working shape is a bash array, plus printing how many files differ from HEAD after the swap, before trusting the run. Lesson (recommended): a red proof must show that the swap happened, not only that the tests ran.
- Observed: `it.each` with a `%s`/`%o` title over objects that hold MCP tools (zod schemas) crashed the vitest worker while formatting the title. Name the cases and use `$name`.
- Remaining risk, reported rather than changed (the design was not re-opened): the version's `seq` half moves with every persisted agent event, so a RUNNING task's version moves while its agent works. A leader's `execution_control` on a running task (cancel, send_message) is refused whenever an event lands between its read and its write. N-03 still holds — this over-rejects, it never lets a stale write through — but whether a stop action should behave differently is an owner decision. Measured, not inferred: the #248 parity case P-13 (cancel a running dry-run task, version read from the store microseconds before the call) failed 1 run in 3 with the cancel refused as stale; with a re-read-and-retry loop it passed 5 of 5. A real leader's read-to-write gap is seconds, not microseconds.
- Regression/control: 104 of 122 new tests are red against `origin/main`'s sources (copies restored and compared byte for byte afterwards); 18 pass both ways and are named controls (unchanged composition cases, fresh-token-goes-through cases, and the two "takes no version" cases).
- Observed (environment, not this change): one `infra-tests.sh` run failed "a registered worktree outside .local/xezar/worktrees is refused" although the captured message DID contain the needle; the log showed `printf: write error: Broken pipe`. `expect_fail` pipes the output into `grep -qF` under `set -o pipefail`, so an early grep exit can turn a match into a reported miss. The rerun passed 463/463. Filed as a follow-up rather than fixed here.
- Observed (conflict loop, after the draft PR): **a contract change that makes an argument REQUIRED breaks every existing caller, including tests that merged while the task was in flight.** `packages/web/e2e/mcp-collaboration.e2e.ts` (#257) landed on `main` mid-task and called `organise_work` / `execution_control` without `expectedVersion`. The local gate passed because the browser suite is not in it; CI's `Cockpit browser e2e` went red (B-01, B-03, B-02 as a cascade). Change: after every merge of `main`, grep for callers of each tightened action (`rg "'organise_work'|'execution_control'|'handoff_git'|remove_worktree" packages`) BEFORE gating, and run the browser specs that call them — not after CI reports it.
- Observed: `worktree-git.sh commit` refuses to finish a conflicted `git merge` (`gitstate.clean`, MERGE_HEAD), and `merge-recovery.sh` admits one only when `record-intent` ran BEFORE the merge. The conflict-loop brief says "git merge" without that step, so the resolved merge was committed with plain `git commit --no-edit` in the task's own worktree. A brief that asks for a merge should say to record the intent first.
- Observed (environment, not this change): `project-config.test.ts` "automations … project B sees none of it" fails its teardown with `ENOTEMPTY` on the temporary `HOME`: 5 of 10 runs on `main`'s sources, so it predates #250. It failed one gate attempt here. Filed as #260 rather than fixed here.
- Remaining limit: fixture-level and in-process bridge only — no real model client drove a stale write in this task. Async effects (git commit/push, draft PR, worktree removal) are checked right before they START; a change landing while one runs is outside what a version check can see.

### 2026-09-11 — issues #251 and #252, `feature-implementation` step 1 (Implement), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: a leader read/ack MCP tool over the existing `reconnect.ts`, and E-05 rows for human config, workflow and agent-config writes. Base `b404e44` plus a local merge of the still-open composition PR #247.
- Observed: the brief said to wait for #247 on `main`. Waiting idle was not needed: merging the PR head (`git fetch origin pull/247/head`) into the task branch let development and focused tests proceed, and the later squash of #247 into `main` merges cleanly on top because both sides carry identical content. The PR is opened only after `main` has #247, so no reviewer sees #247's diff inside this one.
- Observed: the red proof without `git stash` works as the brief describes — copy the changed source files aside, `git checkout HEAD -- <files>`, keep the NEW files so the test still loads, run, copy back. Keeping the new files turns "import error" into real assertion failures (4 red, 1 guard green).
- Observed: a new tool can break a merged acceptance suite by name alone. `acceptance-isolation.test.ts` A-04 rejected any tool name matching `/event/`; `leader_events` is project-scoped, so the assertion was narrowed with the reason and the isolation proof was added with two composed projects. Grep acceptance suites for tool-name regexes before naming a tool.
- Remaining limit: the MCP composition runs only for the boot project, so edits in other projects of the same cockpit write no E-05 row; no live MCP client read the tool — the tests drive the real bridge and socket.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #276, `feature-implementation` step 1 (Implement), Claude Code — adapted / fixture-tested

- Goal: give the kit a research role (`research` workflow + `xezar-research`) and a UX design role (`xezar-ux-design`, no workflow), after #274 showed a spec that reasoned only from the repository. Base `6f699bf`, Node 24.20 / npm 11.19, macOS.

A research brief sent to any existing role would have come back repository-only without saying so. Change: the `research` step lists both tools, and the skill's first line must state whether web access actually worked.

- Observed: `xezar-contract.test.mjs` pins the exact role counts (14 workflows, 16 skills, 17 guidance surfaces), so adding a role forces a check edit; `catalog-check.mjs`'s `MAINTAINED_SKILLS` also had to learn both names, or a new role could silently drop the shared contract. Both edits are the checks working, not scope creep.
- Observed: the shared contract was copied programmatically from an existing skill rather than retyped; byte-identity is what the catalog asserts, and retyping is how it drifts.
- Regression/control: 5 new contract cases. Without the `MAINTAINED_SKILLS` entries, the two "dropping its shared contract fails" cases are red (22 pass / 2 fail); without the new files, all 5 new cases plus the three count cases are red (16 / 8). With the change, 24 / 24.
- Remaining limit: neither role has run in a real task. Unknown: whether a real model keeps the citation discipline, whether pi accepts `websearch`/`webfetch` in `--tools`, and whether planning/implementation agents read `xezar-ux-design` unprompted, since no existing workflow names it. Owner/next trigger: the first real `research` run, e.g. the #274 prior-art question.
- Update (2026-09-15): the research trigger fired in `94a0f114` on 2026-09-11. The `design` and `design-review` workflows now name `xezar-ux-design` (#389); authoring remains unqualified.
- Observed: the first full gate run failed only on `todos.test.ts` › "unsubscribe stops delivery…", a known flake (#204) in a file this kit-only diff does not touch. Run alone it failed 1 of 3 times, so load is not required to trigger it. Repair was a re-run on a rebased head, not a test edit: a source/test change belongs to #204, not to this task.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #271 (item 1 also #240), `bug-fix` workflow, investigate step (`xezar-bug-investigation`), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: verify three findings a triage of the task inbox raised against the MCP tools, then fix the real ones with red-without-fix proof. Base `ef4b768`; all three were confirmed in source before any fix.
- Observed: a finding's file list is a hypothesis, not a scope. The `pick_variant` guard could not be fixed in the tool alone: the route `POST /groups/:groupId/pick` took no version at all, so the fix also moved its request shape into `packages/contract` and added the check in `server.ts`.
- Observed: the red proof by "copy aside, check out `origin/main`, restore" silently did nothing the first time. In zsh an unquoted `$LIST` does not word-split, so `cp` and `git show` received one bogus path, nothing was swapped, and the "without fix" run was green. Only a post-check caught it. Run the swap as a bash script with an array, and print the swapped files' diff before trusting any red or green.
- Observed: making a lenient schema strict turned two existing tests red that had pinned the lenient behaviour on purpose (A-02 "the one lenient schema", F-04 "extra keys do not reach the call"). Those are intended behaviour changes, not regressions; say so in the PR rather than only updating the expectation.
- Red/green: 13 tests in 5 files fail on `origin/main` sources and pass with the fix. Controls that pass both ways: the route's "current token goes through" and "no token is the cockpit", and the tool's "goes through on a fresh read". Logs in the task's primary evidence directory (`red-proof/`).
- Remaining limit: the pick's version covers the KEPT variant only. A human change to another variant after the leader's read (a rename, a pin) is not detected; only an active variant is refused, by the cockpit's all-terminal precondition the tool mirrors.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #262, `feature-implementation` workflow, implement step — real-task verified

- Goal: close four MCP leader gaps in one task (draft PR → ready, base-branch reachability, F-22 quality-gate removal, D-04 connection file). Code base `ef4b768`, branch `xez/b9b4feb6`, Node 24.20, macOS.
- Observed: the red-proof step in a zsh agent shell silently did nothing the first time. `FILES="a b c"; git checkout origin/main -- $FILES` does not word-split in zsh, so `cp` and `git checkout` both failed on one long path, and the "red" run was green because nothing was swapped. Run the swap under `bash` with an array (or check `git diff --stat` after the swap) before trusting a red result.
- Observed: the brief said a test file needed changing to turn A-01 green. It did not: the #118 harness judges the product, so writing the file in the product was enough. Reading the harness's verdict logic before editing it avoided touching a file another task owns.
- Observed: a real forge path (`gh pr ready`) can be driven through the whole composed service without GitHub by putting a small `gh` stand-in first on `PATH` with its state in a JSON file (`packages/xezar/test/helpers/fake-gh.mjs`). The dry-run forge could not show a draft becoming ready, because it reports every pull request as already ready.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #261, `docs-maintenance` step 1 (Update docs), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: a generated MCP API reference (`docs/features/mcp-server/mcp-api.md` + `mcp-api.json`) and a record-to-tool traceability table, both held to the real registry by `npm test`. Base `ef4b768`. Node 24 / npm 11, macOS.
- Observed: **a brief's findings are claims, and two of this one's did not hold.** It counted 8 registered tools (there are 10, plus `health`) and named "the MCP section of AGENTS.md's task-routing table", which did not exist; a row was added instead. Checking every stated fact against the code before designing cost minutes and changed the deliverable. Lesson (recommended): a leader brief built from its own investigation should be verified line by line, and the corrections go in the PR body.
- Observed: vitest's `toMatchFileSnapshot` is a clean regenerate path for a committed generated document — `-u` rewrites it, and a normal run compares byte for byte — **but a missing file is written, not failed, outside CI.** The test asserts the file exists before the snapshot compare, so deleting the reference fails locally too. Whole-page snapshots of a page with hand-written prose stay safe because the test rebuilds only the marker-delimited blocks and keeps the rest verbatim.
- Observed: documenting the result side surfaced defects that neither the per-tool suites nor the parity map could see, because each tool is consistent with itself: only `task_create` takes an `operationId` although D-06 § 5.2 requires one on every mutating tool (#264), `organise_work` is the only non-strict input schema (#265), and only the `mcp` audit origin is ever written (#266). A cross-tool reference is a review instrument, not just documentation.
- Regression/control: a scratch change to one tool's description turned the JSON and page checks red (2 of 6); removing one mapping line turned the page, forward and reverse checks red (3 of 6); both restored to 6/6.
- Remaining limit: the result vocabularies are hand-maintained — no tool declares an output schema, so nothing regenerates them; they were verified against source at `ef4b768` only.

The method (a source table with ids and read dates, "not found" as a required finding, fetched-page instructions reported and not followed) was improvised from the brief. Treat it as a first attempt, not as a recommendation.

The only entry left in the test's temporary `HOME` was `.local/state/gh/device-id`. A logging `gh` wrapper showed the real app starting background `gh repo view` and `gh auth token` probes from ordinary tests, none awaited. The brief forbade test changes, so the trace went to #260 and the branch goes back to the same unchanged gate — nothing skipped or weakened. Lesson (recommended): read the leftover directory before theorising about an `ENOTEMPTY` teardown; it names the writer.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #283, `docs-maintenance` step 1 (Update docs), Claude Code — real-task observed

- Goal: open-source readiness documents (`SECURITY.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`, issue forms, pull-request template, README badge and status note). Base `a339671`. Node 24 / npm 11, macOS.

Stage ownership keeps the pull request out of step 1, so the conflict is passed to the handoff step in the resume notes rather than resolved here. A brief that names a pull-request state should be checked against the workflow's last step before it is issued.

Two brief-level claims needed narrowing against the code: "path escapes out of a task worktree" is a boundary for xezar's own file-serving routes only, because an agent's shell is not confined to its worktree; and "project scope" is a hard boundary for MCP sessions, while the cockpit legitimately drives every registered project.

- Observed (zsh, a sibling of the word-split trap above): an unquoted `--include=*.ts` in a zsh shell is a glob, and with no match zsh aborts the command with `no matches found` instead of passing the pattern to `grep`. Quote globs meant for another program.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #284, `feature-implementation` workflow, implement step (`xezar-implementation`), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: the browsable, read-only half of the MCP API reference (spec `mcp-api-reference-spec.md` § 12, § 18): one project-scoped route whose `tools` is exactly `tools/list`, and Settings → MCP API. No "Try it".
- Observed: **the brief and the spec disagreed on scope, and on placement.** The brief owned four files and said "inside the MCP connection section"; § 18 needs a shipped coverage declaration (coverage rows, outcome labels, per-action read/change) that no file holds today, and § 12.1 puts the page in its own section beside MCP connection. Moving the test-only `api-coverage.testkit.ts` (R-04) would have broken two links in `mcp-api.md`, which the brief forbade editing. The task built everything the running code can state, left the declaration-dependent parts out, and says so in the PR. Lesson (recommended): a UI brief that cites a spec should name which spec sections are in scope, and the kit's planning role should check owned files against what the cited design needs.
- Observed: the red proof without `git stash` worked cleanly as: commit first, then `git checkout origin/main -- <modified files>` plus `git mv <new file> <new file>.aside`, run, then `git mv` back and `git checkout HEAD -- …`. Git restores everything; no `rm`. A `bash -c` script and any command containing `rm -rf` were refused by this machine's permission layer, and zsh does not word-split `$VAR` lists, so a first attempt silently did nothing. Lesson: write file lists out literally in zsh.
- Observed: the shared `chrome-devtools` browser was held by another session; the repo's own `agent-browser` binary (from `.local/qa/test-env.json`) gave an isolated session instead. **The MCP service never starts in a cockpit booted from a task worktree**: a task worktree is never registered as a project (`shouldRegisterProject`), so there is no boot project id and `startMcpSocket` is never called — no socket, no connection file, and no log line saying so (a short `XEZ_HOME` did not change it; the `test-env-up.sh` home is also past the 104-byte socket limit). Useful for "MCP not running" QA; misleading for anything that expects MCP to be up. For "MCP running" QA, boot the built CLI against a throwaway `git init` repo under `/tmp` with a short `XEZ_HOME`: the socket and `mcp-connection.json` then appear.
- Remaining limit: find-in-page opening a collapsed `<details>` was not driven (agent-browser has no find-in-page command); the fragment link and keyboard open were.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #288, `bug-fix` workflow, investigate step (Claude) — real-task verified
- Goal: check the #267 item 1 claim that the cockpit's routes skip the MCP ownership guard, then guard only where it is really missing. Base `a339671`, branch `xez/5f5de3f7`, macOS, Node 24.
- Observed: the brief's premise was half right. No route let a caller NAME another project's id: every project route resolves ids in the bound project's own store. What was really broken was a record that already names another project's worktree, from a copied `.local/xezar`. Pick and reclaim deleted that worktree, and the group read ran `git add -N .` in it. A reproduction that kept the two questions apart (guards that pass both ways, regressions that fail without the fix) is what showed this. A test that only checked "404 for a foreign id" would have passed on the buggy code.
- Observed: the task's TMPDIR is inside the repo, so it is already a real path. Test fixtures that build worktree paths from a plain `mkdtemp` root therefore pass in a task but fail on a developer Mac, where `/var` is a symlink to `/private/var`. Rerun ownership-sensitive suites with `TMPDIR=$(getconf DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR)`. The fixtures now `realpathSync` their roots.
- Observed: proving red without the fix by copying the three source files aside and writing `git show origin/main:<file>` over them worked without touching the shared stash. The same 3 cases failed and the 3 guards passed, and the restored diff matched.
- Observed: my focused run covered every vitest file that names reclaim, but not `test/unit/mcp-isolation.test.ts`, a `node:test` file that `npm test` never loads. Its "control" still expected the unguarded sweep to delete B's worktree, and only the full gate caught it (repair return 1 of 2). When a change touches the behaviour a control pins, search `packages/xezar/test/` as well as `src/`.
- Observed (handoff step, then a conflict round): **main moved between the gates step and the handoff step.** #289 merged about ten minutes after this branch took `origin/main`, and both appended an entry at the end of this file. The PR became `CONFLICTING`, and GitHub then ran no CI at all on it (`gh pr checks`: no checks reported). The handoff role makes no commits, so the conflict waited for a separate refresh round that merged `origin/main`, kept both entries, and ran the full gate again. Lesson (candidate): an append-only shared log gets a conflict from every pair of parallel tasks that touch it.
- Update (2026-09-15, #447 owner decision D3): retain one `dogfooding.md`, sorted newest-first; archive/split work is deferred in `enhancement-ideas.md`.
- Observed: a live self-QA of engine-only behaviour was cheap. It used the built CLI with `XEZ_DRY_RUN=1`, a pinned `XEZ_HOME`, `TMPDIR=/tmp`, and a small `node --import tsx` seed through the real `RunStore`. With one proven worktree and `worktreeRetention: 1`, a manual reclaim correctly returns `[]`. A real task had to start and finish before the task-end sweep showed real reclaim work. Read an empty result against the keep-limit before calling it a regression.
- Remaining limit: unit and route level only, with no UI smoke. The same unproved-path pattern is still open in `DELETE /runs/:id`, `remove-worktree`, `git/commit`, `git/push`, `/pr`, `/diff` and `/changes`; see #288.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #285, `bug-fix` workflow, investigate step (`xezar-bug-investigation`), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: stop one resumed task from holding its agent account's whole queue after a usage limit, without removing the hold that stops the stampede. Base `a339671`, branch `xez/8232dcda`. Node 24 / npm 11, macOS.
- Observed: **the issue's suggested proof point was wrong, and the source said so.** It proposed treating a resumed session's first streamed event as proof that the limit lifted. A refused Claude turn streams its session init and a synthetic assistant frame before the `is_error` result, so any "it said something" signal is true for the doomed turn too. The fix uses the one backend-neutral fact instead: the resumed turn is still running two minutes after its step started (a refused turn lives about 200 ms). Check a brief's proposed signal against what the failing case actually emits.
- Observed: the red proof by file swap (`git show origin/main:<file> > <file>`, run, restore from a copy in the evidence directory) works with no shared stash and no risk to peer worktrees. It is the cleaner form of the #18 and #22 protocol when the fix is in one source file.
- Observed: one end-to-end case can hold both a guard and a proof. Its "still held" assertions passed on `origin/main` and only its "queue moves" poll went red; reporting which assertion is which kept the claim honest.
- Remaining limit: the two-minute proof window is a judgement, not a measurement against a real provider; no real limited account was exercised.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #286, `bug-fix` workflow, investigate step (`xezar-bug-investigation`), Claude Code — real-task observed, fixture-tested

- Goal: confirm or refute "a task worktree without `node_modules` resolves `@qodeca/xezar-contract` from the primary checkout", and only if confirmed make the gate fail loudly.
- Observed (reproduced): in a scratch worktree nested in the primary checkout with no `node_modules`, `npm test` ran the primary's `vitest` (npm puts every ancestor's `node_modules/.bin` on `PATH`) and a test importing `@qodeca/xezar-contract` loaded `/…/xezar/packages/contract/package.json` — the primary's source — so an export added only on the branch read `undefined`. Node ESM and CJS resolution gave the same primary path.
- Observed: the canonical gate was already protected in the ordinary case: `deps_are_fresh` needs `node_modules` and its stamp, so `--fast` declines and runs `npm ci`. The gap was a tree that keeps its stamp but lost a workspace link, and every command run before setup.
- Change: `deps_resolve_in_task` (lib/common.sh) requires each workspace package's `node_modules/<name>` to realpath to the task's own directory and names the borrowed path when it does not. `deps_are_fresh` includes it; `worktree-setup.sh` and `repo-gates.sh` run it after `npm ci` and before stamping, and the gate aborts with an incomplete attempt.
- Regression proof: with the three scripts reverted to `origin/main`, `infra-tests.sh` failed exactly the 9 new cases (463 passed) and `gate-parallel.test.mjs` failed the new abort case; the refreshed-link and gate-completion controls passed both ways.
- Remaining limit: a command run by hand before `worktree-setup.sh` (focused `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, a `--readonly-init` reviewer running code) still borrows the primary's tree; the kit cannot intercept it. Third-party packages absent from the branch's lockfile but present in the primary's `node_modules` would also still resolve upward.

- Goal: a design pass over Part 6 of `docs/features/mcp-api-reference/mcp-api-reference-spec.md` and over the two shipped MCP pages (Settings → MCP API, #284; Settings → MCP connection, #111–#114). The first real use of `xezar-ux-design`. Base `9977c9c`, Node 24 / npm 11, macOS. Documentation only; code findings are listed in the spec's 20.8 for assignment.
- How the role was reached: the workflow was `docs-maintenance`, whose step skill is `xezar-docs-maintenance`. `xezar-ux-design` was read only because the brief named it. This task therefore says nothing about the open item in `installation.md` – whether planning and implementation agents read the UX skill without being told.

"An unavailable browser is not a pass" made booting the cockpit non-optional.

It has no critique step (read the existing design as a reviewer, say what to keep, write the critique before editing), no "what gets cut" question – its seven questions only add, so a design written to them grows, which is the checklist failure this task was meant to correct – and no "worst case, measured" step. The three most useful findings (a 4,113 px open tool, a guard the page calls "not required" that the tool's own description requires, a coverage section missing without a word) came from measuring the running page. The MCP service does not start in a cockpit booted from a task worktree, so the boot used the built CLI against a `git init` repository under `/tmp` with a short `XEZ_HOME` (the #284 recipe). `agent-browser set media light` did not change the cockpit, which keeps its own theme setting; the cockpit's theme button did.

- Proposed skill change, not applied here (the file has its own owner): a short "reviewing, not authoring" paragraph, a "cut" question and a "worst case, measured" question. The wording is in the #296 pull request.
- Verdict: **not enough to call `xezar-ux-design` real-task verified.** One task, in a review mode the skill does not address, reached through the brief rather than a workflow, with its most useful method supplied by the brief and by measurement. Recorded as real-task observed. Next trigger: a `plan-and-spec` or `feature-implementation` task that touches a cockpit surface without the brief naming the skill; the first `design` run whose brief does not name the skill; the first `design-review` run on a PR.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #300, `plan-and-spec` step 1 (Design and specify), `xezar-planning-spec`, Claude Code — real-task observed

- Goal: a release-level Definition of Done for 0.14.0 beside the MCP feature's eight clauses, with the inventory built from the pull requests merged since `v0.13.1`. Base `1ab81ec`, macOS. Documentation only.

Candidate check: refuse a new line inside a dated section whose tag already exists.
- Update (2026-09-15): the tag-aware changelog proposal is recorded as deferred in `enhancement-ideas.md`; this sweep changes no check behavior.
- Observed: an open issue (#229) and a changelog entry claiming its fix coexist. Closing issues is not tied to the changelog, so the tracker and the changelog can disagree silently.
- Observed (confirms the #283 and #296 entries): the brief asks for a ready pull request, and this workflow's last step is "Draft PR into main". The brief was followed in step 1 and the conflict is passed to the handoff step in the resume notes.
- Remaining limit: no clause in the document has been assessed; it defines the assessment. The findings above are observed at `1ab81ec` only.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #302, `feature-implementation` step 1 (Implement, `xezar-implementation`), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: exclusive ownership over a live MCP session (A-17, A-18, the exclusivity half of A-23), which needed the `xez mcp` bridge to keep one connection per process instead of one per tool call. Base `1ab81ec`, branch `xez/cecc8f86`. Node 24 / npm 11, macOS.
- Observed: **the decision records already held every number and rule**, and one internal contradiction. D-02 § 4 says a stdio server closes its output after `-32081`; D-02 § 6 says the exit from `fenced` is an automatic reconnect by the bridge and that no state may exit only by a human action. On stdio the bridge is the server, so the two cannot both hold. The implementation followed § 6 and the pull request reports the choice instead of taking it silently. A brief that says "the decisions are made" is still worth reading for the two places a decision contradicts itself.
- Observed: the "what was it load-bearing for" question found five guarantees of one-connection-per-call (N-07 pick-up without a timer, restart transparency, per-call failure containment, disconnect-cancels-nothing, and the defect itself). Each got a named replacement in the bridge's module comment and the pull request; restart transparency is the one deliberately changed, because D-02 § 5 fences it.
- Observed: making a connection a session changed the on-disk footprint of every MCP call (a claim directory under `.local/xezar`), and four isolation and parity suites that compare project snapshots byte for byte went red for that alone. Removing the empty claim directory on release, and having the A/B fixture's raw-frame client resolve only after its connection closed, restored the "a call leaves no trace" property rather than filtering it out of the assertions.
- Observed (confirms the #285 entry): the red proof by copying the changed sources aside and checking out the base versions, never `git stash`, worked. It has to wait for any background suite or review agent that reads the same files.
- Real clients: the #118 harness (`test/integration/mcp-real-clients.test.ts`, built CLI) recorded A-17 PASSED for two bridge processes and as the second client for Claude Code 2.1.268, Codex 0.154.0 and OpenCode 1.18.30, and A-18 PASSED for idle owner, SIGKILL handover, stale-client fencing, task survival and a `xezar serve` restart. The acceptance record itself was left to the acceptance task (DoD clause 2).
- Remaining limit: one macOS host; the acceptance harness still prints its old "not wired" text as `missing` on every A-17/A-18 record; how each client presents `-32081` to a person in an interactive session was not observed.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #229, `bug-fix` step 1 (Reproduce and diagnose), `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: fix a cancel lost while an agent session spawns. Base `d29a99e`, macOS, Node 24.20. The user's cockpit ran the installed `xezar` 0.13.1.
- Observed: **the issue's code reading was right for the release the user runs and already fixed on `main`.** `publishSession` and its re-check arrived in #249 (`c889f03`), eight hours after the issue was filed; `git merge-base --is-ancestor c889f03 v0.13.1` says it is not in 0.13.1. The stuck tasks came from the installed release, not from `main`. Checking the running version and the fix's ancestry first turned a "write the fix" brief into a "prove the fix and cover its gaps" task, with no engine change.
- Observed: an existing regression test is not evidence until it is re-run red. Removing the one re-check line turned the #249 test red (`expected 'hung' to be 'drained'`), so it was a real proof. It covered only one of the two session sites, and only through `quiesce()`. Restoring the 0.13.1 wiring at ONE site at a time is what showed each new case guards its own site.
- Observed: a synchronous `store.on('event')` listener makes a cancel land at an exact point in the run body. Cancelling on `step-start` hits the spawn window every time; cancelling on the runner's `session.started` hits "one tick after open". Neither depends on machine load.
- Observed: **the brief's "stash the fix" red-proof step does not apply when the fix is already merged.** The honest equivalent is to revert the fix's lines in place, run, and `git checkout -- <file>`. A clean tree makes that safe, and it avoids the shared stash stack.
- Observed (sibling, not fixed): a cancel during a check step is honoured, but `close` waits for every process holding the shell's stdout. In a standalone probe, `sleep 4; echo after` closed at 4012 ms against 303 ms for a single command. The children run on after the cancel.
- Remaining limit: the red proofs are in-process with the bundled mock. No real Claude/Codex/OpenCode/pi session was cancelled mid-spawn; each runner's `interrupt()` was read, not exercised here.

### 2026-09-11 — issue #312, `bug-fix` step 1 (Reproduce and diagnose), `xezar-bug-investigation`, Claude Code — fixture-tested; the guard fired in real runs (update 2026-09-15; see #356/#402)

- Evidence: run b86c6066 (`feature-implementation`, #309). Its event log shows `readiness` PREFLIGHT OK, then `gate evidence sealed  a0cf85e…` – the manifest's own `baseSha`. Gate attempt `0001` ran on that head. The handoff step caught it by hand 8 minutes later and wrote a `BLOCKED` note; no check did.
- Observed: **"who marks this step done?" found the cause in one step.** A non-final agent step is not interactive, so a question in prose or `XEZ:ASK` ends it `done`. The shared contract told agents to use `XEZ:ASK` "in an interactive terminal agent step", but said nothing about the other steps.
- Observed: readiness had one scope check, "is there a `BLOCKED` file". An absent file read as "not blocked", with no populated-input guarantee.
- Change: readiness and both evidence modes refuse when HEAD is an ancestor of `refs/heads/<base>` or `refs/remotes/origin/<base>` (`branch.has-own-commits`). An ancestor test, not an equality test, so a base that moved on does not hide an empty branch. A check that cannot be evaluated refuses. The shared contract of all 18 skills gains one additive sentence: write `BLOCKED` before stopping for a decision.
- Scope check: 9 workflows run these modes. All end in a draft PR or a release, and neither exists without a commit. `business-analysis` and `research` run plain preflight only; a fixture case pins that.
- Regression/control: on the unfixed check the 4 new empty-branch refusals were red and the other 476 cases passed (the plain-preflight, read-only-role and real-commit controls among them – they pass both ways). With the fix, 58 older cases went red: 16 fixtures sealed or verified evidence on a fresh, empty branch. They now start from one real commit (`add_worktree_with_work`); fixtures for plain preflight stay empty. Final run: 480 passed, 0 failed.
- Observed: on this task's own worktree, local `main` was behind `origin/main`, and the refusal came from `refs/remotes/origin/main`. Checking only the local base ref would have let this empty branch through.
- Remaining limit: a branch with commits whose net diff is empty (commit, then revert) still passes. The engine still marks a question-only step `done`; the refusal only moves the stop to readiness.

The `gates` step ran the whole canonical list on the dirty tree and only the seal refused it; run-finalize autosave then committed the files. Fourth occurrence that day.
