### 2026-09-17 — issue #453 batch B6, author step (`feature-implementation` kit workflow), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: design-debt batch B6 (Git tabs and the shared diff renderer): `RunDiff` as a facade over `Diff`, Git pages on the #424 rhythm, 44 px phone targets, with a unit and a browser test.
- Observed: **the new browser spec found three defects the class contracts could not** – diff line numbers at 2.7:1 (light) and 3.1:1 (dark), keyboard focus dropped on `<body>` when the state-opened Commit dialog closed, and two light syntax colours below 4.5:1 on diff tints (tokens outside the batch, recorded as G-38 and reported by the spec, not passed). A dialog opened by JavaScript `.click()` without focus hides the focus-return bug in both directions; focus the opener first.
- Observed: a live dry run parked at review plus git commands in its worktree gives every patch shape the renderer needs (copy, rename, binary, 420 lines). Plain `git diff` reports a copy only when `diff.renames=copies` is set AND the source file changed in the same diff.
- Observed: the full `npm run test:e2e` failed one file (design-debt-b2: the browser provider's `open` timed out, then every hook timed out) while 45 files passed; the same file passed 14/14 alone straight after. Rerun a whole-file timeout cascade alone before treating it as a regression.
- Observed (tooling): the Monitor tool and `bash -c` wrappers were refused; `timeout` does not exist on macOS. A foreground `for … sleep 5` loop capped under five minutes polled the background suite without ending the turn.
- Regression/control: eight named breaks each turned the B6 unit test red and back green (evidence `red-proofs/`, break logs `01`–`06` and `08`–`09`; `00`, `07` and `10` are the green restores). One defensive fix (re-measuring a virtualized diff's start margin when the page above resizes) has only a guard that passes both ways; the drift was not reproduced.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine, headless Chrome; the >1500-row review diff was checked only with the `?diff=virtual` override on a short thread.

### 2026-09-17 — PR #574 response round (`address-review-findings` kit workflow), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: one response round folding three verdicts on the same head (code review REQUEST CHANGES, design review PASS WITH FOLLOW-UPS, QA PASS), with the leader's dispositions fixed in advance: one code fix, one ownership reconciliation, the rest recorded as documentation.
- Observed: **a fixed offset that "only fails at some densities" fails at all of them.** The design review measured 45 / 37 / 19 / 3 px of cover at Roomy / Comfortable / Compact / Compact for real and called the last one fine. Measuring the header in a real browser put its bottom at 253 / 217 / 200 / 184 px against a 160 px offset, so the constant was short at every density — the review's margin at the tightest density came from its own fixture, not from the layout. Measure before deciding a constant "fits one case".
- Observed: the red proof is worth the rebuild. Restoring the two `10rem` literals, rebuilding the cockpit and rerunning only the new cases turned all four red with the exact pixel gap in the message, then green again after restoring — about four minutes, and it is the only thing that separates a new browser assertion from one that passes either way.
- Observed: a fix aimed at one file reached a second owner. Measuring the run header means putting a ref on `routes/task-thread/run-header.tsx`, a batch B5 file, while the finding under repair was itself an ownership breach. Recording both files in the PR body under the same reconciliation line kept one answer rather than two.
- Observed (tooling): `bash -c '…'` wrappers were refused by the permission layer again, and `timeout` does not exist on macOS; a foreground `for … sleep 5` loop under five minutes polled the detached test-env boot. The browser environment is torn down with `scripts/test-env-down.sh`, which stops the recorded PID — no pattern kill.
- Regression/control: one named break (both pages back to `[--diff-sticky-top:10rem]`) turned the four new browser cases red and back green (evidence `red-proofs/nb1-fixed-10rem-offset.red.log`, `nb1-restored-green.log`, hashed). The helper's ten jsdom cases are contracts, not a red proof — jsdom lays nothing out.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine, headless Chrome. The measured offset was verified on the task Changes tab only; the Commits and Files tabs read the same variable but were checked by class contract, not in layout. Two design-review findings are recorded from the reviewer's screenshots rather than reproduced here: the 1024 px run-header overflow, and the review gate's 1.34:1 manual-merge URL, whose source site is not identified in `review-panel.tsx`.

### 2026-09-17 — PR #575 / issue #306 part 3, review response round 1 (`address-review-findings` kit workflow) — real-task verified

- Goal: apply the leader's dispositions to a REQUEST CHANGES verdict on someone else's PR (`xez/38e042c4`, head `350e932`): one Major lock-integrity fix, one proof-strength fix, one docs wording fix, two record-only items. Reclaimed-owner shape — the PR head is merged into this task's own branch and the result pushed back to the PR's branch.
- Observed: `git checkout -- <file>` is the natural way to restore a file after applying a deliberate break, and it silently DISCARDS uncommitted work in that same file. A break re-run loop that touches a file you are also editing must commit first; three of nine break placements were re-run against a source that had quietly lost its new test hook, and the results looked fine.
- Observed: a barrier placed AFTER the critical step proves less than one placed at the DECISION. The rotation race barrier paused a writer after its renames, which left the peer free to decide on a live file that had already moved; moving the pause to the moment the size check decides, and making BOTH contenders hold there, turned all three of the reviewer's break placements red in three runs each, from 2 of 7 before.
- Observed: a stale-lock takeover that reads and then removes is two separate decisions. Two waiters that read the same dead lock each removed what they found, so both ended up holding a lock — 27 times in 300 four-way trials. A unique token in the lock file plus one `wx` guard file under which both the takeover AND the release re-read that token took it to 0; the looped contention test is the only kind that catches it, because a single trial passes.
- Observed: a merge conflict in `BACKWARD_COMPATIBILITY.md` between a PR branch and a main that had moved is the normal case when two parts of one issue land days apart. Resolving it means keeping BOTH sides' paragraphs, not choosing; a word diff of the two conflict halves is what makes that decidable.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine. The lock fix is proven by looped in-process contention and by the rotation suite's real child processes; a lock shared across two genuinely separate xezar installs, and a laptop that actually sleeps mid-write, are unobserved.

### 2026-09-17 — issue #453 batch B8, author step (`feature-implementation` kit workflow), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: the last design-debt batch — the keyboard-accessible image preview (A06), the final `known-gaps.md` reconciliation for 0.16.0, the four gaps B5–B7 deferred to B8, the screenshot-manifest revision, and #584's flaky browser wait.
- Observed: **"still open" notes in a backlog file go stale faster than the code.** Re-reading each row against the source before writing its disposition found five already fixed by earlier batches that no batch had crossed off — G-05, G-06's two close buttons, G-07's `PopoverTitle`, all of G-08, and half of G-14. A reconciliation that trusted the notes would have filed five issues for work already done.
- Observed: **the browser-test provider's `wait --fn` predicate can READ page state but its writes do not survive between polls.** A wait that stamped `window.__hittable` to time a 250 ms hold timed out every run; read back after the 25 s timeout the global was still `null`, while a `window.__samples` array written by `eval` was visible to the same predicate. Keep predicates pure; do anything that compares two moments in time from Node, where two reads are two real round trips apart.
- Observed: **#584's stated cause was half of it, and instrumenting beat theorising three times over.** The issue describes a `scrollIntoView` → `click` race. Fixing that left the spec red; every boolean condition then passed and the box was stable across 40 animation frames, which pointed at the stability clause rather than the geometry. The remaining cause is that the thread REPLAYS its transcript after mount and the scroller follows each chunk with `behavior: 'smooth'`, so the run header keeps being pushed around — which is why it failed one run and passed the next on one quiet machine. Waiting for the scroller to stop growing AND stop moving, then clearing the covering top bar the way a finger would, gave three consecutive green runs.
- Observed: **a new browser spec's first run is worth more than its diff review.** Three defects in my own spec, none visible on the page: a hand-written run record is silently dropped by the store's per-entry schema so the run 404s (seed from the shared record fixture instead); Tailwind v4 serialises a scrim as `oklab(0 0 0 / 0.5)`, so an `rgba()`-only parse threw inside the page; and I had invented a `browser.emulateReducedMotion()` that does not exist — the real `setMedia` carries its own warning that a silent no-op and a correctly-still cockpit look identical, so the `matchMedia` assertion is mandatory.
- Observed: **two readers of one manifest wanted opposite answers.** `e2e/capture/manifest.ts` is both the on-disk screenshot contract (a unit test proves every listed PNG exists) and the capture harness's shot list. Adding a state planned for the next release turned the fast gate red for a picture nobody had taken. A `plannedFor` marker splitting `allShotFiles()` from `allPlannedShotFiles()` let the debt be written down without a red gate — and the planned row also needed a real prepare step, or it would have been a note that throws when someone finally re-captures.
- Observed: **the brief and the accepted plan disagreed, and the plan was right.** The brief asked to take the guardian's spacing ceiling from 2 to 0 "unless #453 says otherwise"; #453 says otherwise in three places, and both remaining rows are absolute 24 px chip floors the density lever must not be allowed to shrink. Going to look for the rows to delete, rather than deleting them, is what surfaced it.
- Observed (tooling): `zsh` does not word-split an unquoted `$VAR`, so `npm test -- $SUITE` sent both paths as ONE filter and vitest reported "No test files found, exiting with code 0" — a green exit code for a run that tested nothing. Two red proofs were silently empty before this was noticed. List filters literally, and never read a vitest exit code without its file count.
- Observed (tooling): the full browser suite takes ~11 minutes, past this session's 600 s per-call cap. Running it in the foreground and polling a `for … sleep 5` loop under five minutes keeps it foreground-owned without ending a turn on it.
- Regression/control: five named T-8 breaks each turned the suite red and back green — the pre-fix clickable-img component (13 failed / 14 passed), the thumbnail no longer the `DialogTrigger` (6/21), an unlisted `gap-[7px]` (2 of 48), a stale allowlist count (2 of 48), a ledger row with no disposition and a reused retired id (2 of 19 each) — plus two browser breaks, the pre-fix component (whole file red) and a targeted `onCloseAutoFocus` preventDefault, which failed exactly the two focus-return assertions. Restores green at 61/61 and 18/18. Logs hashed in `red-proofs/SHA256SUMS`.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine, one headless Chrome. The full suite's one red file, `plan-mode.e2e.ts`, passes 5/5 alone and 23/23 paired with this batch's new spec, so it is not caused here; a second full run is the evidence for whether it is a whole-suite flake. The design review and the independent QA that AC-8 names are later stages and are not claimed by this step.

### 2026-09-18 — browser-test pull request 3 of 4 (issue #549), author step (`testing-and-verification` kit workflow), Claude Code — real-task verified

- Goal: guide-flow browser tests for guides 01, 03, 05–08 (batch 6/7 contracts), following the accepted `browser-test-spec.md` business-analysis deliverable — six new `guide-*.e2e.ts` files plus the shared `guide-browser.ts` semantic-locator helper, role/label/text locators only.
- Observed: **a sibling PR of the same campaign (#579, guides 04/10–16) merged into `main` mid-task**, adding its own `packages/web/e2e/guide-browser.ts`. GitHub's `mergeable` field read `CONFLICTING` immediately after PR creation while `git merge-tree` locally reported a clean auto-merge — the two disagreed because `main` kept moving (two more commits landed within the next few minutes) between the PR-create call and the mergeability check. A real `git merge origin/main` (not a `merge-tree` simulation) was the only way to get a trustworthy answer; it surfaced one genuine `add/add` conflict on `guide-browser.ts` (my copy was a strict superset — two extra methods for reading a checkbox's `checked` and a control's `disabled` state off the accessibility snapshot — resolved by keeping mine) and one content conflict in `docs/testing/coverage-gaps.md` (both PRs added a differently-named row to the same table — resolved by keeping both rows rather than picking one). Re-running the full 6-file suite plus two of the newly-merged sibling files after the merge is what actually proved nothing broke; the local mergeability check alone would not have.
- Observed: **an accessibility-tree snapshot reflects CSS `text-transform`, not the DOM's literal casing.** Several section labels in this cockpit (`Branches`, `Agents' base branch`) are styled `uppercase`; the live `agent-browser snapshot` command reported them as `BRANCHES` / `AGENTS' BASE BRANCH`, and a role/name lookup against the DOM-literal mixed-case string silently found nothing. Verified against a real dry-run server before writing the assertion, not guessed from source — the discipline this whole package leaned on throughout (see the next two entries).
- Observed: **a "follow-up" inbox item is NOT runnable unless it carries `runnable: true`, `suggestedSkill` or `suggestedPrompt`** (`isTodoRunnable`, `routes/inbox.tsx`) — a todo entry seeded with only `{id, summary, action: 'follow-up'}` renders as an Acknowledge-only note, not a Run/Dismiss card, which is not obvious from the `action` field's name alone and cost one full red assertion before being caught by live verification.
- Observed: `git commit -q -m seed` in a from-scratch fixture repo fails outright ("nothing to commit") when the fixture's conditional branch writes no file that path — always commit `--allow-empty` for a fixture-repo seed commit whose content is itself conditional.
- Observed: a scratch-worktree red-proof's own `git add -A` picked up the `node_modules` SYMLINKS created to avoid a fresh `npm ci` — `.gitignore`'s `node_modules/` pattern (trailing slash) does not match a symlink named `node_modules`, only a real directory. `git commit` then included and later `git revert` deleted the symlinks. Fixed by re-creating them and, from then on, staging the ONE changed source file by path rather than `-A`. (Do not add `node_modules` to the shared `.git/info/exclude` to work around this — that file is common across every worktree on the machine, including peers' active tasks; it was tried, immediately recognized as touching shared state, and reverted within the same turn.)
- Observed: a `git worktree add --detach` scratch worktree, cut once and reused for all six red-proofs (`node_modules` and `packages/*/node_modules` symlinked from the authored worktree, never reinstalled), made each break→build→run→revert→build→run cycle cost one `vite` rebuild (~0.2–0.3s) plus one spec-owned server boot (2–17s) rather than a fresh `npm ci`. `git worktree remove --force` afterwards left no trace in the primary checkout or the authored worktree.
- Observed: three green stability runs found one real pass-on-retry rather than zero — a `Reclaim now` `AlertDialog`'s own entrance animation still covered its `Keep it` button for a beat after the confirm text landed in the DOM (`agent-browser`: "Element … is covered by <div.fixed.inset-0>"). Filed as a fix (a settle pause, mirroring `design-debt-b*.e2e.ts`'s `settle()` pattern), not hidden, and all three required runs were then repeated from zero rather than counting the failing one.
- Lesson (recommended): for a browser-test package built from a business-analysis spec (as opposed to reading working UI code directly), spend the first pass calling the exact CLI commands (`agent-browser --session … open/snapshot/find`) against a throwaway dry-run server for every planned assertion BEFORE writing it into a spec file. Every wrong guess this task made (uppercase headings, checked/disabled snapshot syntax, runnability, async-render races) was caught this way, cheaply, rather than after a red CI run.
- Regression/control: each of the six new files was shown red against one named, representative product break (a renamed accessible name / heading / aria-label per file) on the scratch worktree above, then green again after `git revert` — never an in-place edit during a run. Full evidence (commit SHAs, failing assertion, duration, exit status) in this run's own evidence directory, `red-proofs.md`.
- Remaining limit: one macOS machine, headless Chrome via the `agent-browser` provider. Guide 09 got no new file — `project-switching.e2e.ts` (#589, already merged) was judged to already cover the one genuinely browser-only journey, with the remaining flows (tags, Max parallel, Add-project dialog) left to their existing jsdom coverage; this is a scope judgment call recorded in the PR body, not independently re-verified by a second reviewer at authoring time.
- Observed (review response, round 1): the settle-pause fix above did not hold — three green stability runs are not the same guarantee as "always". A real code review on this same PR found the click could still land during the entrance animation despite the pause and, separately, that a naive retry-the-click loop can itself throw: `hasRole` after a click can still read the AlertDialog as present for one more round trip after the click actually dismissed it (React's re-render and the CLI's own snapshot round trip lag the click), so an unconditional retry attempts a click on a button already gone. The fix that held across 8 consecutive standalone runs plus one 6-file combined run re-checks `hasRole` immediately before every click attempt (not just after) and treats "already gone" as success rather than clicking blind.
